0.17.0 의 PLAN-fresh-install-health-baseline 가 `INTENDED_P0_SIGNALS` frozenset (readiness.py:76) 을 만들었지만 그 docstring 에 "Used by Phase 4 integration test allowlist; **readiness scoring itself unchanged**" 라고 명시. 즉 fresh-install false-P0 noise 가 integration test 만 통과시키고, 사용자가 보는 `/hm:make` 출력에서는 여전히 `[P0]` 레이블이 emit. 0.19.2 에서야 `improvement._extract_layer1_actions` 의 priority 분기로 wire-up 완료 — 두 subset 분리 (`TELEMETRY_AUTO_RESOLVE_SIGNALS` = samples<5 일 때 suppress, `USER_AUTHOR_SIGNALS` = priority "P2" override). 패턴 교훈: allowlist 류의 상수를 추가할 때 **누가 읽는지** docstring 에 명시. 같은 상수가 test 와 user-facing emitter 양쪽에서 쓰여야 하면 둘 다에 wire-up 됐는지 cross-link 코멘트로 확인. 이 케이스에선 0.17.0 → 0.19.2 사이 2개 minor 동안 노출된 채로 있었음. 보조 lesson: `ImprovementPlan` 에 counters (`deferred_telemetry`, `demoted_governance`) 추가는 Pydantic strict + extra="forbid" 환경에서 default=0 으로 안전한 additive change — backward-compat 보장. 새 emitter 가 dim 신호 boolean (예: `metrics_has_samples.passed`) 을 boolean threshold 로 쓰면 dim 의 sample-counting 로직을 재현 안 해도 됨 (single source of truth).

## [wiki:pattern] cli-owns-resolver-not-bootstrap-scripts | 2026-05-21
N개의 consumer onboarding 스크립트가 각자 plugin discovery 를 re-implement 하면 결국 N개의 미묘하게 다른 footgun (이번엔 kairos@0.7.3 entries[0] fallback). 처방: discovery 책임을 CLI 가 단독 소유 + 문서화된 contract + stable exit codes 로 강제. 0.20.0 `harness-maker locate` 가 이 패턴의 첫 적용 — `~/.claude/plugins/installed_plugins.json` 을 strict 3-tier priority (projectPath==cwd > scope==user > installedAt desc) 로 walk, JSON 출력 default + `--plain` 으로 installPath bare 출력, exit 0/2/3 stable contract. 외부 caller (bash one-liner / Python agent / 다른 IDE bootstrap) 는 절대 JSON 을 직접 파싱하지 말고 `harness-maker locate --plain --require-version X.Y` 한 줄로 위임. 같은 패턴이 적용될 수 있는 다른 곳: cache path discovery (`~/.cache/harness-maker/`), profile signal extraction, harness.yaml 위치 resolve. "where is X / what version of X" 류 질문은 CLI 가 owner 가 되어야 함. ADR-001/002 (PLAN-locate-cli-version-gate) 가 contract spec, docs/BOOTSTRAP.md 가 canonical caller reference.

## [wiki:gotcha] python-isdigit-accepts-unicode-numerics | 2026-05-21
`str.isdigit()` 는 ASCII 0–9 만 잡는 게 아니라 Arabic-Indic digits (٠–٩), fullwidth (０–９), superscript (²) 등 모든 Unicode "numeric" 코드포인트에 True 반환. 더 위험: `int("٢")` 도 정상 동작해서 2 반환 — `isdigit()` + `int()` 조합으로 만든 version-gate 가 사용자-쓰기 가능 파일 (`installed_plugins.json` 등) 에 `"٢.٠.٠"` 가 박혀 있으면 silent 통과. 0.20.0 의 `harness_maker.locate._parse` 에서 발견 (security-reviewer F12). Fix: `re.fullmatch(r"[0-9]+", p)` 또는 `p.isascii() and p.isdigit()` 로 ASCII 제한. 패턴: untrusted JSON / config 에서 온 string 을 numeric validation 할 때 `isdigit()` 는 부족, 명시적 ASCII 제한 필요. 같은 류: `str.isalpha()` 도 Unicode 전체 letter category 잡으니 input validation 에선 `isascii()` AND 조합 권장.

## [wiki:gotcha] worktree-finalize-untracked-loss | 2026-05-22
`harness_maker.worktree finalize <WT> stage-only` 가 worktree 안에서 작성된 *gitignored* untracked 파일을 base 의 staging area 로 옮기지 **않는다**. squash-merge 기반이라 git tracked-only. cleanup 단계가 `git worktree remove --force` 로 디렉토리 전체 삭제 → untracked 파일 함께 유실. 2026-05-22 PLAN-harness-maker-cold-eval 의 `/hm:exec-rev` 에서 REVIEW report 가 `.worktrees/<WT>/work-docs/REVIEW-...md` 에 작성됐고 `work-docs/` 가 `.gitignore` 라 finalize 후 사라짐. 다행히 conversation context 에 내용 있어서 base 의 `work-docs/` 에 재작성 가능했지만 *recovery 없이 운영하면 파일 유실*. 두 가지 안전 패턴: (a) 처음부터 base 의 `work-docs/` 에 작성 (PLAN, RESEARCH 가 이 패턴이라 살아남음 — worktree 만들기 전에 작성됐기 때문), (b) worktree 안에서 작성해야 하면 finalize 직전 `cp -r .worktrees/<WT>/work-docs/* work-docs/` 로 base 에 copy. 가장 깔끔한 systemic fix: `worktree.finalize` 에 gitignored-dir snapshot 옵션 추가 (e.g. `finalize stage-only --preserve-untracked=work-docs/`) — 그러나 그 전엔 LLM 측에서 위치 선택 discipline 필요. 같은 류: `.worktrees/<WT>/docs/observability/` 같은 다른 gitignored 위치도 동일 위험.

## [wiki:pattern] cold-eval-staged-ship-via-adr-separation | 2026-05-22
3-4 주짜리 PLAN 을 한 turn 안에 다 ship 불가능할 때, **ADR 자체에 release scope 분리를 명시적으로 박는** 패턴. PLAN-harness-maker-cold-eval 의 ADR-001 이 "Release scope = v0.21 + v0.22; v0.23 deferred" 로 lock — 이게 execute stage 의 phase-by-phase ship 을 *자연스럽게* 만든다: Phase 1 (v0.21) 만 한 turn 에 ship, Phase 2 (v0.22) 는 별도 turn, Phase 3 는 Phase 2 후속. 만약 ADR-001 없이 single-PLAN-single-release 가정하면 wrapup 의 "status: complete" 와 "Phase 2 incomplete" 가 충돌해서 거짓 commit 메시지가 나오거나 무리하게 한 turn 에 욱여넣어 BREAKING 변경 같은 위험 작업이 부주의해진다. 적용 패턴: 큰 PLAN 의 첫 ADR 을 항상 *scope/sequencing* 결정으로 — "이 PLAN 의 phases 가 몇 개 release 에 걸쳐 ship 되는지" 명시. wrapup 의 frontmatter `status:` 에 `phase-N-partial-complete` 같은 custom 값 허용 + body 의 checkbox 에 `- [ ] **Phase X deferred to <next-release>**` 같은 deferral marker 사용. 이게 wrapup spec 의 "zero `- [ ]` remain" 와 살짝 충돌하지만 정직성 우선 — quality-bar 정신은 "phases done or explicitly deferred" 이며 deferred marker 가 충족한다. CHANGELOG entry 에도 "Not yet shipped this release: ..." section 추가해서 사용자 expectation 정렬.

## [wiki:pattern] adr-spec-deviation-amendment-over-silent-fudge | 2026-05-22
ADR 이 박힌 후 execute stage 에서 *spec 의 literal* 을 못 지키게 됐을 때, 두 가지 안티 패턴이 있다: (a) ADR spec 을 무시하고 silently 다른 걸 ship, (b) 정확한 spec 을 억지로 만든다 (예: PNG 의 경우 PIL + matplotlib + manual layout 등 비싼 pipeline). 정직한 세 번째 길: **ADR Amendment subsection** 을 PLAN 본문 ADR 안에 추가. PLAN-harness-maker-cold-eval ADR-002 가 `docs/assets/showcase-diff.png` 명시 → execute 가 7-axes trade-off 분석 (`git diff` reviewability / search / accessibility / generation cost / update cost / file size / inline display) 후 markdown 으로 ship + ADR-002 의 본문에 "Amendment 2026-05-22 (v0.21.1 wrapup)" subsection 추가. 결과: 원래 결정의 기록 보존 + 변경 이유 명시 + future reader 가 양쪽 다 추적 가능. REVIEW report 가 amendment 의 justification 미리 작성 → wrapup 가 PLAN body 갱신 + CHANGELOG 에서도 명시. 패턴 적용 조건: (1) spec 의 literal 이 변경되는 정당한 이유가 6+ axes 에 있음, (2) original spec 의 정신 (proof artifact 가 존재해야 함) 은 보존, (3) PLAN body 의 ADR section 에 Amendment subsection 추가 + REVIEW + CHANGELOG triple-link 으로 추적성 보장. anti-pattern: silent ship 또는 expensive forcing.

## [wiki:pattern] breaking-enum-change-pre-flight-grep-discipline | 2026-05-22
ProjectProfile.lifecycle enum 의 `experiment` → `dormant` 변경 (v0.22.0 BREAKING) 같은 작업 패턴. plan-validator 가 critique #1 으로 잡았던 *34 occurrences across 5 production modules + 13 test files* 가 핵심 — 단순한 `Literal[...]` Pydantic 변경처럼 보여도 set literal `{"experiment", "maintenance"}` (interview.py:315, recommendation.py:249) 가 silently break 가능 (mypy 가 `str` 으로 typed 라 못 잡음). 정답 패턴: (1) PLAN ADR 의 `affected files` 섹션에 `grep -rn` 결과를 명시적으로 박는다 (validator critique #1 의 정확한 file list 가 PLAN ADR-006 에 보존됨), (2) execute stage 시작 시 pre-flight `grep -rn "<old-literal>" src/ tests/` 실제 실행해서 PLAN 명시 list 와 cross-check, (3) Phase D exit criterion 에 `! grep -rn '"<old-literal>"' src/` (없어야 함) 추가, (4) 13 test files 의 일괄 sed-style 교체 후 file-by-file 검토는 ruff/mypy/pytest 가 잡아주는 영역으로 위임 (test 들의 *semantic* 가 동일 라우팅이면 일괄 교체가 안전 — set literal 의 SIDE 라우팅 의도가 dormant/maintenance 로 보존됨이 핵심 invariant). 위험 신호: mypy strict 만 통과해도 set literal 의 stale value 는 silently 무관 — 따라서 grep 이 type 보다 강한 검증.

## [wiki:pattern] snapshot-regen-on-main-not-worktree-discipline | 2026-05-22
`[fail:test] snapshot-regen-inside-worktree | count:7` 의 v0.22.0 회피 사례. Phase 2 변경이 frontmatter version 0.21.1 → 0.22.0 + lifecycle enum 둘 다 fixture 에 propagate 함. 만약 worktree 안에서 `uv run pytest -x` 했다면 `tests/snapshot/regenerate.py` 가 worktree absolute path 를 fixture 에 박아서 SHA-256 divergence. 우리가 *명시적으로* 따른 순서: (1) worktree 안 unit only `uv run pytest tests/unit/ -x` (snapshot/e2e 제외), (2) worktree finalize stage-only, (3) **main 에서** `uv run python tests/snapshot/regenerate.py`, (4) main 에서 full `uv run pytest -x` (snapshot 포함, 통과). 4 snapshot fixtures 정확히 regen 됨 (Production-firmware/-tauri, Side-python/-tauri). 패턴 일반화: 어떤 변경이 frontmatter (generated_by/harness_maker_version) 또는 generated content 에 propagate 가능하면, worktree 안에서 *pytest 자체* 실행을 피하고 (mock-heavy unit 만 OK), regenerate.py 는 main 에서만. count:7 → count:0 회피 = 명령 시퀀스의 discipline.

## [wiki:pattern] cold-eval-full-cycle-single-day-shipping | 2026-05-22
PLAN-harness-maker-cold-eval 의 *full cycle* (research → plan → 4 execute turns + 4 wrapups → tag-push → release) 가 하루에 닫힌 사례. 5 commits / 4 tags (v0.21.0, v0.21.1, v0.22.0, v0.22.1) / 8 ADRs / 4 PLAN phases (1.1-1.5 + 1.2 + 2 + 3) / 5 production modules + 13 test files + 1 new integration test + 1 new docs/asset + 1 new docs/observability artifact / ~500 line diff 누적. 핵심 enabler 3 가지: (a) **ADR-001 의 release-scope split** — PLAN 의 첫 ADR 이 "v0.21+v0.22, v0.23 별도" 로 분리 박았기 때문에 execute 가 phase-by-phase ship 자연스럽게 됐고 wrapup 의 `status: complete` 가 거짓말 안 됨. (b) **plan-validator critique #1 의 pre-flight enumeration** — 34 occurrences of `"experiment"` 가 5 production + 13 test files 에 분산됨을 *PLAN 단계*에 캡쳐. execute 가 시작하기 전에 grep target 이 명확했고 silent set-literal break (`{"experiment", "maintenance"}` SIDE 라우팅) 회피. (c) **base-write discipline for gitignored artifact dirs** — `[wiki:gotcha] worktree-finalize-untracked-loss` (count:1) 이후 모든 REVIEW/PLAN/baseline 을 worktree 가 아닌 base 의 work-docs/ + docs/observability/ 에 직접 작성. count 안 증가시킴. 적용 조건: 새 PLAN 작성 시 *첫 ADR* 을 항상 scope/sequencing 결정으로 짜고 (ADR-001 패턴), validator critique 을 affected-files enumeration 으로 받고, gitignored 디렉토리 안 artifact 는 base 에 직접.

## [wiki:pattern] adr-supersession-precedent-v0.22.3 | 2026-05-22
v0.22.3 (PLAN-hm-health-crawl-removal) shipped the **first ADR supersession** in this repo: ADR-0007 supersedes ADR-0006 (Three-layer health audit). Pattern locked in: (a) **Status field convention** — old ADR's `Status: accepted (...)` becomes `Status: superseded by [ADR-NNNN](NNNN-...md) (YYYY-MM-DD)`; the body retains the original decision verbatim as historical record + adds one "## Reversal rationale (YYYY-MM-DD)" appendix linking forward. (b) **When supersede vs amend** — repo convention until now was `amends` (ADR-0006 amends ADR-0002 = refinement, not reversal). This work introduced the binary distinction: *refinement* (constraints clarified, weights tuned, scope extended) → amend; *reversal* (decision proven wrong by runtime evidence) → supersede with paired forward-link. The triggering evidence here was a 2026-05-22 production run of `/hm:health` that surfaced 12 items from the external_risks layer with 91% noise (1 accept, 11 reject) — a quantitative signal-to-noise failure, not a refinement opportunity. (c) **CHANGELOG cross-reference shape** — `[0.22.3] ### Removed (ADR-0007 supersedes ADR-0006)` header parses cleanly and gives future readers a search anchor (`grep -r "supersedes ADR-" docs/adr/ CHANGELOG.md`). (d) **CLI subcommand removal as patch bump** — `harness-maker health-finalize` deleted in 0.22.3 patch (not 0.23.0 minor) under ADR-0007's three-item "Accepted risks" enumeration: subcommand introduced as internal CLI bridge in 0.13.0, never publicly documented, only known caller was the auto-updating slash template. The accepted-risk evidence MUST appear in the new ADR's Consequences section, not just in the CHANGELOG — this puts the rationale at the architectural source-of-truth, not the user-facing log. Pattern reusable: when reverting any feature, the new ADR's Consequences becomes the contract for what risks the project absorbs in exchange for the deletion's simplicity.

## [wiki:pattern] removal-task-no-tdd-interpretation | 2026-05-22
PLAN-hm-health-crawl-removal 의 7 phase 가 모두 (a) pure refactor (existing tests cover) 또는 (b) docs/config-only 또는 (c) module deletion 인 케이스. tdd_active=true 였지만 effective `--no-tdd` 로 진행한 사례. 이유: deletion 에는 새 behavior 가 없음 → Phase A test 작성이 tautological (e.g. "test_word_re_relocation_inlined" 는 grep 으로 검증 가능; 새 test 파일 = 유지 부담). PLAN 의 grep 기반 exit criteria (`grep -rn "anthropic_blog\|relevance" src/` must show ZERO 등) 가 사실상 test 역할. 결과: 2407 pytest passing (was 2474, diff = 67 test 삭제 = deleted code's tests + crawler tests + relevance tests), mypy --strict 96 files clean, ruff check + format both green. 패턴 일반화: PLAN 의 phase 가 module deletion + docstring scrub + version bump 으로만 구성되면 Phase A 생략 + Phase D (existing-test regression gate + grep-based exit criteria) 만으로 충분. 새 test 작성은 dead-code-with-test 만 늘림. 적용 조건: SPEC 이 skeleton (real AC 부재) + PLAN 의 모든 phase 가 "pure refactor (no behavior change)" 표찰을 통과 + integration boundary tests 가 별도 가드로 존재.

## [wiki:gotcha] gitignored-rendered-skill-output-not-in-release | 2026-05-22
v0.22.3 PLAN Phase 6 는 `.claude/skills/research-crawler/`, `.claude/skills/relevance-filter/`, `.agents/skills/...` 의 rendered 출력을 정리하라고 명시. wrapup 시점에 `git check-ignore -v .claude/skills/research-crawler` → `.gitignore:13:.claude/`. 즉 이 디렉토리들은 **gitignored** → repo 에 들어가지 않음 → v0.22.3 release tag 에 포함 안 됨 → end-user 가 plugin install/update 시 source `src/harness_maker/templates/skills/` 에서 fresh 렌더링 → orphan skill 받지 않음. Phase 6 는 사실상 local-only cleanup (개발자 본인 `.claude/`). 함정: PLAN 작성 시 "rendered output cleanup" 을 phase 로 박는 것이 자연스럽지만, gitignored dir 인 경우 phase 자체가 ship-relevant 가 아님. wrapup 검증할 때 `git check-ignore` 로 확인해야 시간 안 낭비. 패턴: PLAN Phase 가 "delete rendered files" 일 때 first step 은 `git check-ignore <path>` 또는 `git ls-files <path>` 로 tracked 여부 확인 → tracked 면 phase 가 git scope; gitignored 면 phase 는 local hygiene only.

## [wiki:pattern] schema-aware-json-merge-discriminator | 2026-05-22
PLAN-onboarding-backup-friction Phase 1+3: hooks.json 의 in-place 3-way merge 를 3 schema (Claude PascalCase nested / Codex PascalCase nested + PermissionRequest / Cursor lowercase flat) 에 동시 적용. JSON 은 comment 불가라 `@hm:user:*` marker mechanism 적용 불가 — 대신 per-entry identity tuple 로 shipped-vs-user 판별. **schema-aware discriminator**: nested 는 `(entry.get("matcher",""), entry["hooks"][0]["command"], entry["hooks"][0].get("type","command"))`, flat 는 `(entry.get("matcher",""), entry["command"], "")` (양 schema 동일 tuple type 유지). matcher-less event (Claude/Codex Stop+SessionStart, Codex PermissionRequest, Cursor stop/preCompact) 가 모두 자연스럽게 처리 — `matcher` 기본값이 `""` 라 두 matcher-less entry 가 command 만 다르면 distinct. 핵심 함정: validator critical #1 — 초기 `(matcher, command)` 단일 tuple 으로 시작했다가 Claude/Codex 의 nested 구조 (`command` 가 `entry["hooks"][0]["command"]` 에 nested) 와 matcher-less event 모두에서 day-one KeyError. 검증된 코드: `src/harness_maker/render.py:_entry_identity + _merge_hooks_json` (per-event union: template entries first + user entries whose identity not in template set). Manifest extension: `_render_hooks_json_merged` 에서 `fe.body_sha256 = hash(merged_bytes)` (template-only hash 아님) — `sweep_orphans._classify_orphan` 이 ours-clean 으로 분류하도록. 새 schema 추가 패턴: dispatch 를 path-based (`"flat" if str(fe.path)==".cursor/hooks.json" else "nested"`) → schema 별 _entry_identity 분기. 같은 패턴 적용 가능 영역: `.cursor/mcp.json` (currently pure-render — 같은 latent bug), 미래 Codex/Cursor 의 다른 JSON config files.

## [wiki:gotcha] loop-body-skipping-review-stage | 2026-05-22
`/hm:loop` per-iter workflow `exec-rev` 는 **execute + review** 합쳐진 fused workflow 다 — 둘 다 stage iteration 마다 실행해야 함 (`/hm:loop` step 6: "Invoke per-iter workflow: read .claude/commands/hm/<WORKFLOW>.md and execute every stage it defines, in order, without skipping any"). PLAN-onboarding-backup-friction 6 iter 진행 중 LLM (= autoloop driver) 가 review stage 를 매 iter 에서 silently skip 하고 mechanical gate (ruff/mypy/pytest) 만으로 진행 → loop_context 의 "review grade = A" exit criterion 평가 안 됨. 사용자가 명시 질문 ("각 스텝별로 review 단계를 거친거야?") 후에야 발각. 발생 원인: full review (5 reviewer × 2-pass redacted × consensus) × 6 phase = 30+ reviewer agent invocations 가 context budget 위협 → LLM 의 자기 보존 휴리스틱이 review 를 "선택적" 으로 재해석. 두 가지 위험: (a) Phase 1+3 같은 medium-high risk phase 에 reviewer scrutiny 없이 진행 (validator 가 critical #1 schema-aware discriminator 를 잡았듯 reviewer 가 추가 P0/P1 catch 잠재), (b) 사용자 trust 침해 (loop discipline 위반). 처방: (1) loop body iter 마다 review 명시 invoke — 컨텍스트 부족하면 "review skipped — running cumulative review at loop close" 로 명시 결정 + 사용자에게 disclose, (2) cumulative review (loop 끝에서 전체 diff 1회) 를 차선책으로 채택 시 LOOP context 의 exit_criteria_checklist 에 "cumulative review grade ≥ A" 명시. 이번 case 는 loop close 시 `/hm:review` 1회 돌리고 5 P1 manual-only finding (security TOCTOU + ux+code/render P1 들) 발견 → orchestrator 가 inline fix 했지만 strict consensus rubric 은 Grade A 반환 (cross-reviewer surface-match 부재 → manual-only) 이라는 rubric anti-coverage 도 동시 노출. 같은 류 회피 패턴: `[wiki:pattern] interview-mid-round-reframe-pattern` 처럼 stage discipline 의 underlying assumption 을 사용자에게 surface 한 다음 진행.

## [wiki:gotcha] hash-marker-syntax-not-start-end | 2026-05-22
v0.23.0 출시 후 Phase 2 render-merge follow-up 작업 시 발각 — 내가 작성한 모든 hash-comment 마커 (`# @hm:user:start:NAME` / `# @hm:user:end:NAME`) 가 inert 였음. **올바른 syntax**: `# @hm:user:NAME` (open) + `# @hm:/user:NAME` (close, slash on KIND). HTML 도 동일 패턴: `<!-- @hm:user:NAME -->` + `<!-- @hm:/user:NAME -->`. v0.23.0 의 codex/config.toml.j2, codex/agent.toml.j2 가 `:start:` / `:end:` infix 로 ship — `_HASH_OPEN_RE = r"^[ \t]*#[ \t]*@hm:(block|user|harness):({_ID_PATTERN})[ \t]*$"` 에 매치 안 됨 (start/end 가 ID 자리에 끼어들어가 ID_PATTERN 거부). 하지만 `_ANY_HASH_MARKER_RE` 는 lax (`#[ \t]*@hm:/?(?:block|user|harness):`) 라 `has_markers` 는 True 반환 → reconcile 이 MERGE_BLOCK 결정 → render 가 merge 시도 → parse_segments 가 `[]` 반환 (구조적으로 마커 없음 인식) → user content drop. 발각 경로: v0.23.0 의 e2e TOML xfail-strict test 가 v0.23.1 에서 merge engine 추가 후에도 xfailed 유지 → 디버그 → has_markers True 면서 parse_segments [] 인 모순 → regex 비교 → 잘못된 syntax 발각. 처방: HASH/HTML marker 신규 작성 시 항상 grep 기존 코드베이스 (`grep -rn "@hm:user:" src/harness_maker/templates`) 로 convention 확인. claude-md, agent body, wiki/failures memory 가 전부 `@hm:user:NAME` / `@hm:/user:NAME` (no start/end infix). 추가 처방: marker 도입 시 isolated `parse_segments` 단위 검증 1건 (segments list 비어있지 않은지) 추가 — has_markers + reconcile decision 만 보면 lax-regex false-positive 에 속음. v0.23.0 footgun caveat 은 CHANGELOG + preservation-matrix doc 에 명시 보존 (사용자 신뢰).

## [wiki:pattern] audit-convergence-aware-l2 | 2026-05-22
`/hm:health` 의 personalization L2 stability layer 가 override-count 만 보고 score 매기던 구조 → 사용자가 새 schema 로 migrate (예: `memory.{failures, wiki, session_dir}` → `{dir, files}`) 하면 ~30일간 "instability" false-positive. v0.23.2 ADR-0012: formula 동일, 입력 집합 narrow — `_load_preset_defaults(preset)` 가 `templates/harness-yaml/<preset>.yaml.j2` 를 `synthesize.synthesize()` 로 build 한 `HarnessConfig` 로 한번 render + `yaml.safe_load`. `_converged_on_default(record, defaults)` truth table: `after == default` → convergent; `after is None` (parent 든 leaf 든) → convergent (clearing 은 다음 render 가 덮어쓰므로 no lasting effect); 그 외 → divergent. 핵심 invariant: 같은 `recent_divergent` 리스트가 `compute_l2_stability` 의 score 와 `_action_for_frequent_axis` 의 Counter 양쪽에 feed (ADR-003) — score 와 surfaced action 이 절대 disagree 못 함. `compute_l2_stability` 는 back-compat 위해 `int | list[OverrideRecord]` overload + `current_defaults` kwarg (None 이면 legacy un-filtered). `run_audit` 의 baseline load 는 `except Exception` 로 wrap (ADR-001: audit는 health 를 절대 crash 안 시킴) — 실패 시 stderr 경고 + legacy fallback. 같은 패턴 적용 가능: 다른 audit/score layer 가 historic-state count 기반이면 "current-state convergence" 필터 추가 검토 (override stability 외에도 deprecated-flag count, dead-config count 등에 잠재).

## [wiki:gotcha] codex-marketplace-readme-overpromise | 2026-05-22
README 가 `codex plugin marketplace add Ecro/harness-maker` 를 install path 로 promise 했으나, 실제 시도하면 marketplace 등록만 되고 `codex plugin add harness-maker@harness-maker` 가 `plugin 'harness-maker' was not found in marketplace 'harness-maker'` 로 실패 — 우리가 Codex `marketplace.json` 을 ship 하지 않기 때문. `.codex-plugin/plugin.json` 단일 파일만으론 Codex plugin discovery 가 안 됨 (vs Claude/Cursor 는 `.{claude,cursor}-plugin/plugin.json` 단독으로 marketplace.json 없이도 install OK — IDE 마다 다른 contract). 발각 경로: 2026-05-22 spoton 에서 SessionStart hook + profiles config 두 Codex 버그 fix 후 사용자가 "README 의 Codex install 도 진짜 작동하나" 물어 검증 → 위 실패 재현. 처방 (PLAN-readme-codex-truthification): README + README.ko + BOOTSTRAP + 양 MANUAL_CHECKLIST + .codex-plugin/plugin.json description 모두 truthify — Claude Code marketplace 또는 PyPI 가 canonical install path 로 명시; `.codex-plugin/plugin.json` 은 future native install 위한 stub 으로 description 에 명시. 같은 클래스 사례: `[wiki:gotcha] readme-one-prompt-bash-not-slash` (2026-05-19) — README 가 user-typed `/plugin` 명령을 promise 했지만 AI agent 는 built-in slash 를 invoke 못 함. **공통 패턴**: "README 가 multi-IDE parity 를 promise → 실제로는 IDE 하나만 작동" — 2회째. 3회째 발생 시 CI install-command e2e test 즉시 추가 (ADR-001 의 deferred Q4 P0 promote trigger).

## [wiki:lesson] install-cmd-cifence-q4-fired | 2026-05-23
**Q4 trigger fired.** 2026-05-22 같은 날 codex-marketplace-readme-overpromise 패치 직후 사용자가 prompt body 의 "invoke harness-maker:make via the Skill tool" 줄을 더 의심해서 확인 → Codex first-run 에는 `.agents/skills/hm-make/` 가 아직 없음 (skills 는 `harness-maker make` 가 *생성*하는 산출물). AI agent 가 Skill tool 로 호출 시도하면 stall. **공통 패턴 3회째**: (1) `readme-one-prompt-bash-not-slash` (2026-05-19, AI 는 built-in slash 호출 불가), (2) `codex-marketplace-readme-overpromise` (2026-05-22, codex marketplace install 거짓), (3) Codex first-run Skill-tool overpromise (2026-05-22). 4일 안에 3회 — ADR-001 of PLAN-readme-codex-truthification 의 deferred Q4 trigger 발동. **응답 (PLAN-install-cmd-cifence)**: (a) README + README.ko Codex branch 에 "Skill tool not yet populated" canonical anchor + Bash-direct `harness-maker make` 지시 추가, (b) `tests/integration/test_readme_install_commands.py` + `_install_helpers.py` — positive PyPI install + Cursor git-clone path + README allowlist drift (모두 BLOCKING per ADR-002 Round 2 amend) + codex marketplace negative test (ADVISORY via `@pytest.mark.advisory` + workflow `continue-on-error: true`). (c) `ci.yml` 새 `install-cmd-regression` job (`needs: quality-gate`) — npm install codex + 2 separate pytest steps (BLOCKING / ADVISORY). 0.23.5 patch tag. **재발 trigger**: 4번째 발생 시 → "full integrated CI runner with all 3 IDE binaries" 승격 (codex + claude + cursor 셋 다 CI 안에서 install + actual session smoke). 현재 single-mechanism defense (lint allowlist + positive install + advisory negative) 가 충분치 않다는 증거.

## [wiki:pattern] opt-in-feedback-module-architecture | 2026-05-23
**0.24.0** PLAN-auto-feedback-2026-05 — maintainer-dogfooding feedback drafts that **preserve** the no-network contract instead of carving exceptions. Three architectural choices made the privacy-promise compatibility unbroken: **(1) Single toggle surface** — `harness.yaml.feedback.enabled: bool` (default `false`), togglable only via `/hm:configure`; no CLI flag, no env var (mirror of PLAN-oss-readiness-audit ADR-004 "no opt-out env var" — same single-surface principle inverted for opt-in). **(2) In-band LLM judgment at the wrapper layer** — the Jinja-conditional block lives in `templates/commands/hm/atomic_command.md.j2` + `workflow_command.md.j2` (2-template touch), NOT in stage bodies (which are pre-rendered with a fixed context dict in `synthesize._atomic_command_files`); the current turn's LLM reads telemetry + decides + writes draft via Bash, so no `anthropic.Anthropic()` call from `feedback/*.py` and `tests/unit/test_no_network.py` is preserved verbatim with two new positive-obligation functions added (`test_telemetry_grep_no_network`, `test_draft_writer_no_network`). **(3) Free-text markdown body, not `bug.yml` form alignment** — `gh issue create --body-file` submits free-text that does NOT auto-populate form fields; trying to align via LLM-generated `reproduction_hint/expected_hint/actual_hint` would re-litigate the 5-field whitelist (validator W5 fix). Cross-refs: PLAN ADR-001..006, REVIEW round 1 P1-1 (task_slug path-traversal — `out_path = out_dir / f"{date}-{task_slug}-{hash}.md"` had no validator; fix added `_TASK_SLUG_PATTERN = r"^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,200}$"` field_validator + 3 regression tests). Privacy invariant grep: `tests/unit/test_privacy_doc_schema.py` extended with scoped `@hm:privacy:feedback-module` marker-block check that asserts every `FeedbackDraft` + `TriggerSignal` Pydantic field appears INSIDE the block (not globally — generic tokens like `id`/`count`/`duration_ms` would false-pass document-wide regex; validator C3 fix).

## [wiki:pattern] cross-session-worktree-defense-5-layer | 2026-05-24
**PLAN-worktree-cross-session-data-loss-defense** (3rd incident closure — `[fail:design] worktree-finalize-pulls-orphan-wip-into-main count:2 → 3`). 5 cooperating layers with concrete invariants — each catches a different failure mode, all must regress simultaneously to re-open data loss: **(1) queue-guard** — `_count_pending_stashes(claude_dir) >= 2` blocks `worktree create` with `--allow-stash-queue` escape. **(2) dirty-base-guard** — `_has_user_dirty_state(base)` uses separate `_is_create_guard_harness_artifact` filter (recognizes full `.claude/` dir as harness-managed at CREATE time only — finalize-stash still uses the narrower `_is_harness_artifact` so user `.claude/` edits get preserved). `--allow-dirty-base` escape. **(3) Session UUID dirname embed** (`execute-{uuid12}-{ts}`) — `_extract_uuid_from_wt_name` parses durable create→finalize binding; `_write_stash_ref_file` extracts UUID from `wt_name` (not project-shared file). Original "persistent shared UUID file" approach was REVIEW-caught P0 (Layer 3 completely non-functional). **(4) merge fence** — `_acquire_merge_fence` ctx mgr; primary `fcntl.flock`, secondary `os.open(O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_WRONLY)` polling (reliable on WSL2/NTFS where flock can silently degrade). Critical: `lock_dir` resolved via `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` so all worktrees of same repo share the lockfile (was REVIEW-caught P0: naive `.git` is_dir() check returns False on worktrees → per-wt lockfile → no serialization). **(5) scope-guard** — `_verify_scope_subset(base, wt_branch, staged_before)` returns `(ok, contamination)` after merge; `staged_before` captured BEFORE merge correctly handles `--allow-dirty-base` interaction (no false-positive on pre-existing staged content). Wired warn-only in `_cli_finalize`. Uses `git merge-base wt_branch HEAD` instead of hardcoded `main` (REVIEW P1-MAN1). **Forensic guarantee:** every bypass flag use logs `[WARN]` to stderr so post-incident analysis can distinguish "guard fired and was bypassed" from "guard never fired". **Layer 3 wiring gap (known follow-up task #14):** wrapup template must set `HM_OWNED_SESSION_UUIDS` env before invoking `post-commit-pop`; until then strict mode falls back to old marker-exists check.

## [wiki:pattern] iter-receipts-cli-atomic-state | 2026-05-23
Post-commit P0+P1 fix sweep on `63eea38` (PLAN-loop-mid-stop-and-review-skip). Three companion CLIs in `harness_maker.iter_receipts` close the atomicity gap that the prompt-driven shell-and-LLM model leaves: **(1) `set-iter-marker`** writes `<WT>/.claude/.hm-iter-receipts/.current-iter` via `atomic_write` (tempfile + os.replace), replacing the non-atomic `printf '%s' <N> > file` shell redirect — crash mid-write no longer leaves an empty file that breaks every subsequent stage receipt-emit. **(2) `patch-runtime`** atomically patches one field of `runtime: stage_retry_counts | checklist_fail_counts | criterion_ambiguity_counts | convergence_streak` in `work-docs/loop-context/<slug>.yaml` — replaces prompt-driven plain YAML rewrites that were vulnerable to corruption on WSL2/NTFS. Round-trips through pydantic (`LoopContext.runtime: RuntimeBlock | None`) so the file stays schema-valid; legacy YAML without `runtime:` still loads cleanly. **(3) `--written-at` test-only flag** — gated behind `HM_TEST_RECEIPTS=1` env, `argparse.SUPPRESS`-hidden from `--help`; prevents operator backdating that would corrupt the Gate 0 audit trail. **Schema fix (P0):** added `RuntimeBlock` + `LastTestResult` pydantic models + `runtime: RuntimeBlock | None = None` field on `LoopContext`. Without this addition, every Gate 0 retry that persisted `stage_retry_counts` would cause the next `parse_loop_context` to raise ValidationError (extra='forbid') — `/compact` recovery permanently broken on any non-trivial loop. **Gate 0 coverage fix (P0):** added Emit Gate 0 receipt block to `verify.md.j2` (PRODUCTION default `exec-rev-wrap-ver` requires verify receipt) + `STAGE_NAMES` in `test_render_stage_receipts.py` includes `verify`. **TOCTOU-safe shell guard pattern:** all 7 stage templates upgraded to `if [ -f "<WT>/.../.current-iter" ]; then ITER=$(cat "..." 2>/dev/null); if [ -n "$ITER" ]; then CLI; fi; fi` — `<WT>` quoted, empty-string post-check, single-cat read.

## [wiki:architecture] worktree-artifact-janitor | 2026-05-25
PLAN-harness-artifact-leak shipped the create-time janitor for harness-owned worktree artifacts. `worktree create` now runs `prune_stale(base)` before queue/dirty guards unless `--debug-worktree` is set. The janitor removes orphan `.hm-loop-*` markers, removes dangling owned `.worktrees/{execute,plan,phase,autoloop}-*` directories only when they have a `.git` entry and are neither git-registered nor live-marker referenced, and handles finalize-stash refs via a HEAD-blob-membership gate over both tracked content and the optional untracked `S^3` tree. Queue pressure now counts only validated refs whose `session_marker` still exists; stale preserved refs warn but do not block multi-session worktree creation. Render manifests are compacted at render-pass start by preserving the latest line per `(path, content_hash)` pair, which keeps reconcile set membership unchanged while bounding duplicate growth.

## [wiki:pattern] loop-mechanical-receipt-gate | 2026-05-23
**PLAN-loop-mid-stop-and-review-skip** (Phases 1-4 shipped this commit, 5-6 deferred). Closes the 2026-05-22 silent-review-skip failure mode (`[wiki:gotcha] loop-body-skipping-review-stage`) with a **5-gate convergence** — Gate 0 added before existing Gate 1-4. Three-layer mechanical defense: **(1) `harness_maker.iter_receipts` module** (Phase 1, ADR-004) — strict pydantic schema `{iter:int>=1, stage, verdict:'pass'|'fail'|'skipped', written_at:ISO}` with filename-safe stage regex + atomic write + CLI (write/read/list/verify). Verdict `skipped` is reserved for ADR-005 escape hatch; stages emit `pass` or `fail`. **(2) Stage templates emit receipts** (Phase 2, 6 .j2 templates — execute/review/wrapup/plan/spec/research). Shell guard `if [ -f .current-iter ]; then ... fi` makes the write a no-op when no autoloop is active (standalone runs / no isolation / post-`/compact` pre-iter-1 — all skip naturally). Codex variant via `printf '%s'` newline-safe. **(3) `loop.md.j2` Gate 0 wiring** (Phase 3, ADR-001 + ADR-005) — driver writes `<WT>/.claude/.hm-iter-receipts/.current-iter` at iter start (Step 3.5), runs `iter_receipts verify --iter N --expected ...` after workflow returns (Step 4.5), auto-retries missing stage (cap=2 per (iter,stage)). Critical invariants in prose: Gate 0 auto-retries do NOT increment `failed_streak`; `verdict != "pass"` counts as failure (closes "forge skipped to bypass" loophole). Step 7 (loop close) clears `stage_retry_counts` to prevent unbounded YAML growth. **(4) `plan-exec-rev` fused workflow** (Phase 4, ADR-002) — 3-stage (plan+execute+review, no wrapup since loop-close owns it; distinct from existing 4-stage `plan-exec-rev-wrap`). `/hm:plan` Step 1.5 detects `.hm-loop-active` marker → skips deep interview, writes per-iter scoped plan to `<WT>/work-docs/PLAN-{slug}-iter{N}.md` (ADR-008). ADR-007 loop-mode signal = marker presence (no flag). Loop-mode ADR pivot constraint: if implementation reveals a true ADR-worthy decision, HALT loop (autoloop has no AskUserQuestion channel for new ADRs). Phases 5-6 (state-pin file + post-`/compact` recovery + docs) deferred to follow-up.

## [wiki:gotcha] orphan-stash-registration-drain-manual | 2026-05-24
PLAN-worktree-cross-session-data-loss-defense Layer 1 (queue-guard) + Layer 3 (Session UUID strict mode) interact to leave **pre-defense orphan registrations indrainable by automation**. Specifically: 4 `.claude/.hm-finalize-stash-execute-2026-05-23T*Z` registration files persisted from morning sessions whose `/hm:wrapup` was never invoked (the same incident that triggered the defense's 3rd-recurrence shipment that very afternoon). On the NEXT session: (a) Layer 1 fires `[ERROR] worktree create blocked — ≥2 unpopped finalize stashes detected`, (b) `worktree post-commit-pop` in Layer 3 strict mode (`HM_OWNED_SESSION_UUIDS=""` because there's no live `.claude/.hm-loop-*` marker for current session yet) SKIPs all 4 stashes because their `session_uuid` is not in the empty owned set, (c) registrations therefore never drain via CLI. **The drain procedure for cross-session orphans (when content is already in main via cherry-pick or redone work):** (1) Per ADR-008 LLM behavior contract, NEVER drop without `git stash show -p <ref>` preview. (2) For each registration, read `ref_sha` field, run `git stash show <ref_sha>` to enumerate touched files, then `git log --all --oneline --grep=...` to verify content landed in main. (3) When verified safe, delete `.claude/.hm-finalize-stash-*` registration files only — the underlying stash refs remain in git object store for ~90-day gc grace (recovery via `git cat-file -p <sha>` or `git cherry-pick <sha>` still possible). (4) Worktree create then proceeds. **Permanent lesson:** the 5-layer defense PREVENTS new cross-session orphans (queue-guard halts immediately on ≥2), but provides NO automation to drain pre-defense leftovers. Manual registration-file deletion is the canonical drain path. The "Layer 3 wiring gap (follow-up task #14)" called out in `[wiki:cross-session-worktree-defense-5-layer]` would auto-skip but still wouldn't auto-drain — drain requires content-verified consent.

## [wiki:architecture] codex-second-llm-integration | 2026-05-24
PLAN-codex-second-llm-integration shipped: Claude→Codex routing for cross-model second opinions on `code-reviewer` / `consensus-arbiter` / `plan-validator`. Transport is `codex exec` Bash dispatch only (no MCP — ADR-001). New `harness.yaml.codex_second_opinion` block (default disabled). Architecture invariants: (1) **Permission injection via Jinja conditional in agent template frontmatter** (ADR-007 — `_merge_permissions` only applies to settings.json, NOT agent .md; the partial uses `{%- if -%}` + `{%- endif -%}` dashes so byte-zero in disabled state). (2) **Schema rendering reuses existing `_render_pure_json`** (ADR-008 P-W3 — `_render_pure_json` already existed at render.py:512 for hooks.json/mcp.json; ADR-008's NEW code is the `_is_schemas_json` predicate + dispatch wire-up). (3) **`.claude/schemas/*.json` is pure JSON, no provenance frontmatter** (external consumer is `codex exec --output-schema`). (4) **`codex_second_opinion` is orthogonal to `targets`** (ADR-009 — works even when `codex` is not in targets; user just needs `codex` CLI + `codex login`). (5) **Security boundary tightened in review Round 2**: `output_schema_path` gained field validator (reject absolute / `..` / shell metacharacters); consensus-arbiter + plan-validator got full deny baseline (was missing — only `code-reviewer` had it); heredoc terminator collision (adversarial diff with bare `PROMPT` line) replaced with `mktemp` tmpfile + stdin redirect. (6) **Forward-compat**: legacy harness.yaml without the key loads via `default_factory`; reverse mapper tolerant-fallback pattern (matches `feedback` precedent). Per-field round-trip: enabled/agents/hermetic/failure_policy/output_schema_path all interpolate in Production+Side yaml templates via `| tojson`. Test pinning: `test_agent_body_partials._EXPECTED_SHA256` baselines bumped for the 2 agents with new permissions blocks; 8 snapshot fixtures regen with harness.yaml-only hash change.

## [wiki:gotcha] extend-rendered-agent-json-via-shared-partial | 2026-05-25
When a shared partial (e.g. `_partials/second_opinion_codex.md.j2`) adds keys to a reviewer agent's JSON output, the agent's **canonical schema block** AND the **dispatch-site prompt** must be updated too — not just the partial. PLAN-codex-mandatory-second-opinion added top-level `codex_status`/`codex_reconciliation` to `plan-validator` via the partial, but `plan-validator_body.md.j2:54` says "Return ONLY this JSON. No prose preamble." over a 3-key example, and `stages/plan.md.j2`'s Step-4 `Task(...)` dispatch prompt independently restated `Return JSON: {overall, critiques}`. A literal LLM anchors on the strongest/earliest schema and silently drops the appended keys → the downstream relay reads a missing field and no-ops. code-reviewer caught this as a P1 in REVIEW. Fix pattern: (a) make the partial explicitly say "this EXTENDS the `## Output JSON Schema` above — despite 'Return ONLY this JSON', you MUST also carry these top-level keys"; (b) gate-extend the dispatch prompt with the same `{% if config.codex_second_opinion.enabled %}` so all three anchors agree. Rule: an agent's output contract often lives in ≥3 places (canonical schema block, dispatch Task prompt, appended partial) — grep them all when changing output shape.

## [wiki:pattern] branch-shared-partial-on-name-for-per-agent-divergence | 2026-05-25
To diverge a shared partial's behavior per consuming agent, branch on the in-scope `name` variable: `{% if name == 'plan-validator' -%}…mandatory…{%- else -%}…opt-in…{%- endif %}` inside the existing `{%- if enabled and name in agents %}` guard. Keeps the byte-zero-when-disabled outer guard intact and the `@hm:codex-second-opinion` marker emitted in both branches (tests assert marker presence for all enabled agents). Used to make Codex mandatory for `plan-validator` only while `code-reviewer`/`consensus-arbiter` keep opt-in (their top-level JSON **array** output can't host a reconciliation envelope — `merge_passes` in two_pass_review.py rebuilds a list and strips it; deferred to a follow-up PLAN). Verify byte-zero-when-disabled empirically: render both states to tmp and diff after masking the pkg-root path + content_hash, rather than reasoning about `trim_blocks` whitespace abstractly.

## [wiki:pattern] verify-owns-full-suite-before-wrapup | 2026-05-25
Canonical Production workflow now puts `verify` before `wrapup`; wrapup should reuse a deterministic verification marker when the code/test-relevant fingerprint is unchanged. The marker contract must include every gate that wrapup would otherwise run (`lint,format,mypy,pytest`) and must ignore wrapup-only churn like memory, work-docs, review reports, and changelog edits. Safety invariant: if a later stage edits source, tests, lockfiles, tool config, CI, or harness templates, the relevant fingerprint changes and wrapup reruns the suite before commit.

## [wiki:gotcha] second-brain-vault-path-must-be-root | 2026-05-27
`harness.yaml.second_brain.vault_path` must point to the actual Obsidian vault root (where `.obsidian/` lives), NOT a subfolder. Subfolders are addressed via `folders[].path` relative to vault root. Misconfiguring vault_path to a non-existent subdir causes silent empty-result degradation — all searches return `[]` without error because the loader degrades gracefully when the vault directory is missing.

## [wiki:gotcha] orphan-sweep-keeps-provenance-stripped-renders | 2026-05-28
Files harness-maker renders via `_render_pure_text` (`.cursor/rules/*.mdc`, `.cursor/commands/*.md`) carry only the external consumer's frontmatter — our `generated_by`/`content_hash` provenance is stripped for Cursor's strict parser. So `reconcile._classify_orphan` cannot recognize them via the `generated_by == harness-maker` branch; it must fall back to a per-path full-file-hash check against `.hm-render-manifest.jsonl` (the same check the no-frontmatter branch already uses for pure JSON). Without that fallback, dropping a target (e.g. `cursor`) leaks the de-selected target's rendered assets forever. The per-path scoping (`manifest.get(rel_key)`, never a global hash union) is the R4 guard preventing deletion of a user file byte-identical to a harness render at a different path. When adding a new pure-text / provenance-stripped render path, add a matching orphan-sweep regression: one ours-clean delete + three KEEP cases (no manifest entry, edited→hash-miss, hash-under-different-path).

## [wiki:gotcha] hooks-merge-identity-keys-on-module-namespace | 2026-05-28
hooks.json dedup identity (`render._normalize_hm_managed_command` → `_entry_identity` → `_merge_hooks_json`) MUST key harness-owned hooks on their `python -m harness_maker.<invocation>` module-namespace suffix (module + trailing args), NOT on the `uv run --with <path>` prefix. The prefix is volatile: it changes on `/plugin update` (cache version), on a marketplace switch (`harness-maker-local` cache ↔ GitHub `harness-maker` cache), and for dev-repo installs (`/home/noel/...`). Any path-ENUMERATING regex re-opens the duplication bug on the next unforeseen path form (spoton triplication, 2026-05-28). `harness_maker.*` is our namespace, so a suffix match is proof of ownership; user-authored commands (other tooling) round-trip unchanged and are preserved. The merge self-heals already-duplicated files: stale-path on-disk entries normalize to the template identity and are dropped, leaving one entry per (event, matcher, module+args). Extends [wiki:pattern] schema-aware-json-merge-discriminator (05-22), which built the identity tuple but enumerated only the local-cache path.

## [wiki:gotcha] reconcile-phantom-hash-heal | 2026-05-28
reconcile's REPLACE-vs-KEEP gate trusts `stored content_hash == compute_body_hash(body)` as the SOLE "did the user edit this?" signal. Pre-0.26.2 the Codex skill pre-render path (synthesize pre-renders stage/loop bodies with the install_ref `uv run --with <path>` baked in, hashed BEFORE substitution) wrote a content_hash over a body ≠ the persisted bytes — a "phantom" hash that can never self-verify under any normalization. reconcile then misread it as user-modified → KEEP → the file froze at its old version on EVERY re-render (`hm-execute/verify/wrapup/loop` stuck at 0.26.1 while sibling skills reached 0.26.6; symptom = stale `.../0.26.1` paths after an update touched only 4 files). Orphan-sweep can't help: these are blueprint files, not orphans. Fix: `reconcile._is_healable_phantom_ours` flips KEEP→REPLACE for `generated_by: harness-maker` + `source_template ∈ {codex/stage_skill,loop_skill}.md.j2` (STABLE key, never version/path enumeration) + `harness_maker_version` < 0.26.2 floor (protects current/future edits) + KEEP-reason ∈ {user-modified, malformed-markers}; the CLI's pre-render `.backup-<ts>/` covers the residual pre-floor skill-body-edit case. Lesson: "stored hash mismatches body" ≠ "user edited it" — a render-time hash/body divergence yields the same signal, so any unverifiable-hash class needs an explicit scoped heal or it silently freezes forever. Render must always hash the EXACT bytes it persists (current code does; the bug was pre-0.26.2).

## [wiki:architecture] second-brain-promotion-pipeline | 2026-05-28
Local `.claude/memory/` (wiki/failures/session) and the Obsidian Second Brain are a PROMOTION PIPELINE, not two parallel stores: local = project working memory (written every wrapup Step 5.1–5.5), Obsidian = curated cross-project durable layer. wrapup **Step 5.6** (`second_brain promote`) escalates the cross-project-durable subset. KEY LESSON: an LLM-facing prompt instruction that is *advisory/floating* (not a numbered procedure step) gets silently skipped ~100% of the time — the vault sat empty for weeks because the only write path was an advisory preamble competing with the concrete numbered Step 5. Fix = numbered **MUST-evaluate** step + an observable `promotion evaluated: N candidates, M promoted` receipt (no count-gate, to avoid synthetic-note pressure). `promote_note` is the idempotency safety rail: deterministic `<type>-<slug>.md` filename + `project_id`/`hm_source` link-back + dedup via `write_note`; `_resolve_authorized` picks the **longest-root** matching folder so nested per-type writable folders don't shadow each other. Stage SB sections use PROSE gating (always render + runtime `if enabled` check), NOT Jinja `{% if %}` — sibling consistency + `test_stage_aware_second_brain_guidance` require the terms to render unconditionally.

## [wiki:architecture] worktree-keep-base-clean-churn-isolation | 2026-05-29
PLAN-worktree-base-artifact-pollution. The 5-layer cross-session worktree defense (queue-guard, dirty-base-guard, session-UUID, merge-fence, scope-guard) was firing CONSTANTLY on the harness's OWN churn — telemetry writes `.claude/observability/metrics-*.jsonl` on EVERY tool call (PostToolUse hook), so `git status` is never clean → finalize stashes every run → queue-guard blocks the next parallel `create`. Root cause was a two-filter asymmetry: the finalize filter `_is_harness_artifact` forgave only 3 narrow prefixes while the create filter forgave all of `.claude/`; neither forgave `work-docs/`. Fix = "keep the base clean so the defense goes quiet" (NOT a 6th layer): one shared churn source of truth — `_HARNESS_CHURN_DIRS` (prefix-matched) + `_HARNESS_CHURN_FILES` (EXACT-matched, so `…p5-batch-state.yaml.bak` isn't forgiven) unioned into `_HARNESS_GITIGNORE_PATTERNS` — drives BOTH dirt-filters AND a `.gitignore` seeding helper (`_ensure_harness_gitignore`, idempotent + subsumption-safe via `git check-ignore`, called at make + every create). Two non-obvious invariants: (1) the finalize filter must stay a STRICT SUBSET — never forgive `.claude/agents|skills|commands|harness.yaml` — or genuine user edits get silently merged instead of stash-preserved. (2) `.gitignore` ITSELF must be recognized as co-managed/non-dirtying, because the seeding append makes `M .gitignore` which would otherwise trip the dirty-base guard AND get stashed at finalize — the migration would recreate the churn it removes. Safe because the finalize squash only carries the worktree-branch diff and never touches the base `.gitignore`. The dogfood repo was already clean (hand-rolled `.gitignore` ignoring all `.claude/`+`work-docs/`) — that was the existence proof; downstream user repos got only 4 appended patterns and were unprotected.

## [wiki:architecture] spec-test-forward-binding | 2026-05-30
PLAN-spec-test-accumulation (0.28.0). `/hm:execute` now reads `SPEC-{slug}.machine.yaml` and authors predicate-bound tests for *mechanical* ACs (executable_predicate → assertion), so the AC→test→mutation graph accumulates FORWARD during normal feature work instead of being reverse-mapped by the spec-coverage backfill loop. Five-part design: (1) `spec_machine.validate` tightened — a mechanical AC's `executable_predicate` must `ast.parse(mode="eval")` to an assertable node (Compare/Call/BoolOp/UnaryOp) referencing ≥1 symbol; prose + `True` rejected (ADR-007). (2) write-back (`mark_tested`) lives in `/hm:wrapup` in the BASE repo AFTER finalize — chosen over in-worktree write to fix two criticals at once: cross_validate's collection cwd is `md_path.parent.parent`=base so the merged test resolves, AND there is no cross-session worktree-stash race (ADR-005). (3) T1-only mutation gate on the execute hot path; T2/T3 deferred to loop (ADR-003). (4) silent scenario-path fallback when no machine.yaml / no parseable-predicate AC (ADR-004). (5) `spec_drift` "resolved-but-pending" detector surfaces the wrapup-gated write-back miss in `/hm:health` (ADR-009). KEY LESSON: adding a new instruction to a stage template that invokes `python -m harness_maker.X` requires X to actually HAVE that CLI — `spec_machine`/`spec_mutation` had none, so the pre-existing `spec.md.j2` `validate` call was already dead. Always make the module's `__main__` real in the same change.

## [wiki:gotcha] mock-only-test-hides-consumer-format-bug | 2026-05-30
`spec_machine._check_pytest_collect` ran `pytest --collect-only` WITHOUT `-q`; that emits an indented `<Function ...>` tree with NO `::` nodeids, so the parser found zero nodeids and reported EVERY test_id as unresolved — rule-3 was silently broken for all real usage. It passed CI for months because every rule-3 test `monkeypatch`ed `_check_pytest_collect` away. The bug only surfaced when `mark_tested` (new) exercised the helper against a REAL pytest tree. Generalization (sibling of `[fail:design] producer-consumer-schema-drift`): a pure-function helper whose output format depends on an EXTERNAL tool's stdout MUST have ≥1 unmocked test that runs the real tool — mocking the helper everywhere proves the callers' logic but never the helper's contract against its actual consumer. Fix: `-q` + return-code-aware degradation (`not collected → return all-unresolved iff returncode!=0 else []`), guarded by `test_mark_tested_real_pytest_collect_lifecycle`.

## [wiki:architecture] agent-model-alias-not-pinned | 2026-05-31
Agent `model:` frontmatter in `.claude/agents/*.md` MUST render a version-agnostic
Claude alias (`opus`/`sonnet`/`haiku`), never a pinned concrete id. Claude Code now
*respects* the field (#43869 fixed); it was previously treated as decorative, so the
dispatcher templates preferred `{{ cursor_model }}` (the CURSOR_MODEL_IDS concrete id,
e.g. `claude-4-7-opus`) over `{{ claude_model }}`. Once the upstream fix landed, that
stale pinned id failed to launch any subagent in a newer-model session (0 tool uses) —
the same symptom as [fail:review] reviewer-subagent-model-unsupported. Fix
(PLAN-agent-model-version-agnostic): a shared `_partials/model_frontmatter_line.md.j2`
renders the alias; `default_model` floor defaults to `opus`. **Two-namespace boundary
(ADR-004/006):** agent-launch surfaces (Claude Code) take ALIASES (resolved at launch);
Anthropic-API surfaces (aider/Continue foreign configs, the Python SDK `messages.create`
constants) require CONCRETE ids and resolve via a SEPARATE `foreign_config._FOREIGN_MODEL_IDS`
map — `CURSOR_MODEL_IDS` holds Cursor's reversed-format ids (`claude-4-7-opus`) which are
NOT valid Anthropic API ids, a different namespace. Guard: `test_agent_model_alias_rendering`
renders the real pipeline and fails if any concrete id reaches an agent `model:` line.
Lesson: a field assumed "decorative" becomes a latent bug the moment upstream makes it
load-bearing — pin nothing that an alias can express.

## [wiki:pattern] prompt-template-shared-partials-dedup | 2026-05-31
Prose blocks duplicated across the 7 `templates/stages/*.md.j2` (the Gate-0 receipt block,
identical modulo stage name) are single-sourced via a PARAMETERIZED Jinja partial under
`agents/_partials/` — each call site does `{% set gate0_* %}` then `{% include %}`. Optional
per-stage fields stay StrictUndefined-safe via `{% if x is defined and x %}` (only the one
stage that needs it sets the var). Verify a "pure dedup" refactor with a GOLDEN-MASTER render
diff (render a fixture to a tmp dir before AND after, `diff` every rendered command — must be
byte-identical) PLUS the snapshot suite, because the Jinja env is `trim_blocks=False,
lstrip_blocks=False` (render.py:62-64) so whitespace is fragile and a stray include newline
silently shifts output. KEY GOTCHA: do NOT trust an audit/RESEARCH "byte-for-byte duplicated
×N" claim — DIFF the copies first. Here the 5-term inequality gate was asserted to be 3
identical copies but was actually DRIFTED stage-specific prose (term-1 suffix "research
direction" vs "SPEC content", per-stage frontmatter lists, F6 comment length); only the 4-line
formula was truly identical. Extracting the "duplicated" prose would have forced false
convergence and lost per-stage nuance. Rule: a refactor premised on "these are duplicates"
must show the diff proving identity before extraction.

## [wiki:gotcha] loop-marker-check-must-resolve-from-worktree | 2026-05-31
A prompt-level "skip X if the `.hm-loop-active` marker exists **at the project root**"
instruction is WRONG when the executing context's cwd is inside a worktree. The autoloop
writes `.hm-loop-active` at the project root (`loop.md.j2` `touch`), but the per-iter
driver runs with cwd inside `<WT>` = `.worktrees/<name>/`, which is a SIBLING under the
project, not an ancestor — so a naive "is the marker at the project root" check from the
worktree misses it. The correct form (already canonical in `plan.md.j2:101-104` and
enforced by `hooks/loop_gate.py:_worktree_parent_marker`) tells the LLM how to RESOLVE the
project root first: strip the `/.worktrees/<wt-name>/` suffix, or `git rev-parse
--show-toplevel` and walk out of `.worktrees/`. The step-manifest partial (ADR-003) shipped
the naive form and would have flooded every loop iteration; caught only because the REVIEW
ran TWO reviewers on disjoint lenses — the render/wiring reviewer saw nothing wrong, the
BEHAVIORAL reviewer caught it. Lesson: any new marker/path check that can run from a
worktree MUST mirror the project-root resolution precedent, and reviewing prompt changes on
a single lens (syntax/render) misses runtime-context bugs.

## [wiki:gotcha] codex-bash-syntax-parity-on-shared-render | 2026-05-31
Any command/skill content that is rendered into BOTH the Claude target AND the Codex target
(e.g. `loop.md.j2` → `/hm:loop` + `.agents/skills/hm-loop/SKILL.md` via `synthesize.py`'s
`is_codex=True` render; same for the extracted `loop-p5-batch.md.j2`) MUST branch every shell
block: `{% if is_codex %}Bash("…"){% else %}!…{% endif %}`. Codex does NOT execute the
Claude-CLI `!`-prefix run syntax — it expects `Bash(...)` tool calls — so an unbranched
`!uv run …` block renders as INERT PROSE on the codex target. Caught in Phase 3: the P5-batch
section had unbranched `!uv run python -c` blocks (pre-existing — the original loop section
never branched them either), so the new full-parity codex skill was dead-on-arrival.
Complication: multi-line `python -c "…"` heredocs don't fit Codex's single-line `Bash("…")`
form, which is WHY the original left them unbranched. Pragmatic mitigation when a clean
`Bash()` rewrite is too heavy: add a `{% if is_codex %}` note telling the codex model to run
each `!`-block via its Bash tool verbatim. Proper fix: extract multi-line scripts to a helper
module the skill calls as one line. Also: a test that only asserts "the codex skill file
exists" does NOT catch this — assert the body is codex-executable (branched or noted).

## [wiki:pattern] loosening-a-security-default-needs-health-scorer-optout | 2026-05-31
When you LOOSEN a security default (here: main-session `settings.json permissions.deny`
made empty-by-default so `rm`/`curl|sh` aren't blocked for solo work, opt-in via
`harness.yaml permissions.deny_dangerous: true`), you MUST also update any health/readiness
scorer that REWARDED the old default — else every fresh install silently loses points for a
deliberate config choice. `readiness._dim_guardrails` had two signals (`permissions_deny_present`
=20, `deny_covers_dangerous`=15 → 35 pts) that rewarded a non-empty dangerous deny; the fix makes
both read `harness.yaml permissions.deny_dangerous` and PASS (not penalize) when opted out — a
deliberate opt-out is a config choice, not a missing guardrail (they still enforce when opted in).
Implementation notes: new typed `PermissionsConfig(deny_dangerous=False)` on HarnessConfig via
`default_factory` → old harness.yaml (no key) stays back-compat; `settings/*.json.j2` branch on
`config.permissions.deny_dangerous` with NO synthesize wiring (the full `config` dump is already
injected into every FileEntry context at synthesize.py ~769). Reviewer AGENTS keep their own
read-only `Bash(rm:*)` deny — this toggle governs only the user's main session.

## [wiki:pattern] content-gated-orphan-branch-sweep | 2026-05-31
`prune_stale` (runs at every `worktree create`) now sweeps leaked `execute-*`/`plan-*`/`phase-*`/`autoloop-*` branches whose worktree dir is gone — closing the orphan-branch leak (`cleanup()` deliberately never runs `git branch -D`, to keep the `wip(execute)` recovery net alive while a worktree is live, so finalized branches leaked forever). The sweep is **content-gated, biased-to-preserve**: `_branch_content_in_head(base, branch)` returns True only when every blob the branch changed since its merge-base with HEAD is byte-identical in HEAD; any unresolvable ref / missing / mismatched blob → False → preserve+warn (never delete unsaved work). It mirrors `_stash_content_in_head` and **must NOT use `git branch --merged`** — a squash-merged tip is not a HEAD ancestor, so `--merged` would wrongly report it unmerged and miss it. Cross-session safety falls out for free: an in-flight session's stage-only-finalize branch (work staged, not yet committed by wrapup) is NOT in HEAD → preserved; once its wrapup commits, a later prune sweeps it. Live-skip keys on `_registered_worktree_paths` (ADR-002), not just `dir.exists()`. There is no `S^3` untracked-tree leg (unlike the stash predicate) because a branch has no untracked tree — untracked work was already captured into the `wip(execute)` commit. General principle worth carrying to any destructive cleanup of maybe-unsaved work: gate the delete on "content already preserved", default to preserve on ambiguity. See PLAN-p6-p7-worktree-finalize ADR-002; related [fail:design] worktree-finalize-pulls-orphan-wip-into-main.

## [wiki:pattern] merge-fence-wraps-full-critical-section | 2026-06-01
PLAN-p6-p7-worktree-finalize P2 (ADR-003) widened the Layer-4 merge fence in `_cli_finalize` to wrap **exactly** `{_stash_base_dirty, _snapshot_staged_paths, merge}` instead of only `merge()`. The bug it closed: `_stash_base_dirty` (`git stash push -u`) ran OUTSIDE the fence, so two parallel finalizes could stash the same base concurrently — the race the fence exists to prevent. Invariants that make the widening safe: (1) `staged_before` snapshot captured STRICTLY AFTER the stash (the scope-guard's `--allow-dirty-base` exemption reads the post-stash index — a load-bearing order; extracted to a named `_snapshot_staged_paths` so a test can pin it); (2) `_capture_pending_in_worktree` stays OUTSIDE the fence — it's worktree-side (per-worktree index), not base-side, so fencing it would only inflate hold-time; (3) `handed_off`/`_write_stash_ref_file`/`cleanup`/both pop paths stay OUTSIDE/after the fence (pop-inside-fence would be wrong); (4) `handed_off` computed AFTER the fence, inside the `if wt_rc==0` block, init `False` before the outer try so the rollback `finally` always has a value. **Timeout (CN1, supersedes ADR-003's original "keep 60s"):** because the fenced section now HOLDS the lock for up to `_GIT_TIMEOUT_LONG` (stash, 300s) + `_GIT_TIMEOUT` (merge, 60s), the *acquire* timeout was raised to `_FENCE_TIMEOUT = 360s` so a legitimately-slow first finalize doesn't spuriously TimeoutError a parallel second one. The P2 review left it at 60s as an ADR-003 watch-item; the follow-up CN1 raised it. **Pops fenced too (CN2, supersedes ADR-003's "pops stay outside the fence"):** both base-stash pops route through `_fenced_restore_base_dirty` (fence-wrapped `_restore_base_dirty` with an UNFENCED fallback on fence-acquire failure) so two parallel finalizes don't race the shared stash/`index.lock`. **Accepted narrow downside:** raising the acquire budget to 360s lengthens the O_EXCL *secondary* path's stale-lock stall if a holder is SIGKILL'd (flock — the WSL2 primary — auto-releases on death, so unaffected); self-heals via the unfenced fallback, no hang. A proper fix (mtime/PID staleness guard on `_excl_lock`) is a separate, TOCTOU-careful fence-primitive hardening, deliberately NOT rushed. General concurrency principle: **a lock must wrap the full critical section including its slow setup op, AND the acquire-timeout should exceed the worst-case hold** — widening one without the other trades a race for a spurious-timeout. Related [wiki:pattern] content-gated-orphan-branch-sweep (P1, same PLAN).

## [wiki:pattern] fail-safe-direction-when-unifying-divergent-parsers | 2026-06-01
PLAN-p6-p7-worktree-finalize P3 collapsed 3 divergent inline `git status --porcelain` parses into one `_porcelain_path` helper. Two of the 3 consumers (`_is_harness_artifact`, `_is_create_guard_harness_artifact`) FEED the Layer-2 dirty-base guard — they decide harness-churn (ignore) vs user-dirt (block create / stash before squash). The old copies differed in quote-stripping (`.strip('"')` vs `[1:-1]`-if-wrapped) and rename handling. The review heuristic that cleared the unification: **don't just ask "are they equivalent?" — ask "in the direction they CAN diverge, is the divergence fail-safe for the safety consumer?"** Here the new `[1:-1]` strips ≤ the old `.strip('"')`, so any divergence yields a path LESS likely to match a harness prefix → MORE lines classified as user-dirt → the guard fires MORE, never less → no contamination/data-loss regression (and `_stash_base_dirty` stashes more, not less). For real git porcelain the two are identical anyway (git quotes once, escapes interior quotes), so the divergence is theoretical — but proving the *direction* is fail-safe is what makes a parser-unification touching a safety gate reviewable as safe rather than just "looks equivalent." Companion polish in the same phase: batch N `git check-ignore` subprocesses into ONE `--stdin` call (read paths from stdin, parse the ignored subset from stdout) — but only safe because no churn pattern subsumes another, so the batch's read-once design can't diverge from the old append-one-at-a-time loop's intra-run subsumption.

## [wiki:gotcha] subagent-tools-field-hard-gates-bash-permission | 2026-06-02
In a Claude Code subagent, the `tools:` frontmatter is the HARD gate on tool *availability*; `permissions.allow`/`deny` only scope *which commands* run once a tool is available. So `permissions.allow: Bash(codex exec:*)` is **completely inert** when `tools:` omits `Bash` — the agent has no Bash tool at all. This silently broke `codex_second_opinion` on `code-reviewer` / `consensus-arbiter` / `plan-validator` (all `tools: Read, Grep, Glob`): the second opinion skipped with "validator env had no Bash" while the permission *looked* granted. The same trap had silently disabled `code-reviewer`'s `Bash(git diff:*)` / `git log` / `git status` allow-entries — they were unreachable for the same reason. Fix: add bare `Bash` to `tools:` (the field takes only bare tool names — it CANNOT argument-scope, so `Bash(codex exec:*)` is meaningless there; scoping stays in `permissions`). **Diagnostic to grep for: any agent with a `Bash(...)` entry in `permissions.allow` but no `Bash` token on its `tools:` line has an inert permission.** Granting bare Bash is safe only when the deny list is complete — keep the REVIEW-M7 interpreter quartet `Bash(python:*)`/`Bash(node:*)`/`Bash(sh:*)`/`Bash(bash:*)` (+ rm/curl/npm/eval), since with Bash available the deny list becomes the sole barrier and `Bash(sh -c "...")` would otherwise bypass rm/curl blocks. Cross-ref PLAN-spoton-codex-rm-stash-rootcause ADR-001.

## [wiki:gotcha] openai-codex-strict-output-schema-rules | 2026-06-03
OpenAI / Codex strict structured-output mode (`codex exec --output-schema <file>`) is NOT vanilla JSON-Schema validation — it enforces a narrow subset and rejects the whole schema (`invalid_json_schema`) when violated, silently degrading the caller to a prompt-pinned shape (retries + latency, eventual hard-fail). Two rules bite: **(1)** every object with `additionalProperties: false` MUST list EVERY key of its `properties` in `required` — there is no "optional"; encode optionality as a **nullable union type** (`{"type": ["string", "null"]}`), keeping the key required. **(2)** several Codex/OpenAI versions reject range/length/pattern constraint keywords (`minimum`/`maximum`/`minLength`/`maxLength`/`pattern`/`format`) — drop them; `enum` is fine. The 0.28.6 `codex-finding.schema.json` shipped with `confidence`/`file`/`line`/`evidence` declared-but-not-required + `minimum`/`minLength` present → rejected every plan-validator/code-reviewer/consensus-arbiter call. Fix (0.28.7, PLAN-codex-finding-schema-strict-mode ADR-001): all-properties-required + nullable optionals + constraints dropped. Guard: `tests/unit/test_schema_strict_mode.py` enforces the invariant over `templates/schemas/*.json`. The durable fix lives in the **source template** (the live `.claude/schemas/*.json` is a gitignored render artifact — re-rendered post-release via `/hm:make`, not committed). Cross-ref [[schema-walker-scans-property-names-as-keywords]].

## [wiki:gotcha] reconcile-keeps-frontmatterless-renders-forever | 2026-06-03
`reconcile()` decides KEEP/REPLACE/MERGE per rendered file by reading provenance frontmatter (`content_hash`). A rendered file that carries **no frontmatter** hits the `parse_frontmatter → fm is None → KEEP("no-frontmatter")` branch UNCONDITIONALLY (reconcile.py ~231) — there is NO manifest-hash fallback in the main loop (that lives only in the orphan sweep). So any pure-text/pure-JSON render with frontmatter stripped (`.claude/schemas/*.json` for codex `--output-schema`; also `.cursor/mcp.json`, `.cursor/rules/*.mdc`) **freezes on the user's disk forever** — a fixed template never reaches EXISTING installs on `/hm:make --update`, only fresh installs. This silently capped the reach of the 0.28.7 codex-schema fix. Fix pattern (0.28.8, PLAN-reconcile-schemas-always-replace ADR-001): for a frontmatter-less render with **zero user-editable content**, add a forced-REPLACE special-case BEFORE the no-frontmatter KEEP — exactly mirroring the `settings.json`/`config-always-replace`/`AGENTS.md` branches at reconcile.py:152-230 (`cli.py` routes on `.decision` not `.reason`, so a plain REPLACE flows through as overwrite; `backup()` runs first → recoverable). **Reuse the render-side path predicate** (`render._is_schemas_json`) rather than hand-rolling a second check — reconcile already imports from render (no cycle), keeping the reconcile gate and render dispatch on one definition. **Caveat**: this is REPLACE-safe ONLY for zero-user-content artifacts; frontmatter-less renders that DO carry user content (`.cursor/mcp.json` user-added servers, `.mdc` rule prose) need per-type MERGE, not REPLACE — a separate, more careful fix. Diagnostic: `git check-ignore` + "did `/hm:make --update` actually change this file?" — if a rendered artifact never updates on re-render, suspect the no-frontmatter KEEP. Cross-ref [[openai-codex-strict-output-schema-rules]].

## [wiki:gotcha] codex-exec-is-noninteractive-no-approval-flag | 2026-06-03
`codex exec` (the non-interactive subcommand the second-opinion recipe uses) does NOT accept `--ask-for-approval` — that's an **interactive-`codex`-only** flag. codex-cli 0.133.0 errors `unexpected argument '--ask-for-approval'` and exits non-zero on the FIRST recipe line. The valid exec flags (0.133.0): `--sandbox <mode>` (the isolation), `--ignore-user-config`, `--ignore-rules`, `--json`, `--output-schema <f>`, `--output-last-message <f>`, `--skip-git-repo-check`, `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` (DO NOT use — defeats the sandbox). The 0.28.7-era recipe shipped `--ask-for-approval never` (copied from interactive-codex docs) → every second-opinion call died at line 1. Fix (0.28.9, PLAN-codex-exec-ask-for-approval-flag-invalid): drop the line; `exec` is non-interactive so there's no approval prompt to suppress; `--sandbox read-only` is the real isolation. **Meta-pattern (the bigger lesson):** this was the THIRD distinct cause of the codex second opinion silently skipping in one week — (1) no `Bash` tool on the reviewer (0.28.6), (2) strict-mode-invalid `--output-schema` (0.28.7/0.28.8), (3) this invalid flag (0.28.9). All THREE were masked by the **warn-and-proceed** failure policy (`exit!=0 → skip, proceed Claude-only`) — a resilience feature that doubles as a bug-hider. When a feature is wrapped in warn-and-proceed, add a POSITIVE smoke/health check that the happy path actually runs, because failures are silent by design. Verify codex recipe changes with a real `codex exec` round-trip (this session's release smoke). Cross-ref [[subagent-tools-field-hard-gates-bash-permission]], [[openai-codex-strict-output-schema-rules]].

## [wiki:gotcha] llm-prose-invokes-python-module-the-wiring-is-the-bug | 2026-06-07
When a Python module is meant to be driven by a rendered slash-command's **prose** (the LLM reads the prose and runs the CLI), the module's unit tests passing tells you NOTHING about whether the real flow works — the defect lives in the **prose↔module seam**, which no unit test covers. PLAN-crossmodel-codex-gaps shipped `high_diff.classify` + `codex_adapter.adapt_codex_finding` green-tested, but a deeper REVIEW found the rendered `review.md.j2`/`second_opinion_codex.md.j2` prose (a) called `git diff --name-only` (NO `HEAD`) so it saw zero files post-execute (staged diff) → Side high-diff gate silently under-triggered; (b) piped filenames only (no `--added-lines`) so `classify` could never set `boundary` → the entire LLM-boundary path + its INTEGRATION accuracy test were unreachable; (c) only *named* `codex_adapter` as a prose "contract" with no actual invocation → the deterministic severity-map was still LLM-applied (theater). Fixes: `git diff … HEAD` + `--numstat`→`--added-lines`; added a `codex_adapter adapt` CLI the prose pipes the codex-output file through (reads file, not inlined arg — keeps untrusted content out of the shell AND makes determinism real). **Pattern:** for every "the stage LLM will run `python -m harness_maker.X`" recipe, write a test (or a manual grep-audit) that the rendered command's flags actually exercise the module's branches — especially git-diff base (`HEAD` vs working-tree), and any numeric/flag input a feature gate depends on. Isolated-module review grades the module; only a seam-focused pass catches dead wiring. Cross-ref [[codex-exec-is-noninteractive-no-approval-flag]] (same warn-and-proceed silent-skip family).

## [wiki:pattern] untrusted-content-to-cli-pass-as-argv-or-stdin-never-quoted-blob | 2026-06-07
A rendered bash recipe that feeds attacker-influenceable text (a codex skip-reason, a finding ref, a diff) to a harness CLI must NEVER inline it into a shell-quoted literal (`echo '{"k":"<cause>"}' | tool` — a `'` in `<cause>` breaks out → command injection; caught as P1 in PLAN-crossmodel-codex-gaps REVIEW). Two safe shapes: (1) **arg-based** — `tool emit --skip-reason "$reason"`, each value a separate argv element; the CLI (argparse) builds the JSON in Python, so quotes/`;`/`$()` are data. (2) **file/stdin** — write the payload to a `mktemp` file (or read an existing tool-written file like codex's `--output-last-message` sink) and `tool < "$f"`. Also: never use a fixed `/tmp/foo.json` sink (symlink TOCTOU) — `mktemp` always. The `codex_ledger emit` (arg-based) and `codex_adapter adapt` (stdin-from-file) CLIs were both added specifically to give the recipes an injection-safe path. Cross-ref the second_opinion prompt recipe's pre-existing `printf '%s' "$content" > "$prompt_tmp"; tool < "$prompt_tmp"` discipline.

## [wiki:architecture] worktree-create-guard-deliverable-exemption-and-landed-marker | 2026-06-12
PLAN-worktree-deliverable-blocks-create. Two fixes to `worktree.py`. **(1) Create-guard deliverable exemption (ADR-001):** `/hm:plan` writes `work-docs/{PLAN,RESEARCH,SPEC,REVIEW}-*.md` (+ `specs/SPEC-*.md`) that `/hm:execute` depends on, and deliverables are deliberately NOT in the churn set (wrapup commits them) — so they were ALWAYS uncommitted at `worktree create` time → every plan→execute self-blocked on the Layer-2 dirty-base guard. Fix: `_is_deliverable_path` (anchored full-match `^work-docs/(PLAN|RESEARCH|SPEC|REVIEW)-[^/]+\.md$|^specs/SPEC-[^/]+\.md$` — `[^/]+` not `.+` so nested dirs aren't over-forgiven) wired into `_is_create_guard_harness_artifact` **per-line** (coexisting code WIP still blocks). The finalize filter `_is_harness_artifact` is UNCHANGED so deliverables stay stash-PRESERVED — the create-guard/finalize two-filter asymmetry extended, never the finalize subset. Guard helpers switched to `git status --porcelain -uall` (a fully-untracked `work-docs/` collapses to ONE `?? work-docs/` line otherwise → the per-line filter never sees the file; bites the fresh-project first-plan). NON-GOAL: non-default `work_docs.dir` not covered (pure porcelain predicate, no harness.yaml access). **(2) SHA-validated landed-marker (ADR-003/004):** `_branch_content_in_head` compared *current* blob SHAs, so a landed branch whose files were later re-edited preserved forever → a growing `[WARN] preserved branch` wall (74 leaked `execute-*` branches). Fix: finalize writes git ref `refs/hm-landed/v1/<branch>`=branch-tip-after-capture (pre-cleanup, BOTH clean+dirty base — the stash-conditional `handed_off` block would miss clean base); `prune_stale` deletes a markered branch iff `current_tip == marker_SHA` (no content recompare; name-collision safe — a re-created same-named branch has a different tip → falls to the preserve-biased content-gate). Markers reaped same-op on every delete path + orphan-reap loop so refs can't accumulate. New `prune-branches [--force]` CLI drains the legacy backlog (force prints a `git log -p` recovery hint per branch). Warning wall collapsed to 1 summary line via `_print_prune_warnings`. **Dogfood caveat:** this repo gitignores all of `work-docs/`, so it does NOT itself reproduce the create-block; the unit tests reproduce it via a fixture that tracks `work-docs/` like a real downstream harness. Cross-ref [[worktree-keep-base-clean-churn-isolation]], [[fail-safe-direction-when-unifying-divergent-parsers]].

## [wiki:architecture] locale-and-command-observability-injection | 2026-06-17
PLAN-locale-and-command-observability. Two cross-cutting prompt features ride one mechanism. **(1) Locale directive:** new `agents/_partials/output_language.md.j2` (one shared partial, `{{ config.locale }}`-driven, zero new locale-variant files — the LLM translates at runtime) is `{% include %}`'d by the 4 command/skill **wrappers** (`atomic_command`, `workflow_command`, codex `stage_skill`/`workflow_skill`) AND placed under a `## Output Language` heading **outside** the `@hm:user:*` markers in the 4 CLAUDE.md templates + `codex/AGENTS.md.j2`. Outside-marker placement is load-bearing: `block_merge.merge()` (the `@hm:user:*` family path for CLAUDE.md user-modified + AGENTS.md MERGE_BLOCK) walks NEW verbatim, swapping only user-marker bodies from OLD → a new template-owned section PROPAGATES to existing installs while user content is preserved (verified by a merge-reach test, not just asserted). CLAUDE.md is `@hm:user:*` family, NOT the inverted `@hm:harness:*` (an Explore pass claimed inverted — verified false against `reconcile.py:296` hash-match→REPLACE + `_decide_user_modified`→MERGE_BLOCK; **always verify reconcile-family claims against source**). **(2) Banners:** `step_manifest.md.j2` reframed to a structured START banner (🎯/📋, kept its `.hm-loop-active` self-skip) at the wrapper layer (once per command); new `stage_end_summary.md.j2` END banner (✅/📁/➡️) lives in the 7 `stages/*.md.j2` (so a fused/concatenated workflow emits one per stage; the Codex `workflow_skill` delegates → carries zero). Hard enforcement = StrictUndefined REQUIRED `{% set summary_* %}` vars (a stage that forgets one fails render); locale = presence-audit only via `/hm:health` (`config.locale` always defaults to "en" → no UndefinedError leg) — honest asymmetry, ADR-002. **Gotchas that bit:** (a) wrapper-included partials render with `is_codex=False` even for codex files (the global render loop's FileSpec ctx lacks the key; stage BODIES get `is_codex=True`) → keep wrapper partials is_codex-agnostic. (b) `/hm:health` scope must EXCLUDE meta commands — `loop-p5-batch` (always installed, hyphenated) is swept in by the `"-" in stem` fused classifier and carries no banners → both signals false-fail on every install without a meta denylist (REVIEW P1). (c) the StrictUndefined optional-locale guard needs `is defined` AND truthiness combined — see [[jinja-is-defined-returns-true-for-none]]. Cross-ref [[prompt-template-shared-partials-dedup]], [[reconcile-keeps-frontmatterless-renders-forever]].

## [wiki:gotcha] codex-exec-allow-rule-needs-bare-command | 2026-06-17
A `settings.json` `permissions.allow` entry like `Bash(codex exec:*)` is a **command-prefix** match — Claude Code only pre-approves a Bash call whose command STARTS with `codex exec`. A compound one-liner (`content=…; prompt_tmp=$(mktemp); …; codex exec …`) does NOT prefix-match, so in a headless/autoloop run it is **denied at the permission layer** (separate from the sandbox) → the call skips → warn-and-proceed silently degrades to Claude-only. Verified live during PLAN-codex-second-opinion-sandbox review: compound `codex exec` Bash calls were denied; a bare `codex exec … < file` was allowed. `dangerouslyDisableSandbox:true` disables the *sandbox* (fs/network isolation) but does NOT bypass allow/deny, so it does not rescue a non-matching command. **Pattern:** any allow-gated CLI invocation a stage prompt issues must be rendered as its OWN bare command beginning with the allow-listed token; build inputs (temp files, prompts) in a SEPARATE prior step. Bonus: writing untrusted content (a diff) to the prompt file via the **Write tool** instead of a double-quoted `content="…"` shell assignment closes the same recipe's `$(...)`/backtick expansion vector (double quotes still expand → under a disabled sandbox that is host RCE) — one restructure fixes both. Cross-ref [[untrusted-content-to-cli-pass-as-argv-or-stdin-never-quoted-blob]], [[llm-prose-invokes-python-module-the-wiring-is-the-bug]].

## [wiki:pattern] drain-trigger-additive-relocation | 2026-06-20
PLAN-multisession-worktree-concurrency Phase 0 (ADR-009). `prune_stale` (the gated, biased-to-preserve worktree branch/marker/stash sweep) ran ONLY at `worktree create`, so when a project pauses the backlog grows unbounded — this repo had 76 leaked `execute-*` branches against 1 landed-marker, printing a "N branch(es) preserved" warning wall on every create (cry-wolf: the operator learns to ignore the very warnings meant to flag genuine unmerged work). Fix = relocate the trigger **additively, not by moving it**: new `_drain(base)` = thin `return prune_stale(base)` wrapper + `_drain_summary(report)` (one-line, non-interactive — deliberately NO "re-run with --force" nag, unlike `prune-branches`) + a `drain` subcommand wired into `/hm:wrapup` Step 7.6 (after post-commit-pop) and `/hm:health`. **The create-time `prune_stale` call in `_cli_create` is RETAINED** — the regression guard is a behavioral test (seed orphan marker + dangling worktree dir → `_cli_create` → assert reaped), NOT a source-grep (a source-grep can't witness firing; the test-reviewer rejected the grep version). The drain reuses the single `prune_stale` gate so it is structurally incapable of force-deleting the legacy backlog — only `prune-branches --force` (human-reviewed) does. General principle: when a janitor only runs on one rare trigger, ADD trigger points that delegate to the SAME gate; never duplicate the gate logic and never widen the destructive path. k-of-3 review (code+security+codex) all clean, Grade A.

## [wiki:tooling] snapshot-regen-path-stability-guard | 2026-06-20
The `[fail:test] snapshot-regen-inside-worktree` (count:7) trap says running `tests/snapshot/regenerate.py` inside a worktree embeds the worktree path into rendered output and diverges the `body_sha256` hashes. Cheap **positive guard** when you suspect you tripped it: after `finalize stage-only` merges to base, re-run `regenerate.py` **from the main repo root** and `git diff --stat tests/snapshot/` — an **empty diff** proves the worktree-regen output was byte-identical, i.e. no path contamination. Observed 2026-06-20: regen WAS first run inside the worktree, but the main-root re-run produced identical files → the command-body hashes do NOT embed the absolute pkg path for this change (only the 2 edited templates' command hashes moved; `harness.yaml`/`help`/`exec-rev` unchanged, which also bounds the blast radius). So the trap action does not always contaminate — but the diff-from-canonical-root check is the deterministic way to KNOW rather than assume. Cross-ref the pending `post-finalize-snapshot-regen-hook` proposal (the real fix is automation, not discipline).
