## [wiki:pattern] new-persisted-config-six-point-wiring | 2026-06-20
Adding a new typed config block to harness.yaml (did this for `autonomy:` — PLAN-human-bottleneck-auto-advance Phase 1) silently drops on re-render unless SIX points are wired in lockstep — the same shape `permissions` / `second_brain` / `codex_second_opinion` already follow: (1) the Pydantic model (`AutonomyConfig`, `ConfigDict(strict=True, extra="forbid")`); (2) `HarnessConfig.<field>` with `default_factory`; (3) `InterviewAnswers.<field>` MIRROR with the same default_factory (drift here = silent drop at synthesize); (4) `synthesize()` passing `<field>=answers.<field>` into the `HarnessConfig(...)` ctor; (5) BOTH `templates/harness-yaml/{Production,Side}.yaml.j2` emit the block; (6) `interview._parse_<field>` tolerant reverse-mapper wired into `answers_from_harness_yaml`'s update dict. Gotcha: a model with an enum-list field (`pipeline: list[AtomicStage]`) needs `model_validate(value, strict=False)` in the reverse-mapper so yaml stage strings coerce to the enum, while the `level` Literal + `gt=0` caps + `extra="forbid"` STILL reject genuinely bad input (→ caught → default fallback). Absent-case = the default_factory delivers the safe default (`gated`) for old yaml with no key. Verified by a round-trip test asserting ALL fields through synth→render→reload — the initial 2/5-field assertion passed review only after being widened to 5/5 (absent-case = feature black hole).

## [wiki:convention] multiphase-plan-wrapup-increment | 2026-06-20
A large PLAN delivered across several `/hm:execute` passes does NOT get `status: complete` at the first wrapup. wrapup Step 4's "status: complete + all checkboxes [x]" assumes one-wrapup-per-PLAN; for incremental delivery, set frontmatter `status: in-progress`, leave the (unmet) feature-level Success Criteria checkboxes unchecked, and record per-phase state in a PLAN `## 🚧 Execution Log` table. Marking complete when 1/9 phases shipped is a lie the commit history then has to walk back. The wrapup commit is the *phase increment* (subject scoped to the phase), not the whole feature.

## [wiki:pattern] fail-safe-session-marker | 2026-06-20
`.hm-autopilot` (PLAN-human-bottleneck-auto-advance Phase 2) is the template for any session-scoped state file that, if trusted blindly, could mis-activate a feature. Three layers: (1) **`load()` returns None on ANY failure** — absent / OSError / `json.JSONDecodeError` (covers empty-file partial-write survivors) / pydantic `ValidationError`; the caller treats None as the safe default. (2) **`active_marker()` adds a session-ownership gate** — the marker stores the `session_uuid` (reuse `worktree._current_session_uuid`, project-scoped today) and is honored only when it matches the current session, so a foreign/stale marker never activates. (3) **`effective_level()` clamps an unknown fallback** — even the harness.yaml default is validated against the known set and an unrecognized value → the safe default (not propagated). Schema discipline: `ConfigDict(strict=True, extra="forbid")` + `pipeline: Field(min_length=1)` (an empty list is a silent no-op, so reject it), and **`model_validate(data, strict=False)` at the JSON-load boundary** so on-disk stage strings coerce to `AtomicStage` while the Literal/regex/forbid constraints still reject — the SAME strict-model/lenient-load idiom that `interview._parse_autonomy` needed in Phase 1 (strict=True rejects str→enum; confirmed cross-cutting, do NOT drop the strict=False).
## [wiki:architecture] feature-branch-land-idempotency | 2026-06-20
PLAN-multisession-worktree-concurrency Phases 3–4 (ADR-003/007). The new `task_land` (squash `hm/<slug>`→base + teardown, all under the merge fence) must be idempotent across EVERY crash point because a wrapup/land can die mid-way. Two non-obvious lessons the k-of-3 caught: **(1) the squash-aware "already landed" signal must be the landed-marker (`_read_landed_marker == _branch_tip`), NOT `_branch_content_in_head` alone.** Content-in-head compares the branch's changed blobs against HEAD; once a LATER land (or user commit) advances base HEAD and touches one of those files, content-in-head flips to False → a re-run would re-squash and double-commit. The marker stores the branch TIP sha, which is independent of base HEAD movement, so `marker==tip` stays true through base advances (content-in-head is only the fallback when no marker was written). **(2) conflict cleanup must be scoped to the squash's OWN path set** (`git diff merge-base..branch`), never a blanket `git reset --hard` / `git clean` — the merge fence serializes harness LANDS but does NOT lock external editors, so a concurrent editor's unrelated work (or a forgiven untracked deliverable like `work-docs/PLAN-*.md`) would be destroyed. Restore per-path (`git checkout -f HEAD -- <p>` individually — batch checkout fails atomically on a branch-added path) and unlink only `(_untracked_files - pre_snapshot) & touched`. Also: teardown steps must each be individually idempotent (`if wt.is_dir()` guard, suppressed-but-rechecked branch delete that returns rc=1 on failure rather than reporting a non-converging orphan as success), and a registry-row drop must be uuid/pid-aware (ADR-004 never deletes a live mismatched row). Irreversible core → 2-round review found 2 P0 a single pass would have shipped.

## [wiki:pattern] never-auto-guard-marker-gated-not-static-deny | 2026-06-20
PLAN-human-bottleneck-auto-advance Phase 4 (`autopilot_guard` PreToolUse hook + ADR-003 refinement). Enforcing a "never-auto" deny-list for an autonomous agent: do NOT render it as a static `settings.json` deny — that is session-wide and would block the user's OWN manual `git push`/`rm` even when autonomy is OFF (a footgun; the maintainer manually pushes every wrapup). The list exists *because auto-advance removes the human*, so gate it on exactly that runtime condition: a **PreToolUse hook that fires only while the `.hm-autopilot` marker is active** (reuse `autopilot.active_marker`). autopilot OFF → pure no-op; ON → block. Three security lessons the k-of-n (2 code + security + Codex) caught that a single pass would have shipped: **(1) the guard MUST resolve the project root worktree-aware** — `Path.cwd()` is the `.worktrees/<wt>/` dir during an autonomous execute while the marker lives at base root → the guard silently no-ops in the exact mode it guards. Mirror `worktree_gate`'s payload-first (`workspace.current_dir`/`cwd`/env) + walk-up + `.worktrees/`-parent resolution. **(2) git detection must be WORD-TOKENIZED, not adjacency-regex** — `\bgit\s+push\b` is bypassed by `git -c k=v push` / `git -C dir push`; tokenize (shlex), skip value-consuming opts (`-c`/`-C` + their value), check the first non-option as the subcommand. On a `shlex` ValueError (unclosed quote) fall back to coarse `str.split` (block-biased = SAFE direction), NOT `return None` (that's a false-NEGATIVE bypass). **(3) the protected surface must cover BOTH the Write tool AND Bash redirects** — block `.claude/settings*.json` + `.claude/hooks/hooks.json` (the file that registers this very guard — self-disable!) via a Write-path regex AND a filename-substring Bash regex (catches `echo>`, `tee`, `sed -i`, `python -c open()`). Marker freshness needs a TTL (reject age<0 AND age>TTL) because the session-uuid is project-scoped. General principle for any agent guard: gate on the autonomy marker, tokenize don't regex-adjacency, and bias false-positives toward block.

## [wiki:pattern] stop-hook-backstop-stop-hook-active-first | 2026-06-20
PLAN-human-bottleneck-auto-advance Phase 3 (`autopilot_guard --mode stop-hook`). A Stop-event hook that returns `decision:block`+exit 2 to keep a session alive (here: don't terminate mid-pipeline while `.hm-autopilot` is active) has ONE non-negotiable contract — **check `stop_hook_active` FIRST and return None (allow) when set**, exactly as `loop_gate` does. Omit it and exit-2 re-fires the Stop event forever (the harness re-invokes the hook, which blocks again → infinite loop). Two more lessons the P3 review surfaced: (1) the block `reason` must be **descriptive, not imperative** until the thing that fulfils it exists — pre-P6 there is no prompt-driven chainer, so "continue to the next stage" is a false imperative the agent cannot satisfy; "pipeline in progress — not terminating" states the fact without commanding an impossible action. (2) the `--mode` argparse dispatch must keep `default="pretooluse"` (NOT `required=True`) because the SAME module is wired into BOTH the PreToolUse entry (no `--mode` flag) and the Stop entry (`--mode stop-hook`) in hooks.json — a required flag would break the no-flag PreToolUse registration at runtime. General: a dual-event hook module shares one `main()`; gate behavior on the event/mode, default to the no-flag caller's mode, and mirror loop_gate's isatty guard (`"" if sys.stdin.isatty() else read()`) so a bare-TTY invocation never blocks on EOF.

## [wiki:gotcha] memory-retrieve-silent-empty-on-missing-close-marker | 2026-06-20
`memory_retrieve.parse_entries` returns `[]` (not an error) when `.claude/memory/{wiki,failures}.md` is missing its `<!-- @hm:/user:entries -->` close marker — `text.find(CLOSE)` → -1 → empty. Consequence is severe and SILENT: every entry in that file becomes invisible to retrieval (the `/hm:plan` memory surfacing, the CLI), so the only symptom is a deep entry never ranking — surfaced solely by the INTEGRATION-tier `test_cli_real_repo_memory_surfaces_recent_entry`. Root cause is always the same: a wrapup wiki/failures append that writes a new entry ON TOP OF the close-marker line (overwrite), deleting it — i.e. [[wrapup-eof-append-outside-marker]] in the other direction (the marker vanishes instead of content landing after it). Diagnosis recipe: `grep -c "@hm:/user:entries" <file>` (expect ≥1; 0 = corrupted) and `git log --oneline -- <file>` to find the commit where the count dropped to 0 (`git show <c>:<file> | grep -c`). Fix = restore the marker at EOF after the last entry; do NOT weaken the test — it is correctly catching real corruption. Hardening proposed (pending): parse_entries should emit a stderr warning naming the file on a missing close marker so the failure is loud, not a silent zero-result.

## [wiki:gotcha] validate-then-unrestricted-update-reopens-the-guard | 2026-06-20
PLAN-human-bottleneck-auto-advance P5 (`autopilot_ledger.append_event`). A membership guard followed by an UNRESTRICTED `dict.update(caller_fields)` re-opens the exact hole the guard closed. The code validated `event in EVENTS` (ADR-009: no `iter_receipts.Verdict` literal on disk), built `record = {"event": event, ...}`, then did `record.update(fields)` — so `append_event(event="advanced", fields={"event": "pass"})` overwrites the validated `event` with a forbidden literal AFTER the check. Both Claude reviewers missed it; the **Codex k-of-3 third voter caught it** (heterogeneous-voter value — a different model surfaces a different blind spot; do NOT treat Codex as a tiebreaker footnote). Fix: set authoritative keys LAST (`record = dict(fields); record["ts"]=...; record["event"]=event`) so a caller's reserved key can never win. General rule: any "validate a field, then merge a caller-supplied dict that can contain that field" is a validation-bypass; apply the validated keys AFTER the merge, not before. Companion hardening this unit: make an enum-disjointness invariant STRUCTURAL not test-only — derive the set from the type (`EVENTS = frozenset(get_args(LedgerEvent))`) + an import-time `assert EVENTS.isdisjoint(get_args(Verdict))`, so a future edit to either enum fails at import, not only in one test. See also [[never-auto-guard-marker-gated-not-static-deny]] (same "the guard must be reachable on the real runtime path" theme).

## [wiki:pattern] deterministic-side-effect-after-llm-gate | 2026-06-20
PLAN-human-bottleneck-auto-advance P6. When a deterministic CLI and an LLM-evaluated prose gate jointly decide an action, the CLI's irreversible SIDE EFFECT must happen AFTER the LLM gate clears, not before. P6's `autopilot_caps boundary` CLI appended the `advanced` ledger event on its proceed path, but the stage-template prose evaluated the mandatory gate (review CHANGES_REQUESTED / wrapup push / plan arch-Q) AFTER calling the CLI — so a gate-pending stage recorded a phantom advance, inflating the step count (caps fire early on STOPs, not advances) and double-counting on retry. The Codex + one Claude reviewer caught it; the other Claude reviewer wrongly judged it benign (only modeled the crash-retry case, not the common gate-stop case). Fix: reorder the PROSE so the LLM evaluates the mandatory gate FIRST (absent-case=STOP, no CLI call) and runs the side-effecting CLI ONLY when the gate is clear. General rule for prompt+CLI hybrids: the LLM judgment gate precedes the deterministic recorder; never let the recorder run on a path the LLM may still abort. Cross-ref [[validate-then-unrestricted-update-reopens-the-guard]] (same family: a guard/recorder placed on the wrong side of a decision).

## [wiki:gotcha] str-of-str-enum-is-repr-not-value | 2026-06-20
`str(AtomicStage.RESEARCH)` returns `"AtomicStage.RESEARCH"` (the Enum `__str__`), NOT `"research"` — even for `class AtomicStage(str, Enum)`. So `[str(s) for s in pipeline]` produces enum-repr strings, and a `"research" in [str(s) ...]` membership test FALSE-NEGATIVES every valid stage (P6 bug: every valid `--current` was misclassified as "unknown_stage"). But the member itself IS a str equal to its value: `AtomicStage.RESEARCH == "research"` is True, and `list.index` / `in` use `==`. So compare/look-up against the enum members DIRECTLY (`current in marker.pipeline`, `pipeline.index(current)`) — do NOT stringify. When you genuinely need the value string use `member.value`, never `str(member)`. Caught by a test that flipped GREEN→RED only after the (buggy) stringify was added.

## [wiki:pattern] enabled-predicate-must-match-the-clamp-fail-safe | 2026-06-20
PLAN-human-bottleneck-auto-advance P7 (`autopilot_ledger.smoke_check`). When ONE code path computes "is X enabled?" as a NEGATIVE predicate (`level != "gated"`) but the rest of the codebase clamps unknown/garbage values to the OFF state (`autopilot.effective_level`: any level ∉ {gated,auto_safe,full} → gated, the documented absent-case fail-safe), the two DIVERGE on every non-canonical value: a typo'd/empty `level` ("off", "") is `!= "gated"` so the negative predicate treats it as ENABLED while the clamp treats it as OFF. Here that produced a false "armed but never fired" /hm:health alarm for any garbage level. Fix: compute enablement with a POSITIVE allow-list that matches the clamp's known-good set (`level in {"auto_safe","full"}`), so unknown → not-enabled, consistent with the fail-safe. General rule: a "feature is on" check must use the SAME membership set the rest of the system clamps against — never `!= <the-one-off-value>`, which silently treats every unknown value as on. This is the [[fail:design]] "absent-case = feature black hole" meta-pattern (CLAUDE.md, count:8 most-recurring) in predicate form. Companion: give the consuming CLI `choices=` so a misspelled value errors loud instead of flowing into the wrong branch.

## [wiki:pattern] cross-base-safety-probe-must-cover-every-base-not-just-primary | 2026-06-20
PLAN-multisession-worktree-concurrency Phase 7 (`worktree.enablement_preflight`, the ADR-008 make-time flip gate). A safety probe that decides a one-shot config flip across a PRIMARY + N sibling repos must apply the SAME blocker set to EVERY base — a primary-only check is a strand gap. Here `enablement_preflight` swept primary+siblings for filesystem residue (`_old_model_residue_blockers`) but ran the git-status dirt/indeterminate probe on the PRIMARY ONLY, so a dirty/indeterminate sibling could silently flip while the primary looked clean. Fix: extract the probe into `_git_dirt_blocker(base, label)` (keep a per-base `(base/'.git').exists()` guard so non-git bases → `[]`) and loop it over `[(target, ""), *[(sib, f" [sibling {sib.name}]")]]` alongside the residue sweep — primary `label=""` keeps messages byte-identical, siblings get attributed. General rule: when a guard iterates bases, EVERY safety signal must iterate the same base list; a signal applied to one base is a parity gap that only bites multi-repo. Trade-off accepted: N siblings → N defer-on-flake surfaces, but defer>false-clean for a one-shot flip. Closes a gap in an EXISTING ADR's stated contract → no new ADR (same shape as faithfully-implementing-a-written-ADR, not a new decision).

## [wiki:gotcha] stage-only-finalize-must-not-write-a-landed-marker | 2026-06-20
Discovered (not fixed — deferred) during PLAN-multisession-worktree-concurrency Phase 7. `_cli_finalize` writes the `refs/hm-landed/v1/<branch>` landed marker on `wt_rc==0` for BOTH `success` AND `stage-only`. But `stage-only` stages work into the base INDEX without committing to HEAD (wrapup commits later), so the marker records the branch tip while its content is NOT in HEAD → `prune_stale`'s marker-SHA-match path deletes the recovery branch unconditionally, destroying the `wip(execute)` recovery net before wrapup lands it (biased-to-preserve invariant violation; cross-session strand). Correct shape: gate the marker on `auto_commit` (success only). BUT a clean fix BREAKS `test_worktree_landed_marker.py::test_finalize_writes_landed_marker_at_branch_tip`, which deliberately PINS stage-only-writes-marker as the "SOLE producer" — i.e. the pinned test encodes the buggy behavior. Resolving the contradiction is a data-loss-core design decision spanning PLAN-p6-p7-worktree-finalize + PLAN-worktree-deliverable-blocks-create → NOT a loop-mode tacked-on fix (count:3 contamination history). Lesson: a landed marker means "content is in HEAD"; never write it on a stage-that-doesn't-commit. The follow-up plan's FIRST question must be "does the pinned test assert the data-loss behavior?" before deciding the fix shape. See [[cross-base-safety-probe-must-cover-every-base-not-just-primary]].

## [wiki:gotcha] timestamp-resolution-must-match-across-producer-and-consumer | 2026-06-21
PLAN-human-bottleneck-auto-advance P8. A producer and consumer that compare timestamps MUST emit the SAME resolution, or a same-instant compare flips. The autopilot marker's `created_at` was microsecond-resolution (`datetime.now(UTC).isoformat()`) but the ledger ts was second-truncated (`strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")`). `count_events` filtered `ledger_ts >= marker.created_at` to scope steps to the session; a same-second `advanced` event truncated to `.000` sorted BEFORE the marker's `.123456` → dropped → step under-count → **the step cap never fired** (a silently-disarmed safety rail). Fix: align the producer to the consumer's resolution (`_utc_now_iso` → `isoformat()`). Rule: when two timestamps are compared, pin them to one format/resolution at a single source; truncating one side is a latent same-instant bug. (Parsing both to datetimes — which we already did — fixes SHAPE mismatch but NOT resolution truncation.)

## [wiki:pattern] e2e-acceptance-catches-what-same-input-units-mask | 2026-06-21
PLAN-human-bottleneck-auto-advance P8. The P5/P6/P7 unit tests for the step cap all PASSED while the cap was actually broken in the real flow — because they fed the SAME explicit `now`/ts to both the marker and the ledger event, so the producer/consumer resolution mismatch ([[timestamp-resolution-must-match-across-producer-and-consumer]]) was masked. The P8 mechanical e2e — which arms a real marker (live `isoformat`) and drives the boundary CLI (live `_utc_now_iso`) through a full pipeline — exercised the REAL two-source path and immediately went RED. Lesson: a unit test that injects the same fixture into both sides of a cross-component contract cannot catch a mismatch between the components' real defaults; an acceptance/e2e test that uses each component's PRODUCTION value is what surfaces it. For any "producer writes X, consumer reads X" contract, add one e2e that lets BOTH sides use their real defaults.

## [wiki:pattern] squash-to-shared-base-must-scope-the-commit-to-its-own-paths | 2026-06-21
PLAN-multisession-worktree-concurrency, `worktree.task_land`. `git merge --squash <branch>` + `git commit -m msg` (NO pathspec) commits the WHOLE base index, not just the branch's diff. On a SHARED base where a concurrent session has `git add`ed unrelated work, that staged churn is swept into this task's squash commit — silent cross-session contamination (the count:3 class). Two compounding traps: (1) the dirty-base guard `_has_user_dirty_state` EXCLUDES `.claude/` + deliverables, so it does NOT abort when the concurrent staged file is harness/work-docs churn; (2) `git diff --name-only` (used to compute the squash's path set) HONORS a user's `diff.renames=true` and then reports a rename as ONLY the destination → a `commit -- <new>` misses the staged deletion of the old path. Fix: scope the commit to the squash's OWN path set (`git commit -m msg -- *touched`) AND compute that set with `--no-renames` (rename = old-delete + new-add, two entries). Compute the path set AFTER capturing pending worktree work (so captured files are included), and on an EMPTY path set (merge-base failure / unrelated histories) abort rc1-preserve rather than `merge --squash` content you can't scope — NEVER `git reset` a shared base index. General rule: any squash/commit onto a base another process may have staged into MUST be path-scoped + rename-proof; a bare `git commit` after `merge --squash` is a cross-session footgun. Empirically confirmed + regression-tested. See [[cross-base-safety-probe-must-cover-every-base-not-just-primary]].

## [wiki:pattern] gate-the-one-way-door-before-entering-its-stage | 2026-06-21
REVIEW-human-bottleneck-auto-advance-fullreview P1-1. The autopilot auto-advance "always stop at wrapup merge/push" guarantee was placed in `wrapup`'s stage-terminal gate (`stage_end_summary` runs LAST) — but wrapup's irreversible Step 7.7 squash-land runs in the stage BODY, BEFORE the terminal. So auto-advancing INTO wrapup performed the land, then hit the gate too late. A mandatory gate that protects an irreversible op must fire at the boundary the chain crosses to REACH that op, not at the terminal of the stage that performs it. Fix: `autopilot_caps._HUMAN_GATED_STAGES={wrapup}` — the boundary CLI refuses to auto-ENTER a human-gated stage (`halt_kind=merge_gate`, records `gate_blocked`, clears the marker so the Stop-hook stands down), so the human invokes `/hm:wrapup` deliberately. General rule: locate the gate at the LAST reversible decision point before the one-way door, never inside the door. Note the never-auto guard only blocks `git push`/`reset --hard`/`stash drop` — a local `git merge`/branch-delete (task-land) is NOT covered, so the prompt-layer gate is the only thing standing between auto-advance and the local main mutation.

## [wiki:gotcha] snapshot-regen-must-render-each-fixture-in-process-isolation | 2026-06-21
After a template change, `tests/snapshot/regenerate.py` produced a DIFFERENT `body_sha256` for fused-command files on every run (0df9f98f → 007faede → a65b9dc8), so the regenerated snapshot never matched the test. Root cause: regenerate.py renders all 8 fixture×mode combos in ONE process; some cross-fixture state leaks into the fused-command body. The actual render is deterministic — proven stable across ~8 SEPARATE `uv run` processes (incl. PYTHONHASHSEED=0, repo-internal target, multi-fixture-in-one-process WITHOUT regen's HOME-pin). The test passes because it renders each combo into a FRESH tmpdir; CI runs the test, not regenerate.py. Workaround when regen disagrees with the test: generate snapshots via the test's exact path (fresh tmpdir per combo, conftest-style `_compute_install_ref` pin) rather than trusting regenerate.py. Follow-up: regenerate.py should render each combo in process isolation (subprocess) to match the test. Diagnosis recipe: run the failing snapshot computation in 2 separate processes — if STABLE across processes but ≠ regen output, the multi-fixture-in-one-process leak is the culprit, not the render.

## [wiki:architecture] loop-marker-session-scoping-via-content-header | 2026-06-21
To make `/hm:loop` parallel-safe across concurrent Claude sessions without breaking the worktree-registry filename contract, the Claude `session_id` is recorded in the per-session marker's CONTENT header (`claude_session_id:` line) while the FILENAME stays worktree-keyed — so `_owned_session_uuids` (reads the filename suffix) is untouched. Three load-bearing design locks: (1) **only `loop.md.j2` passes `--claude-session-id`** to `worktree create`; a standalone `/hm:execute` create leaves the header empty, so its marker is NOT matched by the Stop-hook — that empty-vs-set header is the ONLY thing distinguishing "a loop" from "just a worktree" (both write a `.hm-loop-*` marker). (2) The session_id reaches the slash-command's own Bash via a SessionStart hook (`sessionid_envfile`) writing a sanitized `HM_SESSION_ID` to `$CLAUDE_ENV_FILE` — slash-command Bash cannot read its own Claude session_id any other way. (3) The session-blind legacy global `.hm-loop-active` is honored (by Stop-hook AND `loop-mode-active`) ONLY when the caller has NO id of its own (`not sid`) — a valid-id session relies on content-match alone, so a peer's degraded global can neither block it nor force it into loop-mode. ALL FOUR marker-content readers (`_read_active_worktrees` ×2, `_marker_referenced_paths`, `_session_worktrees`) must drop the header by the explicit `startswith("/")` path rule via the shared `loop_marker.parse_marker_paths` — see [[new-marker-content-field-must-update-every-reader]].

## [wiki:pattern] stale-o-excl-lock-reap-via-atomic-rename | 2026-06-21
A SIGKILL'd holder of the O_EXCL fallback fence lock (`_excl_lock`, the WSL2/NTFS-no-flock path) must be reaped WITHOUT a compare-then-`unlink()` BY PATHNAME — that removes a live successor's lock if a peer reaper deleted the dead one and a successor recreated it in the read→unlink window (REVIEW k-of-3 P1: concurrency + Codex agreed; code-reviewer wrongly passed it "clean"). Correct primitive (`_reap_stale_file`): the lock body is `nonce\npid\ntimestamp`; reap ONLY a dead-pid holder (or an unparseable/legacy body aged past `_EXCL_STALE_AGE`=720s — never a live pid by age, since the fence acquire-timeout bounds WAITING not HOLD time), and claim the stale file via atomic `os.replace` to a uniquely-named private quarantine. Then exactly one reaper wins (losers get FileNotFoundError) and the reaper only ever unlinks ITS quarantine name, never the live lock at the original path (immediately free for a successor's O_EXCL create). Body-mismatch (successor recreated in the decision→rename window) → restore via fail-safe `os.link` (FileExistsError ⇒ never clobber a yet-newer holder). A residual 4-party sub-ms race remains but is strictly narrower than the original and self-heals (live successors are never age-reaped). COMPANION invariant: an exclusivity CLAIM (`claim_task_branch`) must NOT ride the registry's best-effort unfenced fallback — `_registry_mutate(strict=True)` fails closed (`SharedSlugError`) under a wedged lock, else two concurrent claims both pass the foreign-live check and silently SHARE the worktree. General: never reap an advisory lock you don't own by pathname (rename-to-quarantine + identity-verify), and never let an atomicity guarantee silently degrade to unfenced for an exclusivity claim. Also: a body-write failure/short-write must be treated as acquisition failure (drop the half-written lock), else `_excl_release` can't identify it by nonce and it wedges the fence until the 720s age path.

## [wiki:pattern] worktree-inflight-protection-via-pre-create-reservation | 2026-06-21
PLAN-worktree-prune-create-race. Create-time `prune_stale` (lock-free, runs at every `worktree create` BEFORE the session's own create) could `shutil.rmtree` a PEER's in-flight worktree, AND a concurrent `git worktree prune` could crash a peer's `git worktree add` by removing its half-written `.git/worktrees/<name>/gitdir`. Lock-free fix (the user rejected lock-serialization): `create()` writes a `.claude/.hm-creating-<name>` RESERVATION (mtime = create-START, written ONCE) BEFORE the primary `git worktree add`, in a try/finally spanning add→marker that unlinks it. The reservation mtime is the reliable freshness signal — NOT the leaf-dir mtime, which goes stale during a nested checkout (a directory's st_mtime only updates on DIRECT-child changes; nested writes don't bump it — Codex refuted a leaf-mtime age-guard at PLAN time). `_scan_dangling_worktrees` skips a dir with a fresh reservation OR no `.git` (`.git` is a non-worktree FILTER, written early in add — NOT in-flight protection on its own). `prune_stale` SKIPS its `git worktree prune` entirely while `_any_fresh_reservation` (a peer is mid-create) — this is what closes the prune-crashes-add facet; `--expire=300.seconds.ago` additionally spares a recent COMPLETE admin entry. THREE process discoveries worth remembering: (1) the Phase-4 concurrent test (RED) caught the prune-crashes-add facet that the rmtree-only design missed — more severe than rmtree (kills the peer's create). (2) REVIEW Codex (single-source) caught an IMMORTAL-MARKER deadlock in the ADR-003 strand fix — see [[fail:design preserve-fix-deadlocks-its-own-reaper]]. (3) Residual: the reservation can't protect a peer once another session already passed its `_any_fresh_reservation()==False` check; the half-written-gitdir instant is structurally unclosable without a lock → accepted P2 (impact = recoverable create-retry, not work loss), pinned by tests. Accepted limitations: `.git`-less partial dirs leak (operator path), multi-repo SIBLING bases get no reservation (deferred). General: to protect an in-flight resource from a lock-free janitor, have the producer write a RESERVATION the janitor respects (freshness = a single written-once mtime, never a mutating one), and gate the janitor's destructive sub-ops on "any producer active", not just per-item.

## [wiki:gotcha] owned-session-uuids-is-all-markers-not-per-session | 2026-06-21
`worktree._owned_session_uuids(base)` docstring reads "owned by THIS process" but the IMPLEMENTATION globs EVERY `.claude/.hm-loop-*` marker in base and returns ALL sessions' dirname-UUIDs — it is NOT per-session. `post-commit-pop` (`worktree.py:3202`) globs ALL `.hm-finalize-stash-*` refs and uses that all-markers set as `HM_OWNED_SESSION_UUIDS`, skipping a ref only when its `session_uuid ∉` the set (`:3259`) — so a session's post-commit-pop will RESTORE A PEER'S deferred stash. The `_cli_post_commit_pop` comment (`:3244-3254`) admits this "preserves prior (vulnerable) behavior" — Layer 3 (UUID ownership) is shipped-but-unhardened; the real fix is wiring per-session ownership via an explicit `--owned-uuid`/`HM_SESSION_UUID` arg. CONSEQUENCE: the create-time queue-guard's foreign-stash counting (`_count_pending_stashes`) is LOAD-BEARING — it is the gate that keeps a session out of that contamination path. Do NOT make the queue-guard per-session (PLAN-fleet-10-20-parallel-safety C3 tried this and was reverted, see [[fail:design friction-looking-guard-was-load-bearing-safety]]). General: a "owned"/"mine" helper whose body reads shared filesystem state is per-PROJECT, not per-session — verify the implementation, never the name.

## [wiki:architecture] loop-stop-payload-cwd-is-project-root | 2026-06-21
PLAN-fleet-10-20-parallel-safety Phase-0 spike (`tests/fixtures/stop_payload_wsl2.json`). A Claude Code Stop-hook payload's `cwd`/`workspace.current_dir` is the directory where `claude` was LAUNCHED (the project root), unaffected by per-command subshell `cd`; there is NO payload field identifying which `.worktrees/<name>/` the session worked in, and `transcript_path` is not reachable from command-Bash. CONSEQUENCE for `/hm:loop` on WSL2: when the SessionStart env-file fails so `HM_SESSION_ID` is empty, the loop marker gets an empty `claude_session_id` header; the Stop-hook (which always has the real id from stdin) cannot content-match it, so the loop SELF-STOPS after one iteration (it neither kills nor is killed by peers — that needs an id-less *stdin* = Cursor/Codex). A Stop-hook "self-heal" that stamps the real id into a degraded marker is UNBUILDABLE: with cwd=root and no worktree field, it cannot prove which empty-header marker is the stopping session's (a count-based guess would stamp a peer = never-block-peer violation). The only sound C1 fix is the LOUD FLOOR — `loop.md.j2` (CLAUDECODE-branched loop-start warning) + `readiness.sessionid_envfile_live` accurately describe the self-stop symptom + remedy. Triangulate runtime payload facts from docs + the existing test convention (`_stop_payload` uses `cwd=base`) + architecture — don't assume (Fix-4 lesson).

## [wiki:architecture] layer3-per-session-ownership-via-slug-crumb | 2026-06-21
PLAN-layer3-per-session-ownership. `post-commit-pop` decides which deferred finalize-stashes to restore by `HM_OWNED_SESSION_UUIDS`. The old source `owned-uuids` (`_owned_session_uuids`) read ALL sessions' `.hm-loop-*` markers → restored peers' stashes (the count:3 contamination). New per-session source: a **slug-keyed crumb** `.claude/.hm-owned-uuids-<slug>` — execute's finalize records its worktree uuid via `owned-crumb-add "$(pwd)" <slug> "$(wt-uuid <WT>)"`, wrapup reads it by its own `<slug>` arg (`owned-crumb-read`, machine-derived → works on a standalone/recovered wrapup) then clears it on pop-success (`&& owned-crumb-clear`). The `post-commit-pop:3266` guard dropped the `owned_uuids and` short-circuit so an **empty owned-set fail-safe-SKIPs** a `session_uuid`-bearing ref (never the old marker-present pop); legacy no-uuid refs keep the marker accept (writer derives uuid from the dirname → bounded to pre-upgrade — proven by test). `owned-uuids` is loud-deprecated; a render-grep gate (`test_no_envless_post_commit_pop_in_any_producer`) fails if ANY rendered producer (commands OR skills) invokes `post-commit-pop` without sourcing `HM_OWNED_SESSION_UUIDS`. **Producer-gated** safety → an un-re-rendered user harness keeps the old behavior until `/harness-maker:make --update`. **KNOWN RESIDUAL** (see [[per-session-key-that-unions-by-a-non-session-key]]): the crumb is slug-keyed not session-keyed and UNIONs, so two DIFFERENT sessions on the SAME slug can still peer-pop — bounded (flag-on SharedSlug blocks; flag-off single-session-per-slug discipline; no worse than pre-fix); a SharedSlug guard on the crumb path is the follow-up. C3 (per-session queue-guard) re-enablement is now unblocked for the distinct-slug case.

## [wiki:architecture] locked-cli-for-shared-markdown-memory | 2026-06-21
When multiple sessions/processes do read-modify-write on a shared markdown file (memory tiers, indexes), route every write through ONE flock-locked Python CLI that performs the whole RMW inside `exclusive_lock` — never let an LLM `Edit` the file directly (a whole-file Edit races peers and can silently drop a close marker). The lock anchor is a separate `.lock` sentinel, NEVER the target file: locking the target breaks under atomic `os.replace` because a waiter can lock the old (unlinked) or the fresh inode independently. Base-root the lock path (`.resolve()` + strip a trailing `.worktrees/<name>`) so a worktree-cwd writer and a base-cwd writer converge on the same lock (`resolve()` also canonicalizes symlinks, so they cannot diverge). Beyond the obvious marker-string guard, also reject body lines shaped like an entry heading (`## [tier:slug] …`) — a phantom heading corrupts the boundary parser on the next upsert; and fail-closed (do NOT auto-create) when a file has real content but lost both markers (else you split it into unmanaged + managed halves). Prove serialization with a SUBPROCESS test, not threads (flock is process-scoped; a thread test passes even when the lock is broken). Caveat: advisory flock only closes the hazard for PARTICIPATING writers — a stale-rendered template or a manual human Edit reopens it. See PLAN-multisession-fleet-reverify.
## [wiki:architecture] spec-tetrad-completeness | 2026-06-22
PLAN-spec-tetrad-completeness (0.32-dev): added the ORACLE axis to the spec plugin so consuming projects get LLM-era spec/oracle/test/mutation completeness. `SPEC.machine.yaml` schema_version 2 adds `oracle_source` (golden|differential|property|rubric|consensus|legacy-unspecified) + `oracle_evidence` + `oracle_independence_waiver` on every AC, plus a 4th AC `type: property` with the STRUCTURED metamorphic triple (input_domain/transformation/expected_relation/+preconditions/observable_output) — free text is not gateable. `spec_quality` scores a new `oracle_independence` dim on the EVIDENCE quality, NOT the declared enum label (anti-gaming): waiver=100 but ONLY in task-driven (spec-driven blocks regardless — thread dev_mode into the scorer), legacy=0, empty=20, <15char=40, has-specificity-marker=85, else=60. `spec_mutation` gained an audited equivalent-mutant classifier: unknown survivors default to `pending-review` (STAY in denominator — coverage can't be inflated by relabeling), only rule-id'd `equivalent` (typing-cast-string-noop, int-default-near-time) leaves the denominator, with excluded-count surfaced + `detect_exclusion_growth` guard. Matchers key on the MUTATED `source_line`, not the source+context blob (a coincidental cast()/`.get()` in nearby context must not exclude a real survivor). `spec_drift` surfaces `missing_oracle_source` as an advisory (not block) via `to_dict()`. KEY MIGRATION INVARIANT (ADR-006): `SpecMachine.schema_version` field default is the LITERAL `1`, NOT the bumped `SCHEMA_VERSION=2` constant — an omitted version pins to v1 so legacy specs aren't silently promoted and force-failed on the new mandatory oracle_source; `validate()` gates oracle/property requirements on `schema_version >= 2`; new authoring requires explicit `schema_version: 2`. x-contract correspondence (oracle_source ↔ conformance.kind, consensus ↔ adversarial 3-skeptic) is DOCUMENTED-only, not a validated isomorphism (ADR-005). Templates spec.md.j2 (Category 2.1.5 Oracle elicitation) + execute.md.j2 (Hypothesis property-test authoring + ci/dev profile contract) drive the LLM side; snapshots regen path-pinned so worktree-safe.
## [wiki:architecture] wrapup-waiver-enforcement | 2026-06-22
PLAN-wrapup-waiver-enforcement: wired the task-driven oracle-waiver advisory into /hm:wrapup. The per-AC evidence ladder is now a SINGLE pure function `spec_machine.score_ac_oracle_evidence(ac)->int` (legacy→0/empty→20/<15char→40/specificity-marker→85/else→60, NO waiver/mode/load); `spec_quality._score_oracle_independence` imports it (the waiver +100 lift + mode logic stays in the caller; legacy-unspecified is a denominator-RETAINED 0, never a skipped AC). A static dup-guard test asserts the markers/threshold live in exactly ONE module so the producer-consumer drift can't silently re-appear. New `spec_machine waiver-check --yaml --dev-mode --root` CLI emits a TRI-STATE status: `ok` (no AC needs a waiver / spec-driven no-op) | `flagged` (≥1 task-driven AC with weak evidence + no `oracle_independence_waiver`) | `check_error` (the yaml could not be read/parsed — explicitly NOT a clean pass; the false-clean-pass the tri-state exists to prevent). It ALWAYS exits 0 (advisory, never a STOP — ADR-002 consciously softens the prior 'block before close' to visibility-over-friction) and appends a root-anchored atomic JSONL receipt (`.claude/observability/oracle-waiver-check-{slug}.jsonl`, gitignored, slug `_safe_slug`-sanitized, `is_relative_to` guard, 4096-byte truncation). wrapup.md.j2 renders Step 3.6 ONLY on the `{% if config.dev_mode == 'task-driven' %}` branch; a MANDATORY `readiness.py` `wrapup_oracle_waiver_dev_mode_match` signal catches the flip-without-re-render silent drift (mirrors sessionid_envfile_registered). The auditable surface is /hm:health, not the gitignored receipt (100%-local-telemetry).
## [wiki:architecture] nonmechanical-ac-binding | 2026-06-22
PLAN-nonmechanical-ac-binding extends [[spec-test-forward-binding]] (0.28.0) to `property` + `parametric` AC types (`judgment` deferred — no deterministic pytest node). `mark_tested` was ALREADY type-agnostic; the gap was workflow + enforcement, not mechanism. Added `select_pytest_bindable` (judgment-excluded = "has a deterministic pytest node"), `load_golden_table`+`GoldenTableError` (parametric golden_table SSOT; test resolves the yaml relative to `__file__` NOT cwd; data-loading-ONLY — the author writes the oracle body, `f(**input)==expected` is just the default example), execute Phase A (d) parametric authoring, and wrapup write-back extended to property+parametric + the per-type report-flip. **Enforcement (ADR-005 supersedes the originally-planned /hm:verify gate):** the verify surface was DEAD — `spec_drift.scan` is dev_mode-gated to spec-driven (a task-driven Production harness — incl. harness-maker's OWN config — gets an empty report → gate never fires) AND keyed on recorded `test_ids` which a missed write-back never has (`mark_tested` writes `pending_test→false` + `test_ids` atomically, so a miss leaves `pending_test=true` + `test_ids=[]`). Moved to wrapup `find-unbound` (dev_mode-agnostic; scans convention-name (`test_<ac-id>`) ∪ recorded `test_ids` via `pytest --collect-only`; Production fail-closed). KEY LESSON: any enforcement keyed on *recorded* `test_ids` is structurally blind to the primary miss — discover by convention OR collect, never by recorded id alone.
## [wiki:architecture] judgment-ac-binding | 2026-06-23
PLAN-judgment-ac-binding closes the 4th and last AC type — `judgment` (LLM-rubric-evaluated) — completing the spec-machine forward-binding loop ([[spec-test-forward-binding]] + nonmechanical). judgment has NO deterministic pytest node, so binding works differently: a judgment AC is "bound" iff a recorded `pass` verdict whose stored `judgment_subject_hash` STILL matches the recomputed canonical hash (a stale pass = unbound). The verdict comes from an **independent** read-only `judgment-reviewer` agent (ADR-006) — NOT the builder; a self-graded verdict is verification theater (the plan-validator's lead critical). `mark_judged` is pure Python storage (NO `anthropic` call — no-network contract; a static grep test guards it); the LLM judgment lives in the agent. `find_unjudged` is the Production gate: an in-scope judgment AC (subject path exists) that is not-current-pass/stale/unhashable → STOP; a fully-absent subject = future-PLAN = skip; malformed yaml → fail-closed. `compute_subject_hash` builds a canonical manifest (sorted `(declared_relative_name, sha256(bytes))`, de-duped, names included so a rename changes the hash) over the subject, expanding dirs via `os.walk(followlinks=False)` and rejecting any file that resolves outside the repo root. New spec_machine surface: judgment fields, `select_judgment`, `compute_subject_hash`/`SubjectHashError`, `mark_judged`, `find_unjudged`, `stale_judgment_verdicts`, `mark-judged`/`find-unjudged` CLIs. A new `judgment-reviewer` agent (registered in `_ALL_AGENTS`/`PRESET_AGENT_MODELS`/`_COMMUNICATION_VARIANT`/`_CODEX_AGENT_META`). KNOWN deferred: the `/hm:health` stale-verdict DISPLAY wiring (the detector is built+tested and the gate already blocks stale; only the advisory readiness surface is a fast-follow).
## [wiki:architecture] judgment-stale-health-display | 2026-06-23
`/hm:health` surfaces stale judgment-AC verdicts via a `judgment_verdict_freshness` advisory signal in `readiness._dim_guardrails` (PLAN-judgment-stale-health-display). It wires the already-built `spec_machine.stale_judgment_verdicts` detector into the health dashboard WITHOUT gating — the signal is `weight=0` AND `hard_gate=False` (both pinned explicitly + asserted by test, not left to the `_signal` default), so it appears in the Structural `signals_failed` list but contributes 0 to the dimension score and the composite, and therefore never affects `/hm:verify` Check 3 (ADR-001 — the `find-unjudged` Production wrapup gate is the real enforcement; docking the score too would double-penalize). It follows the directly-wired `wrapup_oracle_waiver_dev_mode_match` precedent (a spec-machine-adjacent advisory in the same dimension). Absent-case = N-A (no `specs/`, no `SPEC-*.machine.yaml`, or zero judgment ACs → emit NO signal), but a malformed machine SPEC is fail-LOUD — a failed signal naming the spec, NOT N-A (ADR-002: present-but-unreadable = freshness UNKNOWN, unlike the waiver's genuinely-absent render). The detector internally catches `SubjectHashError` and returns the AC id as STALE, so only a `load()`/parse failure escapes it; the catch is therefore narrowed to `(OSError, yaml.YAMLError, ValidationError, ValueError)` so a genuine internal bug propagates loud instead of being mislabeled as the user's "malformed SPEC". The displayed "N fresh" count reuses the detector's `_judgment_in_scope` predicate (a pass whose subject is fully absent is out-of-scope, neither fresh nor stale, and must not inflate the tally).
## [wiki:architecture] spec-requirement-gate | 2026-06-23
spec-requirement-gate (PLAN-spec-requirement-gate, 9 ADRs) closes the absent-case footgun where `dev_mode=spec-driven` was a declared intent the system did NOT enforce at the work boundary. In spec-driven mode, `/hm:plan` adds a render-gated "Step 1.7" that LLM-judges whether the work needs a machine SPEC operation (`add|change|delete|none|not-evaluated`) — the path **pre-filter** (`spec_need.prefilter` over `paths_to_mutate ∪ judgment_subject_paths`) is a HINT, the verdict runs regardless, and empty/uncertain/degraded ⇒ **`not-evaluated` (fail-closed)**, never `none` (ADR-002). On a forcing verdict it routes the user into `/hm:spec` via a **durable one-shot resume-marker** `.claude/.hm-spec-need-{slug}` (ADR-009): `/hm:spec` is terminal, so re-entry is a marker (not a function-return) — plan writes it, spec authors+STOPs, the user re-runs `/hm:plan`, the marker skips re-detection + asserts `operation_satisfied` + surface-never-re-invoke (the anti-infinite-loop guard); a stale marker (hash mismatch) re-detects. The escape is a **content-hash-bound, diff-expiring, reasoned waiver** (ADR-001 — `compute_subject_hash` over changed files; a stale/reasonless/frontmatter-only waiver is rejected). A render-gated **verify Check 6** is the backstop (FAIL iff verdict∈{add,change,delete,not-evaluated} ∧ ¬operation ∧ ¬valid-waiver; clean N-A on absent PLAN frontmatter; "5/6"-count branched). All spec_need state lives at `--root <WT>` (the worktree, where /hm:plan+/hm:verify run). New `spec_need.py` module (prefilter/record/operation_satisfied/waiver/marker + `_validate_slug` path-traversal guard); Python=rails, LLM=verdict (render-gated `{% if config.dev_mode == 'spec-driven' %}`, task-driven byte-unchanged). The mechanism is enforce-at-plan (inline route) + verify Check 6 backstop; cross-target dispatch = Skill on Claude / instruct on Cursor/Codex.
## [wiki:pattern] mutmut-version-pin-intent-guard | 2026-06-24
A dependency version pin's upper bound must match its *documented intent*, not just its comment. `mutmut>=2.4,<4` carried a comment claiming it "blocks 3.x", but `<4` allows every 3.x release — only `uv.lock` (2.5.1) prevented breakage. When a tool's CLI contract is version-specific (mutmut 2.x has `--paths-to-mutate`, dropped in the 3.x rewrite), pin to the compatible major (`<3`) AND add a runtime guard so a PATH-bypass install (a global `mutmut` outside the dev-group) loud-skips instead of producing a spurious gate FAIL. The guard `_detect_unsupported_mutmut` runs `mutmut --version` (timeout + check=False), and ONLY on major>=3 returns a `_MUTMUT_UNSUPPORTED` sentinel so `main()` skips non-gating (exit 0). On ANY ambiguity (FileNotFoundError / TimeoutExpired / unparsable / non-zero exit / major<3) it returns None and falls through to the existing `mutmut run` path — never false-skipping a working 2.x, and keeping the genuinely-absent contract single-sourced at the existing run-FileNotFoundError handler. Anchor the version regex to the tool name (`mutmut[^\d]*(\d+)\.…`) so a stray dotted number in a preceding warning line can't be misread as the version.
## [wiki:architecture] autopilot-config-surface | 2026-06-25
Autopilot/autonomy is now surfaced in the `/harness-maker:make` interview (`_ask_autonomy`) instead of being a silently-defaulted, hand-edit-only block. Its caps are nullable — `autonomy.step_cap`/`time_cap_min: null` = unlimited (the `evaluate_boundary` cap check skips a `None` cap). The Pydantic field default deliberately STAYS bounded (20/300) so an absent/malformed autonomy block is never silently unbounded; only a fresh interview opts into `null`. `autonomy.autopilot_persistent: true` + a SessionStart hook (`harness_maker.hooks.autopilot_autoarm`) re-arm the `.hm-autopilot` marker each session so the 18h TTL never trips. The real safety boundary is the mandatory gates (plan interview / review CHANGES_REQUESTED / wrapup merge), which fire at every level regardless of caps — the caps are only a runaway backstop, and the autoarm hook is strictly fail-safe (any malformed yaml/write error → no-op, never blocks SessionStart). `next_stage`/pipeline must be duplicate-free for the boundedness invariant to hold (see [[cap-removal-unmasks-pipeline-loop]]).
## [wiki:architecture] render-finish-ux | 2026-06-28
The post-render git-disposition flow (`/harness-maker:make` + `/hm:make`) splits cleanly: a thin testable Python layer (`git_disposition.py` + `git-status`/`git-ignore-roots` CLI subcommands) OWNS git-state detection + idempotent gitignore, while the slash command OWNS the `AskUserQuestion` and the `git commit` (CLI never commits — git policy). Detection is inferred from live git state EVERY run, never persisted: `compute_git_status` reads the render manifest (`.claude/.harness-manifest.json`) minus churn (`worktree._HARNESS_GITIGNORE_PATTERNS`), restricted to the selected target roots from `harness.yaml.targets`, and derives `prior_decision ∈ {undecided, commit, ignore}` + `decision_needed` + `offer_stage` — so a re-render never re-nags (committed harness → offer staging of new files only; ignored harness → silent). All git probes use `subprocess` with `check=False` + returncode branching because `git check-ignore` returns rc=1 when a path is NOT ignored (reusing `worktree._run`, which is `check=True`, would crash the common case). `ignore_roots` re-verifies each root is `check-ignore`-matched after appending and raises loudly on a non-work-tree / unwritten `.gitignore` (an explicit user decision must not silently no-op). Render also gained a re-render-only `--dry-run` preview→confirm→apply (NOT a worktree — backup + reconcile cover overwrite safety and keep non-git installs working) and a severity-aware fresh-install health line (quiet-when-clean / loud-on-P0·P1).
## [wiki:convention] harness-maker-invocation-launcher | 2026-06-29
Every executable harness_maker invocation rendered into a user harness (slash-command `!` lines, `Bash("…")` calls, fenced ```bash blocks) MUST use the full self-contained launcher `uv run --with {{ harness_maker_src_path }} python -m harness_maker.<module>`. The two shorter forms BOTH fail on a consumer plugin-cache install: bare `python -m harness_maker` (the consumer has no `python` alias / no console script → `command not found`), and `uv run python -m harness_maker` WITHOUT `--with` (uv resolves the consumer's own venv, which has no `harness_maker`). The console form `harness-maker <subcmd>` fails the same way (no installed entrypoint). A two-test gate enforces this: `test_invocation_render_gate` renders the whole tree (claude-code + cursor `.mdc` + codex `.toml`) and trips when any executable context reintroduces a non-full-launcher form, and a source-grep test forbids bare `harness-maker <subcmd>` remediation strings inside `src/**/*.py`. Detection is count-based (a compound line carrying a full launcher AND a trailing bare invocation still trips) with fence + multi-line `Bash(` tracking.
## [wiki:architecture] wrapup-memory-base-seam | 2026-06-30
In the per-task feature-branch model `/hm:wrapup` runs inside `.worktrees/<slug>/`, but `memory_md._base_root` strips the `.worktrees/<name>` suffix so the human memory tiers (wiki/failures/session/<today>) are written to the BASE repo — outside the squash's path set, so `task-land` preserved-but-never-committed them (a silent non-commit, NOT an abort). Fix: a `commit-base-memory` worktree CLI that, after `task-land`, amends the base-written human tiers into the fresh squash commit. It is hardened three ways: (1) the check→add→amend runs under task_land's `index.lock-hm` merge fence and amends with `git commit --amend --only -- <tier pathspec>`, so a concurrent session's staged churn can never be swept in and a peer's HEAD can never be rewritten; (2) it anchors `--expect-head` on the squash SHA `task_land` now prints to stdout in-fence (fresh-squash path only), not a race-prone post-hoc `rev-parse`; (3) it folds untracked tier files too (today's session log is untracked-and-ignored on first write) but never newly-tracks anything outside the human-tier pathspec.
## [wiki:tooling] command-surface-registry | 2026-07-01
harness-maker now has a self-describing command surface. `command_registry.py` is the single source of truth mapping every `python -m harness_maker[.<module>]` entry point to its subcommands (plus a reverse index over collision verbs). A fail-open `misroute_guard` is wired into every subcommand-bearing module: it redirects ONLY cross-module misroutes (e.g. `autopilot_caps on` → `autopilot on`) and returns None for registry-unknown tokens, so it can never break a valid command. The lone Typer `autopilot` toggle was down-unified to dot-form `python -m harness_maker.autopilot on` (Typer kept as a thin alias sharing one `resolve_toggle_config`). Two CI gates keep it honest: T-C1 (every rendered invocation is registry-valid) and T-C2 (bidirectional registry↔source parity for subparser modules + per-module guard-wiring).
## [wiki:architecture] review-grade-criteria | 2026-07-01
`/hm:review` now surfaces unverified-severe findings without changing the grade letter (PLAN-review-grade-criteria). The grade table (A=P0:0,P1:0 …) and "only consensus-passed P0/P1 count" are UNCHANGED. New: the Grade Gate computes `unverified_severe` = any `manual-only` OR `weak-consensus` finding at P0/P1, and when true sets `human_review_needed=true` even at grade≥threshold. Path-differentiated halt (ADR-003): interactive/autopilot STOPs for human review; loop mode PROCEEDS — the flag is persisted only in the committed REVIEW-{slug}.md, with NO active loop-close reader (accepted limitation, follow-up). This closes the observed "Grade A while 5 real P1 manual-only findings exist" hole (see [fail-adjacent] loop-body-skipping-review-stage). Also hard-sealed consensus Step 4c in BOTH review.md.j2 and consensus-arbiter_body.md.j2: 4a keeps same-tier candidacy, so the old cross-tier "Middle of the scale" severity-resolution rows were unreachable and are removed; "No tier bridging" retained; arbiter Hard Rules + Out-of-Scope + user-extension comment reconciled to forbid cross-tier bridging. Enforced by a contract render test (`tests/unit/test_render_review_surfacing.py`) that asserts grade-table byte-invariance + no-unconditional-proceed + Gate 0 third-state + hard-seal — a producer-gate, since the grade/consensus are LLM-executed prose with no Python backstop. Follow-ups: (1) a real loop-close reader for `human_review_needed`; (2) reconcile `consensus-passed-by-scope` into review.md.j2 Grade Computation (arbiter defines it, the inline model does not).
## [wiki:architecture] cfr-churn-metrics | 2026-07-03
PLAN-cfr-churn-metrics shipped opt-in local-git delivery metrics (`delivery_metrics.enabled`, default OFF): CFR (rolling 28d change failure rate; releases = `tag_pattern` tags with a first-parent task-land fallback, remediation via revert-message grep + 72h fix-only respin, fix-only releases excluded from denominator, ≤1 failure/release) and post-merge churn (LOC rewritten within a 14d maturation window, cohort-blame survival at the boundary rev with `git blame -w -M -C`). Core module `delivery_metrics.py` splits a PURE classification layer (`classify_cfr_full`/`classify_churn` — the property-test oracle, no git) from a git-adapter layer, so metamorphic invariants (window-outside invariance, `0<=failed<=total`) are testable without subprocess fixtures. Cross-time ledger `.claude/observability/delivery-metrics.jsonl` (O_APPEND ≤4096B, LLM verdicts reused by `(commit_sha,release_ref,algo_version,config_hash)`) makes trends stable across runs. Surface: `/hm:metrics` (rendered ONLY when enabled — disabled = omitted from the command surface, `_delivery_metrics_enabled` gate in `synthesize._base_files`) runs a two-pass adjudication (candidates→adjudicate→compute; exit-3 fail-closed) then interprets the trend + diagnoses WHY a number moved (revert linkage, top churned files, related PLAN/REVIEW slugs) + proposes harness-lever fixes; `/hm:health` gets a 1-2 line narrative. Deliberate Non-Goals: NO readiness dimension, NO gate (Goodhart guard — the number is reflection input, never a target). Real dogfood smoke: CFR 12/51 (tag), churn 548/4497 LOC.
## [wiki:architecture] failure-recurrence-dedup | 2026-07-04
Failure-memory recurrence dedup is repaired end-to-end (PLAN-failure-memory-recurrence-dedup). Root cause: `memory_md._upsert` incremented a failure entry's `count` ONLY on exact-slug match, but wrapup invented a fresh slug each time with no read-back, so recurrences fragmented (edge_testfarm_os: 19/19 count:1, pending-proposals.md never created) and the count>=3 escalation never fired. Three cooperating fixes: (1) `_upsert` on a matched failure slug now PRESERVES the original body and APPENDS a dated occurrence bullet `- [today] <collapsed note>` + count++ (was: replace body) — a count:N entry carries N-1 dated evidence lines; count++ and append are atomic (empty note → MemoryBlockError). New `--occurrence-note` CLI flag; the collapse+guard helper `_collapse_note` re-checks OPEN/CLOSE markers AFTER whitespace-collapse (a newline-split marker reforms otherwise), and all three creation paths (marker-absent, no-match, match-append) share `_new_entry_body` so a seed can neither be evidence-empty nor heading-shaped. (2) wrapup Step 5.2 gained a numbered MUST search-before-write step: `memory_retrieve` over BOTH failures.md and wiki.md (the wiki anchor lets a design reversal be matched against the prior decision it flips), under-merge bias, reuse the exact existing slug on a confident match, and a discriminating receipt `dedup: searched K existing failures, N considered, M reused` (K>0 proves the search ran). (3) Step 5.3 escalation is now a numbered MUST + `escalation: K entries at count>=3, P proposals written`. Design oscillation (reverting a prior decision) qualifies as `[fail:design] <stable-family-slug>`. Non-goals (explicit): migration of existing fragmented entries (forward-only), pure git-churn oscillation mining, wiki-tier occurrence-log (wiki = current-truth, replace-on-match). The proven precedent driving the numbered-step+receipt shape is [[second-brain-promotion-pipeline]] (advisory instructions silent-skip ~100%).
## [wiki:tooling] memory-md-consolidate | 2026-07-04
`python -m harness_maker.memory_md consolidate [--file failures|wiki|both] [--dry-run] [--today YYYY-MM-DD]` merges exact-slug duplicate entries in the memory tiers under the per-file flock. Opt-in maintenance ONLY — never wrapup-auto, and the hot-path `upsert` KEEPS its `matches>1` raise (a doubled slug still crashes upsert until consolidate runs). Failures: merged `count` = SUM of the dups' counts; the earliest-dated entry's body is canonical; later bodies fold to dated occurrence bullets; all occurrence bullets merge chronologically; all-or-nothing (a marker-string fold raises `MemoryBlockError` before any write). Wiki: bodies concatenate (no bullet convention). The no-dup case is a byte-identical no-op (never writes). Singletons with legacy meta (a trailing `| previous_count:N` that fails `_FAILURE_META_RE`) are tolerated and copied verbatim; only dup-group members must parse cleanly. Built to fix the latent crash where a pre-0.37 exact-slug dup (`snapshot-regen-inside-worktree` c7+c4) would crash the next `/hm:wrapup` failure-write. Run `consolidate --file failures` as a one-off after the tool lands to clear the live backlog.
## [wiki:pattern] memory-retrieve-stemmer-recall | 2026-07-04
`memory_retrieve` lexical recall was raised by normalizing both scoring sides (`topic_tokens` + `_entry_token_set`) through a conservative, deterministic, pure-Python stemmer `_stem` before the existing token-overlap score — no ML dep, no trigram, `score_entry` signature/formula unchanged (PLAN-memory-retrieve-lexical-recall ADR-001/002). The stemmer strips one inflectional suffix (`-es`/`-s`/`-ing`/`-ed`) behind a `_MIN_STEM_LEN=4` guard, first-match-wins, no cascade; `-er`/`-tion` are deliberately excluded (over-collapse user→us, action→act) — conservatism IS the precision guard, so a zero-normalized-overlap entry scores 0 and is dropped by the existing `s>0` filter (ADR-003), no tiered score needed. **Key subtlety (cross-model REVIEW, Codex + code-reviewer 2026-07-04):** `-es` must be **sibilant-aware** — strip `-es` only when the pre-`es` stem ends in `_ES_SIBILANTS = ("s","x","z","ch","sh")`, else fall through to `-s`. A naive `-es`-before-`-s` order forecloses the most common technical-plural class (`files`→stuck, `updates`→`updat`, `nodes`/`codes`/`states`/`values`) because the trailing `e` is part of the stem and only the `s` is inflectional; the sibilant guard recovers `files→file` while keeping `boxes→box`/`dishes→dish`/`matches→match` and leaving every prior fixture green. Enumerate the sibilants explicitly, NOT a bare trailing `h`, so `-th`/`-ph`/`-gh`+`es` verbs (`breathes→breathe`) fall through instead of over-stemming (`breathes→breath`). Recall is still lexical-only; the Claude-turn rerank + the `memory_md consolidate` companion cover residual derivational variants.
## [wiki:gotcha] memory-heading-regex-extra-fields | 2026-07-04
`memory_retrieve._HEADING_RE` only accepts a heading of the exact shape `## [<tier>:<cat>] <slug> | <date>[ | count:N]` — the optional group is `(?:\s+\|\s+count:\d+)?\s*$`, so any **additional** pipe-delimited field after `count:N` makes the whole heading fail to match and the entry is **silently not parsed** (never enters the candidate pool — worse than a recall miss). Discovered 2026-07-04 during PLAN-memory-retrieve-lexical-recall: the two live `ruff-format` failures carry `| previous_count:2` headings (written by the failure-recurrence dedup path), so a CLI query returns them from NEITHER the raw nor the stemmed scorer — the empty-looking fence was a parse gap, not a recall gap. Fix is orthogonal to recall scoring (extend the heading regex to tolerate/capture trailing fields like `previous_count`) — candidate for its own PLAN. Diagnostic tell: `load_memory_dir` returns fewer entries than `grep -c '^## \[' failures.md`.
## [wiki:architecture] second-opinion-multi-model | 2026-07-25
`harness.yaml.codex_second_opinion` generalized to `second_opinion.models: list[Literal["codex","antigravity"]]` (PLAN-second-opinion-multi-model) — Codex CLI and Google Antigravity (`agy`) each cast a real k-of-N second-opinion vote, independently or both. Load-bearing invariants: (1) K stays **fixed at 2** as the voter pool N grows — `conditional_router.scope_aware_consensus`'s `len(reviewers)>=2` is source-agnostic, so this was a prose-only template generalization with ZERO Python consensus-math change (ADR-006); enabling more models makes consensus *easier* (recall-favoring) by design. (2) Per-model config sub-blocks (`second_opinion.codex.{hermetic,output_schema_path}`, `second_opinion.antigravity.{model}`) so Codex-only flags never silently no-op on antigravity (ADR-002). (3) **CORRECTED 2026-08-08 — `agy` DOES enforce output shape.** This line used to deny that agy had any schema flag, naming the Codex spelling. That denial is true only of that spelling: agy's flag is `--json-schema`, gated behind `--output-format json`. Nobody ran `agy --help`, and the un-checked half became the assertion — the same shape as the `--print` arity bug one entry below. Nine parse-failure rows are attributable to it. Enforcement is best-effort though (`structured_output` observed ABSENT on a `status: SUCCESS` reply), so the fail-closed extractor stays as a MANDATORY fallback. `agy` has NO hermetic flags — `codex_adapter.extract_antigravity_payload` fail-closes: strips fences, requires EXACTLY ONE JSON payload **anchored at the first structural opener**, caps input, converts RecursionError→ValueError. **(4) CORRECTED 2026-07-25 — the old text here was the bug.** It said the recipe "MUST begin with `agy --print --sandbox`". That order is wrong: `--print` takes the prompt as its **VALUE**, so `agy --print --sandbox … < file` made the literal string `--sandbox` the prompt, ran with no sandbox, and never read stdin — every antigravity vote ever cast was vacuous at exit 0. Correct order is **`agy --sandbox --print "<prompt>"`**. The stated rationale (an external `timeout` wrapper would break the allow-rule prefix match) is also moot: no rendered recipe inlines either raw CLI any more — `harness_maker.second_opinion_invoke` owns argv construction for both models (PLAN-second-opinion-invocation-and-slug-cap ADR-001), and `subprocess.run(timeout=)` is not banned. agy's native `--print-timeout` is still used, with the process-level timeout as an outer backstop so agy's own diagnostic fires first. **(5) CORRECTED — "CLI missing → exit 127" is false under `shell=False`**: there is no shell to synthesize 127. A missing binary raises `FileNotFoundError`, a timeout `TimeoutExpired`, a non-executable `PermissionError`. Any branch matrix keyed on 127 is dead code. Graceful degrade still holds for every mode (warn-and-proceed, never blocks the stage), and the ledger now carries one row per invocation written to the **base** repo. Migration: schema_version 2→3, silent `codex_second_opinion→second_opinion.models=["codex"]`. Output contract: plan stage `second_opinion_results:[{model,status,reconciliation}]` (one entry per model).
## [wiki:architecture] session-tier-checkpoint-only | 2026-07-10
The `.claude/memory/session/<date>.md` tier is compaction-checkpoint-only (PLAN-session-tier-slim ADR-001): written SOLELY by the `flush_session` PreCompact hook, read only by `execute`/`workflow_command` for interrupted-session resume. It is deliberately excluded from `memory_retrieve` (which indexes wiki+failures only) and is NOT written by wrapup. Durable decisions belong in `wiki.md`/`failures.md` (retrieval-indexed) + PLAN ADRs — never the session tier. The removed `[decision:...]` journal had zero machine consumers: a write-heavy, read-thin tier whose per-stage whole-file reads cost context for no realized retrieval value.
## [wiki:convention] parallel-bash-dispatch-literal-paths | 2026-07-10
When a rendered command tells Claude to dispatch multiple recipes as parallel Bash calls (e.g. codex + agy second-opinion invokes in one message), and the per-model recipes reuse the same shell var names (`prompt_tmp`/`out_tmp`), the directive MUST instruct substituting each recipe's LITERAL printed temp-file paths — NOT the shared shell variable. Rationale: Claude Code's Bash tool gives each call a FRESH shell (env vars do not persist across calls), so identical var names across recipes collide and a shared `$prompt_tmp` could resolve to the wrong model's file. Verified in-session: parallel codex+agy dispatch with literal paths works; code-reviewer flagged the var-name footgun when parallelizing `second_opinion_dispatch.md.j2` (PLAN-session-tier-slim ADR-002).
## [wiki:architecture] spec-optional-task-driven | 2026-07-15
PLAN-spec-optional-task-driven hardened the task-driven "don't force SPEC" contract against the flip-without-re-render window. spec_need.py (the verify Check 6 ORACLE) now self-guards dev_mode at runtime: op-check/waiver-check short-circuit to satisfied/valid ONLY on a confident `dev_mode == "task-driven"` read, so a spec-driven→task-driven flip without re-render can't force SPEC via a stale verify.md (Check 6 reads the exit code → auto-PASS). Crucially FAIL-CLOSED — the deliberate INVERSE of spec_gate.py:111's advisory fail-OPEN: missing/unreadable/malformed/wrong-root → enforce, never a silent PASS, because this module IS the verification oracle (a config-read failure becoming PASS would bypass the gate). marker/record/marker-* stay pass-through so the ADR-009 anti-loop marker machinery is untouched (an earlier design that forced marker-read→null was proven by the plan-validator to defeat the anti-loop guard and cause an interactive re-prompt loop). plan Step 1.7 §1.7.2 enforcement is LLM-prose — unreachable at runtime — so it is SURFACED (not fixed) by a new readiness `plan_verify_dev_mode_match` health signal (mirrors wrapup_oracle_waiver_dev_mode_match, weight 15, N-A on absent/unreadable/unrecognized). Separately, 4 bare-HarnessConfig() render fallbacks (synthesize ×3, workflow_fuse ×1) are pinned to dev_mode=SPEC_DRIVEN so a future model-default flip can't silently drop Step 1.7/Check 6 (Codex 2nd-opinion caught this render blast radius; the models.py default stays SPEC_DRIVEN, the reverse-mapper interview:940 stays task-driven — an intentional, now-documented asymmetry). General lesson: a runtime dev_mode self-guard's fail-direction is surface-dependent — an advisory PreToolUse gate fails OPEN (spec_gate), a verification oracle fails CLOSED (spec_need).
## [wiki:architecture] hooks-load-from-settings-not-hooksjson | 2026-07-17
Claude Code reads project hooks **only** from settings files — a plain project's `.claude/hooks/hooks.json` is NOT a hook-config location (that path is valid for a *plugin bundle* only; see hooks.md's location table). harness-maker had been rendering all 11 hook modules there, so telemetry / worktree_gate / spec_gate / loop_gate / autopilot_guard / sessionid_envfile / flush_session / permission_gate / post_write_reminder / autopilot_autoarm were **dead in Claude Code** — while `sessionstart_drift` survived because the plugin bundle (`hooks/hooks.json` at plugin root) ships it. **Cursor (`.cursor/hooks.json`) and Codex (`.codex/hooks.json`) were unaffected — the gates are live there.** Scope the claim: dead in Claude Code only; do not generalize. Phase 1 moves the 5 non-blocking hooks into `settings.json`'s `hooks` key (ADR-006 staging; blocking gates follow in later phases). **Evidence trap:** `.claude/observability/metrics.jsonl` is the pre-0.7.1 `_LEGACY_NAME` (`_metrics_io.py:18`); telemetry writes `metrics-YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` (`telemetry.py:216`). Reading the legacy file and concluding "telemetry never ran" is wrong — the dated files held 7,550 entries. The valid proof is a **controlled experiment** (2026-07-17): hand-add two hooks to `settings.json`, open a NEW session, and watch both `metrics-<today>.jsonl` and `HM_SESSION_ID` flip from a recorded baseline; the same commands in `.claude/hooks/hooks.json` fire nothing. Merge contract: `hooks` is harness-owned but DEEP-merged (`_merge_hooks_json`, nested schema) so user hooks survive; `_entry_identity` keys on **every** command in a matcher group (a group holds N — SessionStart carries 2), and `_strip_shipped_commands` drops from a preserved mixed group only the commands the template already ships, else our command registers twice and fires twice (the 2026-05-28 spoton-triplication class). There is deliberately **no retire/delete branch** — see the failure entry.
## [wiki:architecture] permission-rule-matchability | 2026-07-17
Claude Code permission-rule matchability (0.40.0, `permission_syntax.is_matchable_rule` is the oracle): the file-permission check consults ONLY `Edit(<path>)` / `Read(<path>)` — `Write(<path>)`, `NotebookEdit(<path>)`, `Glob(<path>)` are accepted but never matched and warn at startup. Bash rules are matched per-subcommand after splitting on `&& || ; | |& & \n`, so a rule spanning a separator (`Bash(curl * | sh)`) can NEVER match and fails SILENTLY. Subagent frontmatter has NO `permissions:` field — Claude Code silently ignores any such block; the only real per-agent boundary is `tools:` (a tool the agent lacks cannot be used). Per-agent command scoping needs a PreToolUse hook or sandbox. The opted-in main-session deny baseline is `["Bash(rm:*)", "Edit(/etc/**)", "Edit(~/.ssh/**)", "Edit(~/.aws/**)"]`; `curl|sh` detection is the `permission_gate` PreToolUse hook's job, not a settings rule. `test_permission_syntax.py` fails the build on any unmatchable rendered rule and was verified to FAIL against d895800b.
## [wiki:architecture] textual-bash-guard-cannot-be-complete | 2026-07-17
A textual guard over arbitrary bash CANNOT be made complete — `autopilot_guard`'s `permission-surface-write` proved it over 5 adversarial rounds (security-reviewer + codex, K=2). Each round codex found one more in-scope spelled-path bypass the prior fix missed: verb-axis (`python -c`/`perl -i`) → path spellings (`cd .claude &&`, `.claude//`, `.claude/./`, quoted, `git -C`) → `..`-laden cd → pushd/CDPATH/`--work-tree`/multi-`-C`/exec-FD (`{fd}>`, `<>`) → command-substitution masking (`cat $(truncate … settings.json)`) → an allowlisted reader's write option (`less -O settings.json`). The convergent design: (1) resolved-target-path (shlex + cwd tracking + PurePosixPath), (2) a GENERAL BLOCK-BIASED BACKSTOP — block any segment naming a `.claude`/`.cursor`/`.codex` token or config basename unless it is a clean read (read-only allowlist `cat/head/tail/grep/jq/git diff/git log`, no `less`, no command substitution, no write-output flag). The conceptual boundary that ENDS the whack-a-mole: **path spelled in the segment → block; path reached by indirection (helper script, `$VAR`/`$(…)`/base64-assembled path, symlink) → out of scope, because no textual guard can see it.** Ship as defense-in-depth with that residual documented; the worktree sandbox is the real boundary (CLAUDE.md already says so). Do NOT enumerate spellings — draw the boundary.
## [wiki:architecture] guard-when-interactive-scope | 2026-07-18
PLAN-autopilot-guard-interactive-scope. `autonomy.guard_when: always|pipeline_only` (default `always`) controls WHEN autopilot_guard fires. Under persistent autopilot the `.hm-autopilot` marker is armed every session (autoarm), so `always` makes the never-auto guard + Stop-nag fire even in plain INTERACTIVE chats (marker armed, no pipeline running = pure friction). `pipeline_only` keeps the guard dormant until a real pipeline stage starts THIS session — signalled by a leading `.claude/.hm-pipeline-active` crumb (stamped at stage START by the `autopilot pipeline-active` CLI from the shared `stage_start_autopilot` partial, gated on `guard_when==pipeline_only` + not codex, byte-identical in `always` mode via `{%- -%}` trims) OR an active `.hm-loop-*` marker (existence, so a loop is guarded from its start). The crumb stores the PER-SESSION `HM_SESSION_ID`, NOT the project-scoped `_current_session_uuid` (see [[per-session-key-that-unions-by-a-non-session-key]] — the review-caught P1: a stable project key matches every future same-project session → the dormancy silently defeats after the first stage run). The guard reads its own `session_id` from the hook payload (`_payload_session_id`) and matches: a prior/parallel session's crumb bears a different id → foreign → dormant, so NO clear-on-arm is needed and parallel same-project sessions never interfere. Fail-toward-guarded: an unreadable crumb, a `.claude` glob error, or a degraded (empty) id on either side all block-bias to `True` (guarded) — never a silent disarm, and a crashed hook (which Claude Code treats as allow) is avoided. Absent/typo'd `guard_when` → `always` (fail-safe, no silent guard-disable). To go live a user sets `autonomy.guard_when: pipeline_only` in harness.yaml + re-renders.
## [wiki:architecture] install-ref-home-portability | 2026-07-21
harness-maker's `_compute_install_ref()` bakes the install ref into every rendered hook command AND every command/skill body. Before 0.42.0 it returned the absolute, home-prefixed plugin-cache path from `direct_url.json` (`/home/<user>/.claude/plugins/cache/harness-maker/harness-maker/<ver>`), so a committed `.claude/settings.json` flip-flopped per developer's home across a team repo. `synthesize._portablize_ref` now substitutes the render-machine home prefix with the literal `$HOME` — boundary-safe (`raw == home or raw.startswith(home + os.sep)`, so a sibling `/home/noel-other` is never corrupted to `$HOME-other`) — and it wraps EVERY `_compute_install_ref` return branch, so hooks and command/skill bodies are portable together. Hook-JSON `--with` MUST be double-quoted (JSON-escaped `\"`) so the IDE's shell expands `$HOME`; single quotes block expansion. Command/skill `.md` bodies stay unquoted (POSIX-only, space-free homes — ADR-003 accepted limitation). `render._assert_portable_install_ref` fails the render if a home-prefixed ref leaks — and it must fire on ALL hook renderers: `_render_settings_json`, `_render_hooks_json_merged`, AND `_render_pure_json` (the FRESH-render path for cursor/codex hooks — the first cut missed it, caught in review). Snapshot/conftest determinism: `regenerate.py` and `tests/unit/conftest.py` pin `_compute_install_ref` to the portable `$HOME/harness-maker` form so snapshots stay home-free and the assert passes under the monkeypatch. Team migration is a plain re-render (path-agnostic hook identity in `_merge_hooks_json` replaces the old absolute entry) then commit once — see docs/migration/portable-hook-paths.md.
## [wiki:architecture] hook-retirement-mechanism | 2026-07-21
Retiring a hook from the rendered `.claude/settings.json` needs MORE than deleting it from the settings template: `_merge_hooks_json`'s per-event union (`render.py`) deliberately has NO blanket retire branch (`<HM>:` marks namespace, not authorship — a hand-wired harness module must survive), so a template-only removal leaves the hook on every existing user's disk forever as pseudo-user content. The retirement mechanism is the append-only frozenset `_HARNESS_RETIRED_HOOK_INVOCATIONS` (normalized `<HM>:...` forms); `_strip_shipped_commands` drops `shipped_cmds ∪ retired` from a preserved entry, so re-render actually removes the hook. Safety differs from `_HARNESS_SHIPPED_DENY_LITERALS`: a LIVE hook has NO "provably enforces nothing" second invariant, so membership rests on ONE test (`test_retired_invocations_absent_from_current_templates` — absent from every current NESTED template: settings + `.codex/hooks.json`) plus the accepted risk that no user hand-wired the internal module. The frozenset applies to ALL nested-schema merges (settings AND codex), not just settings — Cursor is flat-schema and skipped. Separately: `templates/hooks/hooks.json.j2` is NOT dead source even though `.claude/hooks/hooks.json` is never read by Claude Code — `render.py`'s `render_stale_hooks_json_bytes` renders it as the pristine-oracle for `cli._retire_stale_hooks_json` (byte-match → delete stale pristine copies). Grep for readers before deleting any rendered asset.
## [wiki:architecture] harness-economics-observability | 2026-07-25
