PLAN-harness-economics-observability shipped a spend model built on Claude Code's OWN session transcripts (`~/.claude/projects/<enc-cwd>/*.jsonl` + `<session>/subagents/agent-*.jsonl`), because that is the only local artifact carrying token counts — `telemetry.py`'s four token fields were structurally zero on every line ever written (measured 0 non-zero in 2175) since the `PostToolUse` payload has no `usage`. **Load-bearing invariants:** (1) **No cost ÷ deliverable-count metric, ever** (ADR-002) — any such ratio puts verification spend in the numerator and nothing in the denominator, so a task that ran 3 review rounds and hardened a defect scores as *less* economic than one that skipped review; enforcement is SPLIT and stated as such — the data layer cannot express it (`ratio_field_kinds()` + a recursive real-schema scan incl. `@property`), the `/hm:metrics` prose layer carries a `@hm:economics:no-ratio` instruction block that a render-grep only proves is PRESENT. (2) The classifier is an **ordered ladder** `REWORK > VERIFY > PRODUCE > OTHER`, exactly one label per turn; rule 1 needs BOTH a "no VERIFY since that path's last write" clause AND a "the turn is not itself verify-attributed" clause, and rule 2 needs a "writes nothing" clause — without all three a `hm:review`-attributed turn that writes a fix classifies as VERIFY or REWORK, re-introducing the ratio bug *through the taxonomy*. `last_write_at` is TASK-scoped (a task worktree spans sessions) while `last_verify_at` is SESSION-scoped (concurrent sessions are supported; a peer's review must not absolve my rewrite). (3) Pricing is **per-turn from the turn's own `message.model`** against a versioned table; the configured `price_model` is a fallback for unrecognised strings only and must resolve through the SAME matcher (an exact-key lookup silently priced `claude-sonnet-4-5` at opus — 5x). (4) Discovery keeps lossy-prefix matching on purpose (a worktree is deleted when its task lands but its transcripts survive), so the project boundary is the per-turn `is_own_cwd` check, not the directory name; `resolve_project_root` collapses a worktree `--root` back to base first. (5) ADR-009 puts the liveness smoke in `health.md.j2` as a Bash step, NOT a `readiness.py` signal — `compute_readiness` takes no transcript root, so a signal would force `Path.home()` into a scored path and break HOME isolation. Measured on this repo: `hm:wrapup` is the least economic stage (carry 0.81, 451k ctx/turn, PRODUCE 95 of 1087 turns) because it runs last and carries the most inflated context; `hm:review` is nearly pure VERIFY with the LOWEST carry — review was never the cost problem. **Corrected 2026-07-29 ([[transcript-records-are-not-api-calls]]): the TURN COUNTS in that last sentence are ~2.1× too high** — `load_turns` emitted one turn per JSONL record, and one API call spans 2–3 records carrying identical `usage`. Ratios (carry 0.81) and ctx/turn are unaffected; the ranking stands. `IngestionDiagnostics.coverage` now divides by `assistant_calls`, not `assistant_lines` — a denominator left in the old unit would have pinned this dimension's one drift signal near 0.45 permanently.
## [wiki:architecture] second-opinion-invoker | 2026-07-25
Both cross-model second-opinion CLI invocations (`codex exec`, `agy`) now live in `harness_maker.second_opinion_invoke`, a single tested module that owns argv construction, base-root and config resolution, prompt delivery, status classification, adaptation, and the ledger row; `/hm:health`'s smoke calls the same entrypoint. Before this, the two calls lived only in rendered prose, which has no execution surface — render tests can do nothing but grep the text, and four distinct silent-skip bugs shipped that way. The rule that follows: **an external contract that a rendered prompt asks the model to execute must be exercisable by a test, or it will be wrong and report success.** Three specifics worth carrying: under `shell=False` there is no exit 127, so "CLI not installed" arrives as `FileNotFoundError` (a timeout as `TimeoutExpired`, a non-executable binary as `PermissionError`) and any branch matrix keyed on 127 is dead; a linked worktree is detected by `--git-dir != --git-common-dir`, not by porcelain's first entry, which under `--separate-git-dir` is the external git dir rather than the checkout; and ledger rows must be written to the **base** repo, because a worktree copy is destroyed by `task-land`. The ledger now carries one row per invocation rather than skip-only rows, so skip-rate is `skipped/total` excluding `stage: "health"`.
## [wiki:architecture] allow-literal-prune | 2026-07-26
`render._merge_permissions` unions the on-disk `.claude/settings.json` permission lists with the template's so user-authored rules survive re-render, which also preserves every rule harness-maker itself ever shipped. `deny` already had an exact-literal prune (`_HARNESS_SHIPPED_DENY_LITERALS`); `allow` now has one too, plus a second mechanism the deny side does not need. **The two halves have different safety arguments and must not be conflated.** The deny prune is gated on a PROOF — `permission_syntax.is_matchable_rule(rule) is False`, i.e. the rule provably enforces nothing, so deleting it removes zero protection. Every allow literal here is LIVE, so that proof is unavailable and is deliberately not faked; the substitute is the DIRECTION of failure — a wrongly-pruned allow rule never removes protection, it can only cause a refusal, and never silently. That "it only costs a prompt" half is INTERACTIVE-ONLY: under `claude -p` an affected call fails instead of asking, and under `--dangerously-skip-permissions` the prune is a no-op. Membership is bounded by two real invariants (we shipped it — `git log -S` over `templates/settings/`; we no longer ship it — absent from every rendered settings.json) plus near-miss tests. A third, "never leaves less than a fresh install", is STRUCTURAL not test-enforced: the union builds from `(*new_list, *existing_list)` while the prune filters only `existing_list`, so it holds by construction and constrains nothing — do not cite it as evidence about the prune set. `_HARNESS_SHIPPED_ALLOW_PATTERNS` is the second mechanism: retired rules whose text embeds the user's resolved `harness_maker_src_path` cannot be named by any exact literal, so they need an anchored regex. A regex that deletes user config is strictly more dangerous than a literal, so it carries a tighter leash — doubly anchored, the wildcard confined to the interpolated slot AND required to name harness-maker itself (`[^ ]*harness[-_]maker[^ ]*`), must full-match a shape git history proves the renderer emitted, and must match nothing the templates currently render. A free `.+` there deleted `--with requests`, `--with .` and `--with /path/to/my/fork` — rules only a user can have written. Adding an entry to either table means satisfying every one of those tests, not just the two membership ones.
## [wiki:architecture] attribution-source-ladder | 2026-07-26
Turn-level spend attribution now runs a five-rung precedence ladder, and every priced turn carries exactly one label: `direct` (the turn's own `attributionSkill` — ground truth) > `ledger` (a stage-span record from `.claude/observability/stage-spans.jsonl`, emitted as a side effect of `worktree task-preflight --stage <name>`) > `inferred` (a cached run-boundary verdict from `.claude/observability/run-verdicts.jsonl`, judged once by the `/hm:metrics` prose layer and keyed by `(turn uuid, classifier_version)`) > `adjacency` (the pre-existing neighbour estimate, which resolves to `(unattributed)`) > `none`. The conserved axis is the SOURCE: per-source USD sums to `total_usd`; `by_agent` remains a deliberate cross-cut and claims no conservation. Three properties are load-bearing and easy to break: (1) both span caps (`span_max_turns` 400 / `span_max_min` 240) are TERMINAL — a capped turn must reach `source: none`, so it is excluded from `inferred` and `adjacency` and must not open a run, or spend gets reported as both capped and attributed; (2) spans are chained PER SESSION on both the read side (`_build_spans` keys its open span by `session_id`) and the write side (`span-end` inspects the last event of the CALLER's session) — the ledger is shared across concurrent sessions, and either half being session-blind lets one session truncate or claim another's; (3) `boundaries` (the CLI the classifier drives) and `economics report` MUST derive their `find_boundaries` arguments from the one shared `run_classify.boundary_inputs` helper — when they diverged, verdicts were recorded under UUIDs the report never looked up, so paid-for judgments were silently discarded and counted as cache misses. Safe default throughout: an unresolved boundary stays unattributed. A wrong `continuation` is invisible (spend lands on a stage that did not incur it and the report looks complete); an unattributed run is visible. Cache miss, `unknown`, unparseable verdict, and "nothing to continue" all resolve to leave-it-alone-and-count-it.
## [wiki:gotcha] strict-json-use-model-validate-json | 2026-07-26
Pydantic models configured with `ConfigDict(strict=True)` reject JSON-native shapes in PYTHON validation mode that they accept in JSON validation mode. Two instances in one task, both of which made every well-formed record read as malformed: (a) `SpanEvent.model_validate(json.loads(line))` rejected an ISO-8601 string for a `datetime` field, so every correct ledger line counted toward `malformed_lines` and the span reader saw an empty ledger; (b) `WrapupReceipt.model_validate(dict)` rejected a JSON array for a `tuple[str, ...]` field, so every well-formed agent receipt was reported as schema-violating and the delegated wrapup would have fallen through to its degraded path on every run. **Rule: when the bytes came from JSON, validate them AS JSON — `Model.model_validate_json(raw)`, never `model_validate(json.loads(raw))`.** The JSON-mode validator applies the lax coercions JSON actually needs (string→datetime, array→tuple) while `strict=True` still rejects genuinely wrong types. Both instances were invisible to type-checking and to any test that constructed the model in Python; only a round trip through real serialized bytes found them, which is why a `write → read` test is the minimum bar for any on-disk record format. Related: `[fail:test] shipped-entry-point-not-exercised` — same shape one layer up (the Python path works, the serialized path does not).
## [wiki:architecture] delegation-needs-render-and-task-branch | 2026-07-26
Setting `delegation.stages: ["wrapup"]` in `harness.yaml` is **two** steps short of the stage actually delegating, and both are silent.

**1. The flag only takes effect after a re-render.** `templates/stages/wrapup.md.j2` wraps Step 0.5 in `{% if config.delegation is defined and 'wrapup' in config.delegation.stages %}`, so the dispatch instruction is *absent from the rendered command file* until `/hm:make --update` runs. Editing `harness.yaml` alone changes nothing a session will ever read. Confirm with `grep 'Step 0.5' .claude/commands/hm/wrapup.md`, not by re-reading the yaml.

**2. Even rendered, it self-skips off a task branch.** `wrapup_brief` returns `status: degraded` whenever HEAD is not `hm/<slug>` — the standalone / recovered wrapup that ADR-006 makes first-class — and Step 0.5 then sends the stage to its inline body. A wrapup run from the base repo on `main` therefore takes the **inline** path with the flag fully on, indistinguishably from the flag being off unless you read the `verdict.reason` line.

The practical consequence for ADR-011's soak: the "10 clean reconciles" clock starts at the first wrapup that runs **inside a task worktree**, not at the config flip. Work that accumulates uncommitted on `main` will complete any number of wrapups without producing a single receipt.

Check the live state, don't infer it:

```
uv run python -m harness_maker.wrapup_brief --root .
```

`status: ok` means the next wrapup will dispatch; `status: degraded` names the missing field in `verdict.reason`.

See also [[fail:design written-under-one-key-read-under-another]] for the neighbouring failure shape — a config that is written correctly and never reaches the consumer.
## [wiki:review] reproduction-outranks-consensus-count | 2026-07-27
Consensus counts voices; it does not check them. Round 5 of `economics-attribution-and-carry` produced both error directions in the same run, and the resolution was the same both times: **go measure**.

**Three concurring voices, refuted.** `code-reviewer` (P1), `codex` (P1) and `antigravity` (P0) all flagged `git diff --shortstat | grep -oE '[0-9]+ insertion'` as a live silent-zero. That is 3-of-4 at K=2 — auto-fix territory. Both named mechanisms are false: the singular is a prefix of the plural so `17 insertions(+)` yields `17`, and **none of the 18 git catalogs installed here translate the string** (verified via `gettext.translation('git', …).ngettext(...)` across every `git.mo` on the box, plus end-to-end — `git status` under `ko_KR.UTF-8` prints Korean while `git diff --shortstat` stays English). Shipping their verdict verbatim would have put a fabricated "live P1" in the changelog. The line was still changed, because `--numstat` is strictly better; the *severity* was downgraded to fragility.

**One voice with a reproduction, adopted over the count.** `antigravity` alone reported that `test_read_only_agents_…` parses `tools:` as `str(value).split(",")`. Running the real parser on `tools: [Read, Write, Bash]` showed the intersection empty and the test green — a security check silently not enforcing. A single finding I can execute is a fact; four findings I cannot are a poll.

The operative rule: **a reproduction outranks a count, in both directions.** Reproduce before adopting a consensus finding, and reproduce before dismissing a single-source one. Severity is set by what the reproduction shows, not by the highest number any voter assigned.

Mechanics that made this cheap:
- Refutation: run the mechanism the reviewer named, on this machine, before writing the changelog line.
- Adoption: mutation-check — delete the code, run the suite, name the test that dies. If none dies, the fix is untested no matter how the test reads. That check caught the first draft of this round's own vault regression test, which asserted a property the *other* half of the same fix already satisfied.

See [[fail:test assertion-invariant-over-named-dimension]] — the shape all four of this round's broken gates shared.
## [wiki:convention] family-row-fallback-in-per-model-tables | 2026-07-27
A lookup table keyed by model id where a bare FAMILY row (`opus`, `haiku`, `sonnet`) coexists with point-release rows (`opus-5`, `haiku-4-5`) is NOT a table with one stale row to fix. The family row is a legitimate fallback carrying the LEGACY rate for genuinely older ids, and `resolve_model_family` matches keys as substrings with longest-match wins, so a point-release key shadows its family by design. Two rules follow, and one change violated both, in opposite directions, in a single pass: (1) **never edit the family row in place** to a newer model's value — an earlier draft rewrote `PRICE_TABLE["haiku"]` from 0.25/1.25 to Haiku 4.5's 1/5 and thereby repriced every Haiku 3 turn 4x, the same class of error the change existed to remove; (2) **never omit a point-release key that falls on the wrong side of the boundary you just drew** — without an `opus-4-5` key, `claude-opus-4-5` falls through to `opus` = 15/75, the exact 3x error, sitting precisely on the "pre-4.5" line the ADR itself drew. Third rule, less obvious: **two tables keyed the same way may legitimately DISAGREE about which ids they carry.** `PRICE_TABLE` prices `opus-4-5` / `sonnet-4-5`; `cache_diagnostics._MIN_CACHEABLE_PREFIX` deliberately does not, because no release-specific cache minimum is published for them — an intermediate revision added both at 1024 "to make the two tables agree" and that 1024 was an inherited guess. A wrong RATE yields an approximate dollar figure; a wrong MINIMUM yields a confident `miss_min_threshold` verdict plus a remediation telling the user to grow a prefix that may already be large enough. Pricing and diagnosing have different tolerances for a guess, so let the tables disagree and say why in a comment on the absent keys. Finally, **the fallback needs its own signal.** Because a family key matches as a substring, an id released after the table is written (`claude-opus-9`) resolves to `opus` and `used_fallback = family is None` never fires — it is priced at the legacy rate with no trace in `unknown_models` or `fallback_priced_turns`, which only ever caught ids matching no key at all (`gpt-*`). A distinct counter (`report.family_priced_turns` / `family_priced_models`, detected as `family is not None and "-" not in family`) is what makes the next model release visible instead of silently 3x, and it must be asserted through the serialized report the CLI emits, not through the in-memory flag.
## [wiki:gotcha] worktree-score-must-be-base-to-base | 2026-07-27
`/hm:verify`'s structural-score check — and anything else built on `harness_maker.readiness` — must run in the **BASE** repo, never inside `.worktrees/<slug>/`. `readiness` scores its guardrail and workflow signals by reading `.claude/commands/`, `.claude/skills/`, `.claude/hooks/` and `.claude/settings.json`, all of which are harness render output that this repo leaves UNTRACKED (and partly gitignored). `git worktree add` materialises the tracked tree only, so in a worktree those paths simply do not exist and six signals fail for absence rather than for defect. Measured 2026-07-27 on `hm/token-economy-step-pruning`: base scored **58**, the same commit re-scored inside the task worktree scored **47** (−11, past `/hm:verify`'s −5 FAIL threshold), while the change's real base-to-base movement was **55 → 58 (+3)**. The trap is that a score is a bare number with no provenance — once it is written down, a worktree number and a base number look identical, and the threshold check will happily subtract two values produced in different trees. Rules: state which tree produced each endpoint, compute both endpoints in the same one, and when a signal drops unexpectedly run `git ls-files --error-unmatch .claude/settings.json` — a non-zero exit means the file is invisible to any worktree checkout and the drop is an artefact. Related: [wiki:gotcha] cwd-shifts-across-bash-calls (the other "right command, wrong tree" trap), [fail:test] worktree-score-omits-untracked-assets, [fail:test] snapshot-regen-inside-worktree.
## [wiki:architecture] cache-key-allowlist-not-blocklist | 2026-07-27
A cache key that fingerprints the ambient environment is a key for the CONTEXT, not for the code — and in a multi-agent harness the context changes constantly. Measured here: the main loop's Bash exports `CLAUDE_EFFORT`, a subagent's does not, so `_env_hash()` differed by exactly one variable and every marker `/hm:verify` wrote was structurally unreadable by `/hm:wrapup`. The general rule this repo now follows for `observability/verification_cache.py`: **hash an explicit allowlist of variables that can change what the checks CONCLUDE, never a blocklist of ones you happen to know are noisy.** The inverted form is seductive because "over-invalidate rather than risk a false PASS" sounds strictly safer, but it is only safer if the cache ever hits — a permanently-cold cache pays the full cost of the safety property and collects none of the benefit, and it looks identical to a healthy one from the outside. Two corollaries worth carrying to any similar cache: (1) do NOT derive the test's coverage list from the allowlist itself — deleting a member would delete its own test case, making the fence invariant over the exact dimension it guards; keep an independent literal plus a subset assertion for the growth direction. (2) Assert the fence against the component the policy governs (`_env_hash()`), not the composite key — a first draft here asserted the composite and gated nothing, because `_tool_versions()` shells out with the patched environment inherited, so a sentinel value moved the key through tool resolution rather than through the env hash; removing `PATH` from the allowlist left all 17 cases green.
## [wiki:convention] golden-render-pin-before-first-capture | 2026-07-27
A committed golden render is a claim about the code; if it also encodes WHERE it was produced, it is a claim about the machine and it will be false everywhere else. Three rules this repo now follows for any test that renders templates into a committed fixture. (1) **The pin comes before the first golden.** `synthesize._HARNESS_MAKER_PKG_ROOT` and `_compute_install_ref` resolve via `__file__`, so from a `.worktrees/<x>/` checkout they bake the worktree path into `harness_maker_src_path` — 13-45 occurrences per rendered command. Two places own that pin, `tests/snapshot/regenerate.py` and `tests/unit/conftest.py`, and a NEW test directory inherits neither; `tests/render/conftest.py` had to add its own. (2) **Expose the pin as a function, not only as an autouse fixture.** Goldens are captured by a script that never enters pytest, and a pin the capture path cannot reach is exactly how the leak got in — `tests/render/conftest.py::pin_install_ref(monkeypatch)` is callable from both. (3) **Gate the property, not the symptom.** `test_no_golden_bakes_a_machine_specific_absolute_path` rejects any `/home|/Users|/root` path in a golden and requires the portable `$HOME/...` form to be present, so a capture from any checkout location fails — not just from a worktree. Grepping for `.worktrees` is NOT that gate: this repo's templates contain prose about the worktree workflow, so that grep returns hits on a perfectly clean golden and hides the real signal, which is the worktree NAME. Companion rule for the consumer side: an invariance extractor over a rendered document must return position-INDEPENDENT witnesses (enclosing headings, counts, sorted names) rather than character offsets, or any edit that changes the document's length before the matches fails the guard for a reason unrelated to what it guards.
## [wiki:convention] jinja-comment-whitespace-moves-renders | 2026-07-27
Jinja whitespace is load-bearing in this repo's partials, and a *comment* is not free. Three ways a comment silently changed every rendered command and every snapshot hash during PLAN-token-economy-step-pruning Phase 4. (1) **A comment on its own line contributes its trailing newline to the output.** `{# note #}\n{% if ... %}` renders one extra blank line wherever that partial is included — 20 snapshot entries moved instead of the expected 5, in files the change was not supposed to touch. (2) **Trimming it with `{#- ... -#}` overcorrects**: `{#-` eats the blank line *before* the comment, which was the blank line after a heading, so the heading and its paragraph fused into `## Stage summary — print before you STOPSkip this banner...`. (3) **A literal `#}` inside the comment's prose terminates the comment early** — writing "trimming it with `-#}` eats the blank line" produced `TemplateSyntaxError: unexpected 'end of statement block'`. Rule: put explanatory comments in the partial's FILE HEADER, where the surrounding newlines are already inert, never beside the conditional they explain; and never write `#}` or `%}` inside comment prose. Related: `keep_trailing_newline=True` in `render._make_env`, so an `{% include %}`d file's final newline reaches the output — a partial split out of a larger one must end with `{% endif -%}` (or have its trailing byte truncated) to render byte-identically to the text it replaced. The way to catch all of these is to diff a full pre-change render against a post-change one, and to check WHICH snapshot entries changed rather than accepting that some did.
## [wiki:convention] autouse-pin-misses-module-fixtures | 2026-07-27
A module- or session-scoped fixture is set up BEFORE any function-scoped autouse fixture, so an autouse `monkeypatch` pin is **not in effect while that fixture runs**. This matters wherever a test directory renders templates into a module-scoped fixture and then asserts on the bytes: `tests/structural/test_command_size_budget.py` freezes character counts as committed constants, and an unpinned render bakes the running checkout's absolute path into `harness_maker_src_path` dozens of times, making every constant a measurement of one machine. The fix is the one `tests/render/conftest.py` already learned for a different reason (its goldens are captured by a script that never enters pytest): **expose the pin as a plain `pin_install_ref(monkeypatch)` function as well as an autouse fixture**, and have the module-scoped fixture apply it itself inside `with pytest.MonkeyPatch.context() as mp`. Four directories now own a copy of those five lines — `tests/snapshot/regenerate.py`, `tests/unit/conftest.py`, `tests/render/conftest.py`, `tests/structural/conftest.py` — deliberately, because a shared helper imported across test directories depends on pytest's rootdir-sensitive import machinery and a pin that silently fails to load is the exact failure being prevented. Extract only if a fifth is needed. Gate the property, not the plumbing: assert no `/home|/Users|/root` path survives in any rendered artifact and that the portable `$HOME/...` form is present, so a render from any checkout location fails, not just from a worktree. See [[fail:test snapshot-regen-inside-worktree]].
## [wiki:architecture] carry-is-a-main-loop-phenomenon | 2026-07-27
Carry is a MAIN-LOOP phenomenon, measured over 23.3M chars of local transcript via `python -m harness_maker.economics composition --root .` (committed 2026-07-28; the report side is `economics report`). Main loop = 87.9% of spend at 70.0% carry, 300k–431k ctx/turn; the 13 subagent kinds = 12.1% at 35.2%, 33k–128k. **Corrected 2026-07-29 (see [[transcript-records-are-not-api-calls]]): every TURN COUNT and every ABSOLUTE DOLLAR in this entry is ~2.1× too high** — the reader counted JSONL records, and Claude Code writes one per content block with the same `usage` stamped on each. The SHARES and ctx/turn figures survive unchanged (a share is a ratio of two equally-inflated terms; a mean over identical values is that value), so every ordinal finding below stands. On the frozen corpus the shipped fix takes 24,082 records to 10,945 calls and main-loop carry from 74.9% to 79.2%. Context per turn is **monotonic in pipeline position** — research 80k/25% → plan 187k/42% → execute 309k/67% → review 322k/65% → verify 388k/83% → wrapup 431k/82% — so `verify`+`wrapup` pay **$4.75 of cache-read rent per $1 of work** while needing almost none of what they carry. Delegation does NOT fix that: `delegation.stages: ["wrapup"]` is live and wrapup is still the worst carrier. **Corrected 2026-07-29 — the reason given here was wrong twice over.** (a) The stated mechanism, "delegation reduces what is ADDED, not what is already in the prefix", is true of ctx/turn and FALSE of carry: carry is ctx/turn × calls, so moving a stage's turns into a subagent removes their carry outright. (b) The empirical claim rested on a config that was live but not firing — measured 2 dispatches in 16 runs, because the rendered `!` line derived the brief from cwd while `!` runs at the base repo (`degraded` on the normal path). Both are now addressed: `--slug` fixes the seam and `/hm:health`'s weight-0 `delegation_fires` signal makes a configured-but-never-firing delegation visible instead of silent. Whether delegation actually moves the number is UNMEASURED — the corpus this entry was built on contains almost no dispatched wrapups. Composition of the prefix: Bash 27.9% (grep/rg 10.8%, file inspection 5.2%, harness_maker CLI 4.3%, pytest 3.5%), slash-command bodies 17.6%, Write 15.9%, Edit 11.2%, Read results **7.8%**, human-typed **0.1%**. Two consequences that redirect work: prompt compaction targets a real 17.6% but PLAN-token-economy-step-pruning Phase 4 moved 4,437 chars of ONE of twenty commands while Phase 3 added 3,765 to the heavily-invoked `review` — net the shipped command surface GREW 0.75%; and Phase 3's reviewer read-budget was installed on the cheap population (reviewers carry 23–36% and cost $183) while the expensive reader is the main loop at 65.4%/$1,080 with no budget at all. **Measurement trap:** do not multiply a share computed WITHIN one bucket (entry-weighted) by that bucket's carry-weighted total — that mixed weighting understated grep/rg as 8.3% when it is 10.8%. Entry- and residency-weighting otherwise agree within ±5% (content enters uniformly across a session), so residency weighting is deliberately not implemented.
## [wiki:architecture] transcript-records-are-not-api-calls | 2026-07-29
Claude Code writes one assistant JSONL record **per content block** (`thinking` / `text` / `tool_use`) and stamps the **same** `usage` on every one, so `economics_source.load_turns` — which emitted one `TurnRecord` per record — billed a single API call two or three times. Verified verbatim: `msg_011CdF1bxf4qyKS3RE7i9RQR` spans 3 records all carrying `in=2 out=1663 cr=20352 cw=56171`. Over the frozen corpus, 24,082 assistant records are **10,945 calls**. Effect on every previously quoted figure: main-loop spend $4,614 → **$2,150**, carry 74.9% → **79.2%**, `hm:wrapup` $782/$657-carry → **$339/$294**. What SURVIVES the correction: `mean_context_tokens` (a mean over identical values is that value) and `context_composition`'s character shares (each content block appears in exactly one record) — so the ordinal findings of RESEARCH-context-carry-economics stand and only its absolute dollars were wrong. **Grouping is per-FILE, not global**: two `message.id`s genuinely recur across different `subagents/agent-*.jsonl` transcripts, so a global key would fuse different agents' turns into one call — this was written as a defensive "an id might repeat" and turned out to be real, found only because the pre-registered oracle disagreed by exactly 3. Collapse rules (ADR-003): `usage` from the max-`output_tokens` record (ties→last), `written_paths` **unioned** (the `tool_use` block lives in exactly one record, and losing it silently re-labels PRODUCE/REWORK as OTHER), identity fields (`uuid`/`ts`/`session_id`/`is_sidechain`) from the first record in file order so the retroactive-classification verdict cache key is stable. Measured across all 5,757 multi-record groups: every field except `output_tokens` is identical within a group, and `output_tokens` differs in **1** — so the max rule fires essentially never on real data and a fixture must synthesise it or the branch ships unexercised. `IngestionDiagnostics` gained `duplicate_records_collapsed` (records dropped by grouping, summed over ALL groups including later-skipped ones — a residual would fold in `no_usage`/`foreign_cwd` skips) and `assistant_calls`; `coverage`'s denominator moved from record-counted `assistant_lines` to group-counted `assistant_calls`, without which collapsing would have driven it to ~0.45 permanently and retired the one ingestion-drift signal by making it always red.
## [wiki:architecture] delegation-fires-health-signal | 2026-07-29
`/hm:health`'s `delegation_fires` answers "is the configured delegation actually firing?" — the question four dead months could not. It reads `.claude/observability/delegation.jsonl`, written at the BASE repo by three call sites (`wrapup_brief.main` → `kind: brief`, `wrapup_receipt.main` → `kind: dispatch`, and the rendered self-skip branch → `dispatch/unavailable`). **A derivable brief is not a dispatch** — a signal built on brief rows alone reinstalls the blind spot one level up, which is why `kind` exists. The verdict reads a RECENCY WINDOW, never the whole file: an append-only ledger makes "has a dispatch ever succeeded" go green on the first success and stay green through every regression. Window = the 10 most recent **same-stage** `kind: brief` rows **of ANY status**, ordered by PARSED timestamp, with undatable briefs excluded before the slice, plus same-stage dispatch rows at or after the oldest. Every clause there was a defect first: anchoring on `status: ok` briefs means the anchor stops being written by the very regression being detected (floor freezes in the last healthy era → `ok` forever); ordering by file position diverges from timestamp order under late writes and interleaved sessions, and `Z` vs `+00:00` are the same instant a string compare ranks hours apart; an undatable brief sorted "oldest" survives the slice, becomes the floor, and admits every dispatch in the file (fail-OPEN — the opposite of the same rule in the dispatch position); and the window is stage-scoped because `verify` is delegatable and its briefs degrade structurally, so unfiltered they flip a healthy wrapup to `brief-degrading` while a verify dispatch row vouches for a wrapup that never ran. Four distinct FAILING actions (`no-rows` / `brief-degrading` / `no-dispatch` / flag-off), and they must differ — `Signal.action` has exactly one consumer (`improvement._extract_layer1_actions`) gated on `if sig.passed`, so a PASSING signal's action renders NOWHERE; "pass with an action" ships inert. Weight **0**, `hard_gate=False`: `_score_signals` sums the weights of PASSED signals, so a failing weight-0 signal is score-neutral and adding a weighted one would re-score every existing harness. **Accepted detection floor (user, 2026-07-29):** `ok` needs only SOME dispatch in the window, so intermittent dispatch — including the 2-of-16 regime that motivated the work — reads green; counting instead of existence was declined because ADR-005 makes `dispatch ÷ brief-ok` a lower bound (reconciliation is model-executed and skippable), so any threshold catching 2-of-16 reddens healthy harnesses. Re-opening it needs a measurement nobody has: how often a real dispatch skips reconciliation.

## [wiki:architecture] cursor-reads-the-claude-command-render | 2026-07-29
`.cursor/commands/` is **dead code** (`render.py:585-597` — no template feeds it), so **Cursor reads `.claude/commands/hm/*.md`, the same file Claude Code reads.** Consequence for every target-conditional feature in a stage template: the gate must be `"cursor" not in config.targets`, NOT `"claude-code" in config.targets`. The two are not the same condition, and the difference is not cosmetic — a `targets: [claude-code, cursor]` harness satisfies the second and would be shipped whatever the block emits. PLAN-workflow-step-audit ADR-010 specified the research fan-out as "Claude target only" and that phrasing is under-determined at the file level: `Task(subagent_type="Explore")` is a **Claude Code built-in, not an asset this harness renders**, so a Cursor session reading the shared file gets a dispatch that cannot resolve. This is CLAUDE.md checklist-#2's second layer — the bytes on disk are right and only the executed content differs, which no render-grep for presence can catch. The gate that DOES catch it renders **with** cursor in `targets` and asserts the dispatch is absent; and the general form, worth keeping, is `test_every_dispatched_agent_resolves_on_the_target_that_gets_it` — collect every `subagent_type="..."` from every rendered command and require each to be either a rendered `agents/*.md` or a named built-in. Practical cost, stated because it is easy to miss: this repo's own `targets` is `[claude-code, cursor, codex]`, so the fan-out is implemented, tested on both arms, and **renders nowhere here** — the 12.1%-of-wall-clock lever it was built for is dormant for any harness that lists Cursor. Two variants, not three, is the durable fact: `.claude/commands/hm/*.md` (Claude + Cursor) and `.agents/skills/hm-*/SKILL.md` (Codex).
## [wiki:architecture] stage-prose-costs-5x-via-fused-inlining | 2026-07-30
Every fused command (`exec-rev`, `exec-rev-wrap`, `exec-rev-wrap-ver`, `plan-exec-rev`) **inlines the whole stage body**, so a character added to `templates/stages/review.md.j2` is paid FIVE times in the shipped command surface that `test_command_size_budget` (per-command `measured * 1.02`) and `test_aggregate_shipped_surface_does_not_grow` (`now <= was`, zero headroom) both police. Measured on PLAN-second-opinion-acceptance-gate: +12,333 chars in the stage produced **+61,665 aggregate** against ~9,575 of banked headroom — a feature that "adds two paragraphs" can miss a budget by 6x. **A stage is the most expensive place in this repo to put procedure; skills and agent bodies are the sanctioned escape** — a skill is loaded on demand and is NOT in the frozen command surface (`_surface_baseline.render_surface()` collects `.claude/commands/hm/*.md` + `.agents/skills/hm-*`, never `.claude/skills/*`), so moving prose there costs zero against both arms. Placement rule before writing stage prose: rationale goes to the PLAN/ADR, an agent's own contract goes to the agent body, main-loop procedure goes to a skill with a one-line pointer, and only "the main loop must do X right here" earns a place in the stage. Two corollaries learned the hard way: (1) the pointer is load-bearing — a rule in an unreferenced skill is dead prose, so assert BOTH the rule (in the skill) and the pointer (in the stage), and decide per pointer whether it is guarded (a second-opinion-only rule) or unguarded (a rule that also governs Claude findings); (2) a skill referenced from an UNGUARDED line must be in the preset's `enabled` list, not merely `installed`, or the reference dangles in that preset — Side omitted it and the round-state contract would never have loaded there.
## [wiki:architecture] autopilot-authorization-vs-entry | 2026-08-01
Autopilot announced the next stage and never ran it, and the ledger reported that as success. Four defects compounded, and the ledger one is why it survived: `_cmd_boundary` appended `advanced` BEFORE the model acted, so permission was recorded as progress — the step cap counted authorizations, and "announced but did nothing" was indistinguishable from "advanced" in every row ever written. The vocabulary is now split: `advance_authorized` (the boundary grants it) vs `advance_entered` (the next stage's own boundary/gate-blocked call retro-confirms it, via `find_unconfirmed_authorization`'s greedy in-APPEND-order pairing — file order is write order, so timestamps are used only for the window filter and `elapsed_s`, never for ordering). `count_entries` is the cap numerator and counts legacy `advanced` rows only while they PRECEDE the first new-vocabulary row, which is exactly the upgrade point inside a mixed 18h window; dropping them wholesale would hand a partially-consumed session a fresh budget. A stage that dies mid-body reaches neither call and is correctly never confirmed — that silence is the signal the old ledger could not produce. The second defect was the picker: its arm condition had only `on`/`off` behind it, so the model decided "already armed" from whether the marker FILE existed, and nothing collected a stale marker — hence `hm autopilot status` (deterministic JSON with a load-bearing `reason`, plus TTL-only GC that deletes `stale`/`unparseable` but never `future`, which may be a peer's clock-skewed live marker). Third: no slug rode to the next stage, so argument-parsing stages started blank; the marker now carries `task_slug` + `task_slug_stage` and the boundary reports `task_slug_source` so an inherited slug is announced rather than silent. Fourth: four stage bodies carried an unconditional "Stage terminal ... STOP" that a model resolving the conflict conservatively obeyed over the later auto-advance block — both sides now name each other. Session identity is the sharp edge: `session_uuid` is PROJECT-scoped, so within one project every session read every other session's marker as its own; ownership now keys on `HM_SESSION_ID` one-directionally (ids compared whenever EITHER side has one), and `autopilot_autoarm` must thread the id from its SessionStart stdin because its sibling `sessionid_envfile` publishes to `$CLAUDE_ENV_FILE`, which reaches only LATER Bash, never a sibling hook's own process. Landed with three known-open findings (REVIEW round 7 F31/F33/F34); ADR-007 (session-scoping) and ADR-010 (the ownership guard) generated nearly every follow-on defect across seven review rounds and are candidates for reduction.
## [wiki:pattern] import-root-from-project-not-init-py | 2026-08-01
An import-graph test selector must derive its import root from the PROJECT LAYOUT (src-layout vs flat vs a `lib/` source dir), never from `__init__.py` presence. Walking up while a directory contains `__init__.py` is the textbook rule for a regular package and it is silently wrong for a namespace package in every layout — the walk stops one level too low, the derived qualified name is short by a segment, and every comparison against a fully-qualified import target misses. The failure is invisible because a missed match degrades to "no importers found", which is the same observable as "this module genuinely has no importers", and the conservative fallback (run the full suite) then reports green. `test_dep_map` hit exactly this while replacing substring import matching with qualified dotted-name resolution: `from harness_maker import autopilot_ledger` (121 such lines under `tests/`) resolved to zero importers, so the "run only affected tests" optimisation had been dead for every alias-only module while looking merely conservative. The resolution carries three distinct roots that must not be conflated — `package_root` (for the changed module's own qualified name), `search_root` (`package_root.parent`, the disk probe anchor that decides whether `from P import n` names a module or a symbol), and `importer_pkg` (the importing file's own package, needed to resolve `node.level` relative imports). A draft that probed under `package_root` instead of `search_root` expanded to `src/harness_maker/harness_maker/...`, never existed, and would have degraded every `from P import n` back to a bare `P` edge with no error anywhere. Corollaries that generalise: derive the root from a project-level signal and state it, treat a zero-result graph query as suspicious rather than as an answer, and give any selector a fail-safe that over-selects (a wrong root must yield FEWER dependents and therefore a fuller run, never a missed one).
## [wiki:architecture] review-autofix-batch-trigger | 2026-08-01
`/hm:review`'s Auto-Fix Loop patched one finding at a time and never re-derived the state model behind them, so each round's fixes reproduced defects at roughly a 1:1 rate and one prior task ran to 6 rounds (9 of 30 findings were defects in a previous round's own fix). Two measures now ship. **Measure A — two-arm batch trigger:** before editing, re-derive the underlying model when **either** (a) >=2 of this round's findings share a subsystem / state model, **or** (b) any finding **new this round** carries a non-null `caused_by`; otherwise the lone unattributed finding keeps the fast path, which is the majority case and must not get slower. On a fire the round emits a per-group block (`group_key` with its derived prefix recorded alongside, `covered_finding_ids`, the model's dimensions, one consolidated edit). Two ordering facts are load-bearing, not stylistic: `caused_by` must be determined **before** the trigger is evaluated (computed at record-append time it arrives after fix selection, so a lone regression finding takes the fast path and arm (b) is unreachable), and arm (b)'s domain is findings **new this round**, not the merged voter state (over the merge it would be true from round 3 onward). `caused_by` is stamped once at a finding's first appearance and never recomputed, which is well-defined across a `stale`-then-re-report because `codex_adapter.finding_id` hashes `[source, file, line, message]`: a re-report at a shifted location is a different `id` (genuinely new — arm (b) may fire), one at an unchanged location keeps its `id` (nothing regressed). It also needs one literal grammar, because it shares the iteration record's `Status` cell with a different enum: `Applied · caused_by=#7` / `· caused_by=none` / `· caused_by=unknown`, with the same suffix on `Skipped — overlap` and `Reverted — build failure`. **Measure C — three reporting-only counters plus a `terminal` discriminator** on `ReviewTelemetryRecord`: `unreviewed_fix_count` (fixes applied in the terminal round, which the loop never re-reviewed), `regression_attributed_n`, `attribution_unknown_n`, counted over **distinct finding `id`s** rather than iteration-record rows (a reverted or overlap-skipped fix leaves its finding `pending` and emits another row next round carrying the same `caused_by`). All four are typed `| None` so "never measured" stays distinguishable from "measured zero" — a `0` default would make an un-upgraded harness indistinguishable from a clean run. They gate nothing; they exist so an `A` grade is not read as "settled". **Ownership:** the normative rules live in the `second-opinion-gate` SKILL §5 — the single normative owner — and `review.md.j2` carries only an unconditional load imperative (it renders even under `second_opinion.models: []`) plus the mechanical surface. Under the aggregate + atomic size ratchets the stage's prose was cut four times across two rounds; every cut removed a rationale sentence that the pinned round order and the tests already enforce, never a rule, and the atomic `review` budget was re-frozen 29235 -> 29848 with a written justification because the stage's rendered size is config-dependent.
## [wiki:architecture] hm-session-id-unexported-shell-var | 2026-08-02
`hooks/sessionid_envfile.py` writes `HM_SESSION_ID=<v>` into `$CLAUDE_ENV_FILE` with **no `export`**. Claude Code sources that file into the Bash-tool shell, so the value is a **shell variable, never an environment variable**. Consequence, on every platform (this was never a WSL2 quirk): `echo "$HM_SESSION_ID"` in slash-command Bash **works**, and `os.environ.get("HM_SESSION_ID")` is `None` in **every** subprocess the shell spawns — `uv run`, `python -m`, `hm ...`, pytest. The two consumer classes therefore diverge and must be reasoned about separately: a rendered command that *interpolates* `"$HM_SESSION_ID"` into an argv is correct and needs no change; any Python that *reads the env* is dead code. Fix pattern: the only context that can see the id is the slash-command shell, so it must **hand the value down as an explicit CLI argument** (`--session-id` / `--claude-session-id`), keeping the `os.environ` read only as a fallback for a host that does export it. On a diagnostic/gate surface make the argument **tri-state, and keep it so**: `None` = never wired (a stale render — emit a separate self-accusing signal rather than going quiet), `""` = wired but genuinely absent (Cursor/Codex, real hook failure — this is the degraded case worth hard-gating), non-empty = healthy. Collapsing `None` and `""` is what makes the un-wired case indistinguishable from the degraded one and re-hides the bug. **Adding a new consumer means wiring the argument** — the env read will silently never fire, and nothing will tell you. See [[fail:design runtime-env-gate-dead-on-arrival]] (count:2 — the same variable, the inverse symptom) and `PLAN-sessionid-env-propagation` ADR-001.
## [wiki:pattern] writer-without-readers-turns-off | 2026-08-02
When ownership is decided by a predicate that compares ids **whenever either side has one** (`autopilot._is_own`: `env_id is None and marker_id is not None` → foreign), stamping an id in the **writer** while any **reader** still resolves id-less does not half-enable the feature — it turns the feature **off**. The id-bearing marker is foreign to every un-wired reader, so `active_marker` returns `None` everywhere and the whole subsystem silently no-ops. This makes such a change **atomic by nature**: writer and all readers must land in one commit, and a phase plan that lists the writer plus "some" readers is not a smaller safe increment, it is a strictly worse state than not starting. Practical rule: before wiring the producer of an ownership/identity field, `grep` the comparison helper's **call sites**, not the field's — the readers that break are the ones that call `_is_own`/`active_marker`, and they will not mention the field's name anywhere. In `PLAN-sessionid-env-propagation` five such readers were missed on the first cut and caught only by review: `autopilot.touch`, `autopilot.set_task_slug`, `autopilot.effective_level`, `autopilot_caps.evaluate_boundary`, and the Typer `hm autopilot` surface in `cli.py` — that last one because only the sibling `argparse` `python -m` entry point had been wired, so the *same* function was reachable by one wired path and one dead one. Two entry points onto one function need the same audit as two readers. Gate it with a pure-function ownership unit test (foreign marker → not own) plus a render-grep over every rendered call site. Cross-ref [[fail:design new-marker-content-field-must-update-every-reader]] (count:2), [[autopilot-authorization-vs-entry]].
## [wiki:architecture] harness-diet-fused-axis-removal | 2026-08-05
Phase 1 of the harness diet removed the fused-workflow axis entirely: `workflow_fuse.py`, `validators.py`, four Jinja templates, the `workflows`/`default_workflow` schema on both `HarnessConfig` and `InterviewAnswers`, `STAGE_ABBREV`/`auto_workflow_name`/`WorkflowDef`, and the `workflow_context` conditionals in all seven stage templates plus three shared partials. Shipped prompt surface went **1,173,667 → 641,452 characters (−45.3%)**, matching the PLAN's predicted −45.2%. The evidence that justified it was measurement, not taste: the five rendered fused commands were 58.6% of the Claude command surface and had **zero** recorded invocations across the project's full `harness_maker.economics` history, while autopilot had already performed 40 stage advances. `/hm:loop` was structurally coupled to the deleted axis — its per-iteration unit WAS a fused command name, with a file-existence check and a halt path — so ADR-014 rewired it to `--per-iter-stages`, a list of atomic stage names that rejects `wrapup` because loop-close owns it. The framing that made each cut decidable: **keep state scaffolding, cut behavior scaffolding.** Anthropic's Opus 5 prompting guide says to delete instructions the model already follows ("the same applies to legacy harness scaffolding that adds separate verification steps"), while its long-running-agents guidance argues for *more* structure; the two reconcile along exactly that axis. Applying it consistently reversed one of this task's own decisions: `/hm:verify` was nominated for deletion on cost (0.4% of measured spend) and kept once its template turned out to be five deterministic machine gates reading `dashboard.md`, `findings-*.jsonl` and git state — not a request that the model re-check its own reasoning. Deferred to later phases: the autonomy default flip, the migration loader, command `description:` frontmatter, and the failures archive.
## [wiki:architecture] harness-diet-phases-2-6 | 2026-08-05
PLAN-harness-diet Phases 2-6 (0.47.0), the follow-on to Phase 1's deletion of the fused-workflow axis. Four independent changes plus a re-freeze. (1) **Retired keys are stripped at LOAD, not at render** — ADR-012 originally put the `workflows`/`default_workflow` drop-list in the renderer, which only runs on `/harness-maker:make`, so a project that upgraded the package without re-rendering never reached it; `io_utils.load_harness_yaml` (the loader every config entry point already shares) now strips `RETIRED_TOP_LEVEL_KEYS` and logs one advisory per project, and `schema_version` moved 3 -> 4 to record when a file was written while the migration itself keys on key PRESENCE so a file left at 3 still migrates. The generalisable rule: a migration that lives in the writer only reaches users who write; put it in the reader if you want it to reach users who merely load. (2) **Autopilot is the class default** — `AutonomyConfig` moves to `level: auto_safe` / `autopilot_persistent: true`, with SEVEN sites pinned conservative (`_parse_autonomy`'s absent and malformed branches, the interview's explicit decline, `cli._build_autonomy_override`'s absent base, the non-bool `autopilot_persistent` guard, the `--preset` switch, and the `--reinterview` rebuild). It reaches NEW INSTALLS ONLY: every `harness.yaml` any previous version rendered already contains all six `autonomy:` fields explicitly and `answers_from_harness_yaml` round-trips them, so the template's `else "auto_safe"` arm is unreachable and even `--update` re-emits your existing `level`. Adopting it on an existing project means editing `.claude/harness.yaml` or running `harness-maker make . --update --autonomy-level auto_safe --autonomy-persistent`. (3) Every rendered `/hm:` command carries a frontmatter `description:` (ADR-016) — without one both Claude Code and Cursor fall back to the first body line and 14 of 15 commands showed the identical string in the tool listing; the per-target question was answered by rendering all three targets rather than assumed (commands render to `.claude/commands/hm/*.md` only, never to Codex TOML or Cursor `.mdc`, so the field is unconditional). (4) `upsert-failure` archives at WRITE time: `count:1` AND older than 90 days moves to `.claude/memory/archive/failures-<YYYY>.md`, `count>=2` exempt at any age because recurrence, not age, is what makes an entry worth keeping. The pass runs inside the same lock and transaction as the write, so the growth point and the eviction point are the same call; an archive failure skips the eviction entirely rather than half-applying it, and never fails the write. `worktree._HUMAN_MEMORY_TIER_PATHSPEC` gained `.claude/memory/archive` in the same phase — see `[fail:design] wrapup-memory-base-seam` for why that was not optional.
## [wiki:convention] partial-config-block-is-stated-intent | 2026-08-05
When a class default is PROMOTED to a less conservative value (here `AutonomyConfig.level` gated -> auto_safe, `autopilot_persistent` false -> true), the question "what does a config block that OMITS this field mean?" changes answer, and the safe answer is not the class default. The criterion that settled it in PLAN-harness-diet Phase 3: **a present-but-partial block is the user's stated intent, so an omitted field falls back to the CONSERVATIVE value, not the newly promoted class default.** Concretely, `autonomy: {autopilot_persistent: false}` — a user who wrote a block specifically to hold autonomy down — silently became `level: auto_safe` under naive class-default inheritance, because the parser only pinned the ABSENT-block and MALFORMED-block branches. Absent block means "no opinion, take the ship default"; present block means "I have configured this feature", and inheriting a promoted default into the fields they did not enumerate escalates them past what they wrote. This overturned a recorded ADR of the same PLAN on new evidence from two cross-model reviewers, which is the right reason to reverse a decision: the ADR had reasoned about the absent-block case and generalised it to the partial-block case without checking that the two carry different user intent. Two adjacent traps in the same enumeration, both worth checking whenever a default flips: (a) a guard that DELETES a bad value so it falls through to the then-safe class default inverts into silent auto-arming the moment that default is promoted — pin the conservative value explicitly rather than relying on fall-through; (b) any rebuild path that reconstructs the answer set from scratch (`--preset`, `--reinterview`) destroys an explicit opt-out unless it carries the field forward. General rule: a default flip is not a one-line change — enumerate every bare construction of the type and every path that reconstructs it, and for each one decide whether it represents "no opinion" or "stated intent".
## [wiki:architecture] workflow-loop-efficiency-stage-1 | 2026-08-05
Stage 1 of the workflow-efficiency work: cut the one measured-low-yield review step, instrument the three that had no data, and gate every fix on a reproduction. What it actually changed — Pass 1.5's `code-verifier` dispatch is gone from `/hm:review` (Pass 2 now consumes the raw Pass 1 list) and the cross-model voters launch concurrently with Pass 1, taking a review round from five serial barriers to three; `/hm:execute` gained a Phase D.5 repair guard against `fix-introduced-defect-passes-all-gates` (count:4); a new `stage_agent_ledger` records every `plan-validator` and Phase A.5 `test-reviewer` dispatch, which previously had ZERO rows despite 76 lifetime dispatches, plus per-round reviewer payloads so a replay corpus exists for the first time; `delegation_ledger` mismatch rows now carry a diagnosis. The measurement to carry forward is uncomfortable and is written up in `BASELINE-DELTA-P7.md` section 1: a cost-REDUCTION plan raised the shipped surface by 7113 chars, because detection and instrumentation are both prose. That only pays back if stage 2 reads the ledgers and deletes something; if stage 2 never runs, the net effect is an increase. Two decisions worth remembering rather than re-deriving. First, three acceptance criteria could not be closed and were recorded rather than forced green — AC-005 (the ablation was pre-registered but not run), AC-006 (waived by ADR-005 because the reproduction gate never opened), and ADR-003's operative-force requirement, which was ultimately routed to a `judgment` AC after two mechanical mutation controls turned out circular; a literal grep cannot evaluate whether "STOP, do not advance" differs from "consider pausing", so the oracle is an independent `judgment-reviewer` against the `repair_guard_force` rubric with a subject hash that invalidates the pass on edit. Second, the `.gitignore` under `work-docs/` is an ALLOWLIST of prefixes — `ABLATION-*` and `MATRIX-*` were on neither it nor `wrapup_land`'s staging manifest, so two phases' entire deliverables were invisible to `git status`; the gitignore half is fixed, the manifest half is not, so a new document type still needs an explicit `--optional` at wrapup.
## [wiki:architecture] single-reader-single-writer-config-axis | 2026-08-06
A config axis is only as real as its reader and its writer, and the safe number of each is **one**. harness-maker's `worktree:` block carried four keys and exactly one had runtime effect: `feature_branch_workflow` was live, `enabled` existed in the in-memory answers dict but was never rendered and never read, and `scope` / `branch_prefix` were emitted by the **preset template as hardcoded literals** rather than from the config object — so a hand-edit to either was silently reverted on every `/harness-maker:make --update`, and a `Side` harness answering `{'enabled': False}` still rendered `scope: [execute]` and turned execute isolation ON. Four user-visible symptoms, one root cause: the block was written by the template, not by the config. The collapse to a single `worktree.enabled` boolean is the visible half; the durable half is the shape enforced around it. **One reader**: `worktree.worktree_enabled(base)`, a 3-generation fallback chain (`enabled` → legacy `feature_branch_workflow` → legacy `scope` contains `execute` → absent = False + one warning) that is first-key-present-wins and **fail-closed on present-but-malformed** — terminating the chain matters, because falling through from a malformed `enabled: "false"` to a stale `feature_branch_workflow: true` sitting beside it would turn isolation *on* against the user's visible intent. **One writer**: `cli._apply_worktree_enabled`, the single choke point through which all five producers (preset flip, `--worktree`/`--no-worktree`, interview answer, `/hm:configure`, migration) converge; the `--reinterview` disk re-apply that used to clobber both the flag and the fresh interview answer was deleted rather than ordered, because ordering two writers is a fix that decays. Both invariants are pinned **structurally**, not by behavior tests: `tests/unit/test_worktree_reader_singleton.py` fails when a second reader appears, and `tests/render/test_worktree_surface_gating.py` fails when the surface stops tracking the flag. The reason to spend a structural test here rather than a case test is the failure mode: a second reader does not crash, it disagrees — `/hm:health` reports one mode while the execution path takes the other, and no single test run can see both halves at once. Generalises to any config key with more than one consumer: name the one function that resolves it and the one function that sets it, make every other call site delegate, and let a test forbid the second of either. Cross-ref: `[fail:design] template-literal-shadows-config-key`, `[fail:design] retired-key-still-read-by-runtime-gate`.
## [wiki:architecture] onboarding-disclosure-and-six-gates | 2026-08-06
The fresh-install fast path set 10 of 14 config axes silently and showed 5, and nothing in the codebase detected installed tooling — so "codex is installed, want a second-opinion vote?" was impossible to ask, not merely unwired. What shipped: `harness-maker detect-tools --json` (uncached on purpose — `ProjectProfile` is served from a 24h cache invalidated by project-manifest mtime, and installing a CLI touches no manifest, so a cached answer would report a tool installed minutes ago as absent); a "Set for you — not asked on this path" disclosure table on the make summary; exactly one conditional second-opinion question, asked only when a CLI is detected; `/hm:configure` entries for `second_opinion` / `autonomy` / `locale`, which is the first way an axis silently disabled at install can be turned on without hand-editing `harness.yaml`; a `/hm:health` advisory for the installed-but-disabled state, which was silent for that feature's entire lifetime because the smoke was gated on the axis already being ON; and the removal of `consensus` / `caching` from the interview (no Python and no stage template branches on either — the fields and preset defaults stay, so no migration). The durable lesson is not the feature. **Editing one rendered template trips SIX gates in this repo**, not the one the PLAN anticipated: the aggregate surface ratchet (`tests/structural/surface_baseline.json`), the baseline-delta attribution document (`work-docs/BASELINE-DELTA-P7.md`, whose gate requires every changed baseline key to have an attribution row AND the document's aggregate to MATCH the baseline), the command-surface registry, the synthesize snapshots (8 preset×dev_mode `.expected.yaml` files), the round-trip budget (`tests/structural/test_roundtrip_budget.py`), and the render fixtures. Four of the six were discovered by tripping them mid-execution; the PLAN's ADR-005 said "four coupled fields" and the real number was five artifacts before the sixth appeared. Two consequences worth carrying: a template edit is never a one-file change, so size the phase by the gate set rather than by the diff; and the ratchet has zero headroom by design, so growth must be paid for by an equivalent offset elsewhere and never by raising the frozen baseline (`assert_sha_is_durable` refuses to regenerate from a task branch, which is why `render_sha` legitimately names the previous freeze point on a task-branch commit — an accepted, recorded artefact rather than a defect).
## [wiki:convention] wrong-transparency-table-worse-than-none | 2026-08-06
A transparency table that is WRONG is strictly worse than no table: it converts "the user does not know" into "the user has been told the opposite", and it does so with the authority of a disclosure. This work added a "Set for you — not asked on this path" table to `/harness-maker:make` precisely to remove silent defaults, and the table's highest-consequence row was false — it stated `autonomy.level: gated` (off) while a fresh install renders `auto_safe` with `autopilot_persistent: true`, re-armed by a SessionStart hook every session. A user on the default path was told auto-advance was off while the harness shipped it on. Measured directly by rendering a real fresh install, not argued from the template. Two reviewers (code-reviewer and security-reviewer) caught it independently on the same line; the structural gate written in the same phase to protect the table did not, because `test_the_disclosure_table_includes_the_axes_dispatch_argv_cannot_carry` asserted `re.search(r"autonomy|autopilot", summary)` — **presence, not truth**, which is satisfied by any content including the inverted one. The remedy that works is to bind the assertion to an INDEPENDENT source of the value: the replacement arm reads `AutonomyConfig()` and asserts the row states the default the class actually carries, verified to fail on a revert. The general rule for any document that discloses configuration: a claim about a default must be tested against the object that produces the default, never against the prose that describes it, and the first question about a new disclosure gate is "would this still pass if the disclosed value were inverted?". Cross-ref: `[fail:test] assertion-invariant-over-named-dimension`, `[fail:design] fix-introduces-the-defect-class-it-closes`.
## [wiki:architecture] stage-agent-ledger-first-findings | 2026-08-07
The `stage-agents.jsonl` ledger that PLAN-workflow-loop-efficiency shipped found three defects in its own first six rows, one of them in its own pre-registration — which is the strongest evidence so far that instrumenting the unmeasured steps was the right stage-1 call. What the first rows say, and what they do not. `P(verdict changes | a later validator pass ran)` is **0/3**: across two PLANs the validator returned MAJOR_REVISION on pass 1 and returned it again on every later pass, and those later passes account for **57% of all validator wall-clock** (880s of 1540s). That is the first real number behind the user's original complaint that plan→validator repetition eats time. It is NOT yet a decision to cut the second pass, for a reason worth keeping in view: the counter-example — this very PLAN's own second validator pass catching two P0s — predates the ledger, so the 0/3 sample structurally EXCLUDES the case against deletion. Read the ratio knowing its population. Three fixes landed on the instrument itself. The pre-registered filter was amended from `pass_or_attempt == 2` to `>= 2` after a third pass appeared, with the amendment recorded beside the rule (direction conservative, timing disclosed, conclusion unchanged) — see `[fail:design] metric-filter-assumes-unenforced-cap`. `check_run_coherence()` was added because the defect it catches — two `terminal` rows in one run — is a relationship BETWEEN rows and therefore invisible to a row-level pydantic validator no matter how tight; cross-row invariants belong at the read boundary. And the plan template now states the 2-pass cap it previously only implied through a `--pass <1|2>` placeholder, requiring an over-cap pass to be recorded WITH a reason rather than dropped, because the ledger otherwise cannot separate "the operator asked for another pass" from "the stage overran its own limit" — opposite remedies. One meta-observation: the attribution gate strengthened the previous day caught its own scoping bug on the first subsequent task, and both that fix and this one worked for the same reason — the check reads the artifact (the baseline JSON, the ledger rows) instead of trusting prose about it.
## [wiki:architecture] per-session-marker-scoping | 2026-08-07
A per-session marker file (`.claude/.hm-autopilot-<id>`, `.claude/.hm-task-<worktree>`) is a **claim record, not an access-control list** — and PLAN-multisession-marker-scoping shipped at grade B precisely because the two were conflated. "Who claimed this worktree" and "who is actually working here" are different questions, and **no rule about mutating the marker reconciles them**. Takeover-on-claim (ADR-013) answers the first: the last writer of the header owns the record. Enforcement (`worktree_gate`, ADR-004) needs the second, and reads the record as if it were the answer. So a peer's `task-preflight` rewriting the header is, to the gate, indistinguishable from the live owner having left — and the live owner's next `Write` inside its **own** worktree exits 2 with a remedy that tells it to re-run preflight, which seizes the marker back. Flip-flop, not resolution (REVIEW CR-2, consensus-passed P1, still open).

The reason no marker-mutation rule fixes this is structural: **the system has no liveness signal that outlives its own CLI.** The session registry `.claude/.hm-sessions.json` records `pid=os.getpid()`, which is the `uv run` subprocess that has already exited by the time anyone asks — so `reclaim_stale` finds every foreign row dead, `_foreign_live_rows` is permanently empty, and `SharedSlugError` never fires. The one guard that was supposed to bound takeover is thus unbounded in production, and it fails *silently*: nothing anywhere reports "I evicted a live peer". The task marker also carries no timestamp, so expiry is not available as a fallback — recovery had to be takeover, which is exactly the mechanism that has no bound.

Two further constraints shape any fix here, and both are easy to re-derive wrongly:
- **The gate's cwd is the base repo, not the worktree** — the `pf_tail.md.j2` contract. A gate that roots at `cwd` finds no `.claude/` markers at all and then enforces *nothing*, silently and greenly. That is why `worktree_gate` resolves the base root first via a local `_strip_worktree` (kept local to avoid a pydantic + 5k-line `worktree` import on every Write/Edit), pinned against drift by `tests/structural/test_gate_base_root_parity.py`.
- **Unattributable markers (empty header) must be ignored entirely** — a third bucket beside mine/peer. Only `loop.md.j2` passes `--claude-session-id`, so a standalone `/hm:execute` worktree's marker has an empty header; a two-way classification blocks those sessions from their own worktrees.

A real liveness signal (a long-lived pid, a heartbeat file, or an OS-level lock held for the session's duration) is the missing primitive. Until one exists, treat marker ownership as advisory and keep the gate **fail-open** (ADR-006: no `session_id` in the PreToolUse payload → allow before reading any marker) — the enforcement here was prompt-level before this work, so a soft floor is a gain, whereas a hard gate on an unreliable record locks users out of their own work.
## [wiki:tooling] mechanical-guards-from-backlog | 2026-08-07
Four backlog proposals became mechanical guards, and `mutation_receipt` finally got the consumer that makes it bite. **G1** (`test_no_golden_bakes_a_machine_path.py`) rejects a machine path in any committed golden — and, after the first cut proved largely vacuous, also the `$HOME/…/.worktrees/<slug>` form: `synthesize._portablize_ref` rewrites the render machine's home to a literal `$HOME` *before* the golden is written, so every discriminator except the `.worktrees` segment is normalised away by the transform that makes the path look portable. The two rules do not cover each other; the `.worktrees` rule is anchored at `$HOME` because the rendered command bodies say `.worktrees/<slug>` in prose ~25 times. **G3** (`test_no_dead_string_pins.py`) bans a leading step ordinal in a test literal. **G4** (`test_cli_surfaces_are_driven.py`) requires every `hm` subcommand to be executed somewhere in its shipped spelling, detected by AST over six executing shapes — and its allowlist is now re-checked by an over-generous detector rather than trusted, because three rounds of hand-written reasons produced three rounds of false ones. **The consumer** (`test_new_gates_file_a_mutation_receipt.py`) fails when a new `tests/structural/` gate lands with no ledger row; pre-existing gates sit on a shrink-only debt list.

Three things worth carrying forward. **A guard's population must come from a glob, not `git ls-files`** — this task's own three gates were untracked while being written, so a tracked-only population would have passed over exactly the files the guard exists for. **A receipt earned by a collection error is worthless**: any line kills a module that way, so only an assertion failure counts as proof; two candidate lines were rejected on that basis. And **one gate here cannot be killed at all** — the repo-scan test in G1 gets *greener* when its matcher is weakened, so its teeth live entirely in the demonstration cases, which is why a receipt filed against the scan test was false the moment it was written.

The whole round is also the clearest available evidence for a claim that keeps proving true: building a check confers no immunity. Every guard here shipped carrying a defect of the class it was built to catch, on a fully green suite, and in every instance a reviewer or an executable probe found it — never the suite.
## [wiki:architecture] wrapup-land-stages-the-worktree | 2026-08-07
`wrapup_land` now stages the task worktree itself. Its `--required`/`--optional` manifest names DELIVERABLES (PLAN, REVIEW, SPEC, memory) and never named `src/**`, because it was written for the ephemeral-worktree model where `/hm:execute` Step 5's `worktree finalize <WT> stage-only` had already filled the index. **The per-task feature-branch model has no finalize** — `stage-only` must NOT run there, since it merges into base and collides with `task-land`, which owns the merge — so nothing staged the code and the composite committed the paperwork while returning `ok: true` and `commit.status: created`.

The fix is `stage_isolated_worktree`, and two properties of it are load-bearing. It runs **after** the manifest, because `git add -A` cannot notice that a REQUIRED deliverable is absent; running it first would silently dissolve the typed manifest's whole purpose. And it is gated on **`worktree != base`**: with isolation OFF those are the same directory — a shared branch that may carry unrelated in-flight work — and sweeping there reproduces the contamination class this repo keeps a five-layer defence against. Inside a task worktree the opposite holds: the checkout exists to hold exactly one task, concurrent sessions each get their own, and harness churn is gitignored, so everything present IS the work being wrapped up. `--manifest-only` restores the old behaviour.

Two things worth carrying beyond this fix. First, the receipt's `index_after` is the only field that distinguishes "committed the work" from "committed the paperwork" — every exit code and every printed status was success in both incidents. Second, the upstream mismatch is still live: the rendered `/hm:execute` opens with the per-task `task-preflight` and then Step 5 still instructs the legacy `finalize <WT> stage-only`. Running it on a task worktree does a merge that `task-land` owns; not running it is what leaves the code unstaged. This fix removes the cost of the second branch, not the mismatch itself.
## [wiki:architecture] execute-step5-scoped-to-its-model | 2026-08-07
`/hm:execute` Step 5's `worktree finalize <WT> stage-only` is now scoped to the model it belongs to. Both worktree models render under the same `wt_on` flag, so the stage opened with the per-task `task-preflight` and then instructed a LEGACY-model merge into base — on a worktree `task-land` owns — citing a "Step 0 `worktree create`" the rendered document no longer contains. The finalize is CORRECT under `/hm:loop`, whose `<WT>` is an `execute-<uuid>` and which stages each iteration back to base, so the separation cannot be made at render time. **Step 5 now opens with a runtime read of `git -C <WT> rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` and skips itself on `hm/*`** — the same discriminator `/hm:wrapup` Step 7.7 uses, and the mirror of the loop's existing "SKIP wrapup's Step 7.7" override.

Writing that up exposed a second `<WT>`. Under loop dispatch the driver reads each stage file inline and runs *every* step, Step 0 included, and `task-preflight` **creates** `.worktrees/<slug>/` when absent and declares that path `<WT>` too. Two definitions in one iteration resolve either way: follow the loop's and the task worktree is an orphan (friction, mostly reaped by `prune_stale`); follow the stage's and the iteration's work lands on `hm/<slug>` while loop-close finalizes the **empty** ephemeral worktree — stranded and invisible to convergence, with every exit code 0. The loop already carried this override for wrapup; the per-iter stages now get it too. No incident was observed — `hm/*` branches and `.worktrees/` were checked and held only the current task — so the possibility is established and the frequency is unknown.

Two things generalise. **The per-task model was added without auditing the model-specific instructions on either side of a handoff** — this is `[fail:design] handoff-assumes-a-skipped-step` at count:3, and its three instances are one root cause: `wrapup_land` assumed a finalize that must not run, `execute.md` instructed the finalize that must not run, and the loop let a preflight run that must not. **And the audit has to cover instructions a stage ISSUES, not only assumptions it MAKES** — the entry's own prevention line said "audit every 'X was already done' assumption", which by construction could not have caught instances 2 or 3. The escalation proposal for a mechanical version is in `pending-proposals.md`, with the false-positive caveat that killed a similar render-grep rule.
## [wiki:gotcha] project-dir-encoding-hid-a-whole-project | 2026-08-08
PLAN-workflow-time-token-savings Phase A1. `economics_source.encode_project_dir` mirrors how Claude Code names transcript directories from the launch cwd, and its regex was `[/.]` when the real encoding also folds `_`. The failure was **silent and total**: any project whose path contains an underscore matched no directory on disk, so `load_turns` returned zero turns and every economics report printed `$0` — indistinguishable from a project with no spend. `/home/noel/strange_chess` hid **$1,637** of real spend for the lifetime of the meter, and the RESEARCH document that measured $11,022 across four projects was reading three. The fix is one character (`re.sub(r"[/._]", "-", ...)`, `economics_source.py:125`), verified against five underscore-path projects, all hyphen-encoded on disk with no underscore-encoded sibling.

**The widening enlarges the collision set, and the guard against that is conditional — this is the part to remember.** `/x/a_b` and `/x/a-b` now encode identically, so a foreign project's transcript directory becomes discoverable. The per-turn filter `is_own_cwd` (`economics_source.py:160`) drops foreign turns, **but only those that carry a `cwd`**: it returns `True` for `cwd is None` by design, to keep older transcript lines. So a collision plus one legacy `cwd`-less line does admit a foreign turn. That limit is stated rather than closed, because making `is_own_cwd` fail-closed would drop legacy no-cwd turns in `load_turns` **and** `context_composition` — a strictly larger change than the meter fix. `test_cwd_filter_is_the_real_boundary_when_two_paths_encode_alike` pins both branches and will fail loudly if anyone flips the default. The first draft of that docstring asserted the boundary was unconditional; it was refuted in review and is now the entry's cautionary half.

**Two consequences for anyone reading economics output.** (1) Numbers produced before 2026-08-08 for an underscore-path project are zeroes, not measurements — re-run rather than compare.
