(2) The diagnostic `hm stage_agent_ledger reconcile --root <p>` exists to make ledger-vs-transcript agreement re-derivable instead of narrated; it is **diagnostic-only**, and its non-zero exit must never be wired into a gate (see [[stage-agent-ledger-reconciled-against-transcripts]] for the reconciliation table and the `ledger-trustworthy: yes` verdict).
## [wiki:architecture] antigravity-structured-output-and-tier | 2026-08-08
`agy` (Antigravity CLI) **does** have a structured-output mode — `--output-format json --json-schema <abs path>`, where `--json-schema` is only honoured when `--output-format json` is also passed. Six sites in this repo asserted the opposite ("antigravity has no CLI-level enforcement") purely because the flag is not spelled `--output-schema` the way Codex spells it; that one-word difference is what produced 9 parse-failure rows in `.claude/observability/second-opinion.jsonl`. Enforcement is **best-effort, not guaranteed**: a reply with `status: SUCCESS` was observed with the `structured_output` key entirely absent, so the tolerant fence-stripping extractor stays a **mandatory fallback, not dead code**. Payload acquisition is therefore a 6-way table, not a happy path: stdout unparseable as JSON → `failed`; envelope `status` not `SUCCESS` → `skipped`; `structured_output` present and schema-valid → used directly; present but invalid → **fail closed** (`failed`, with no fall-through to the extractor, so a malformed enforced payload can never be silently re-parsed into a different answer); absent → run the extractor over the `response` string; `response` empty or not a `str` → `failed`. Separately, the shipped default model moved from `Gemini 3.1 Pro (High)` to `Gemini 3.6 Flash (High)`: on one measured 41 KB review prompt the old tier ran 4 m 04 s and returned zero bytes against the 240 s cap, while the new tier returns 3–4 findings in 27–28 s — the model tier, not the timeout value, was the binding constraint. **A residual remains and is NOT fixed**: agy intermittently returns `status: SUCCESS` in ~7 s with an empty `response` and no `structured_output` (3 of 7 large-prompt calls). This change does not stop that; it only makes it legible as a named `failed` reason instead of an anonymous parser complaint.
## [wiki:observability] stage-agent-ledger-reconciled-against-transcripts (2026-08-08)

PLAN-workflow-time-token-savings Phase A2. Two apparent ledger anomalies; both resolved, and one
of them was a **measurement error in the research that raised it**.

Reconciliation against `load_turns`, all-time window, after the Phase A1 encoder fix, as
printed by `hm stage_agent_ledger reconcile --root <p>`:

| project | preset | ledger dispatches | subagent turns | sidechain turn-groups | agrees |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| strange_chess | Side | 37 | 1704 | 616 | yes |
| harness-maker | (self) | 13 | 4160 | 855 | yes |
| edgelog | Side | 4 | 615 | 150 | yes |
| spoton | **Production** | **0** | 6153 | 1036 | **NO** |

**The turn-group column changed meaning once, and the first version of this entry had the
hand-rolled one.** An earlier draft reported 45/85/30/57, computed in a shell one-liner as
`{(session_id, stage)}` — which merges every dispatch of one agent in one session into a single
group and therefore **under-counts**. The shipped `sidechain_turn_groups` counts maximal
contiguous runs of sidechain turns in timestamp order — and it is wrong in BOTH directions, not just one: a parallel dispatch batch merges N
dispatches into a single run (undercount), while a peer session's main-chain turns split one
dispatch across several (overcount). Neither key is "number of dispatches"; the tested one is at
least reproducible and pinned
(`test_turn_groups_counts_contiguous_sidechain_runs_not_turns` discriminates the two keys
directly). The verdict below is unchanged under **either** key, because spoton's shape is
zero-vs-nonzero and no grouping choice moves a zero. `ledger_dispatches` also shifted (39→37,
12→13): sentinels are now excluded, and this PLAN's own validator passes added rows.

**strange_chess's "39 dispatches vs 0 sidechain turns" was never real.** The `0` came from an
ad-hoc hardlinked transcript directory built by hand to work around the underscore-encoder bug
(`-home-noel-strange_chess`), not from the real corpus. Against the real transcript root the
project has 1704 subagent turns over 616 runs, so 37 ledger rows is corroborated and properly
smaller — the ledger records only `plan-validator` / `test-reviewer` / `code-reviewer`, not every
subagent. **The lesson is about the fixture, not the ledger: a hand-built stand-in for a broken
reader is itself unvalidated, and a finding drawn from it inherits that.** RESEARCH Open
Question 2 is retracted in place.

**spoton's zero is real and has a positive mechanism.** The emit IS rendered
(`grep -c 'stage_agent_ledger emit'` = 1 in both `plan.md` and `execute.md`, version 0.50.1).
spoton re-rendered to 0.50.1 on **2026-08-08 19:27** (`261843e`); its most recent `/hm:plan` and
`/hm:execute` ran on **2026-08-01/02**, a week before the emit existed there. Zero rows is
therefore "no observations yet", not a silent no-op — the `absent-case = feature black hole`
hypothesis is **refuted** for this instance.

**ledger-trustworthy: yes** — with one caveat that changes how the numbers may be used. Every
row in the population comes from a **Side-preset** project. The pre-registered rates
(`P(validator verdict changes | later pass)` = 2/9 = 22.2%; `P(Phase A.5 FAIL)` = 9/24 = 37.5%)
are therefore Side-only, and Production is absent for a scheduling reason rather than a defect.
Both verdicts point the same way (**keep** both steps), and a preset with *more* review pressure
is unlikely to push either rate toward zero, so the conclusion holds — but a future aggregation
must not describe these as cross-preset.

`reconcile` exists to make this re-derivable rather than narrated: it is **diagnostic-only** and
its non-zero exit must never be wired into a gate, because spoton's disagreement is expected to
persist until that project next runs a gated stage.


## [wiki:architecture] autonomy-levels-live-in-two-places-and-a-test-finds-the-third | 2026-08-09
PLAN-workflow-time-token-savings B1/B2/B4. The autonomy level vocabulary is `gated | auto_safe | auto_full` (operational, a marker can hold them) plus `ask` (**yaml-only**, meaning "the session-start picker chooses"). `ask` is the **default for a freshly rendered harness** (ADR-012) — the right level is a property of the work, not of the project.

**Two spellings, one table, three readers.** `full` is the retired name for `auto_safe` and is demoted by `models.normalize_level` — never promoted to `auto_full`, because `full` never had wider-advance semantics and loading must not escalate autonomy. Three readers must go through that one function, and **two of the three were found by pre-existing tests, not by the new matrix**: `autopilot.effective_level` (an un-re-rendered `harness.yaml`), the `AutopilotMarker` model (a live marker written by an older version), and `resolve_toggle_config` (a stale rendered picker interpolating `--level full`). All three failed the same way — the unknown-value clamp returned `gated`, so autopilot was **silently off** with no diagnostic.

**The strings are spelled out in exactly two `models.py` nodes** (`OperationalLevel`, `AutonomyLevel`) plus the alias map; everything else derives (`OPERATIONAL_LEVELS = get_args(...)`, `ARMED_LEVELS = OPERATIONAL_LEVELS - {gated}`). Before this they were restated in nine places across six modules, one of which was an if/elif ladder ending `else: return False` — adding a level without touching it would have made that level **silently never arm**. `tests/structural/test_autonomy_level_literals.py` walks the AST of `src/` and fails on any node spelling out ≥2 **distinct** level strings; distinctness matters, because counting occurrences flagged `{"full": "auto_safe"}`, a `try/except` pinning `gated` twice, and `synthesize._COMMUNICATION_VARIANT` (whose `"full"` is a communication variant that merely shares a spelling). A meta-test re-adds the deleted ladder and asserts the guard reports it — a discovery test nobody has seen fail is a hand-list with extra steps.

**Absent still means `gated`.** `_parse_autonomy` pins an absent or malformed block to `gated`, and an explicit interview decline pins it too. The class default asking is not a reason to weaken either: a default that asks is still an autonomy change and must arrive through `--update`.
## [wiki:architecture] the-judgment-gate-is-source-stage-keyed-and-fail-closed | 2026-08-09
PLAN-workflow-time-token-savings B3 (ADR-009/010). `stage_end_summary.md.j2` Step 1 used to evaluate a stage's mandatory gate and STOP on pending, explicitly forbidding the Step 2 boundary call — so `boundary` sat *below* the gate and could not govern it. `auto_full` needs to govern exactly two of those gates, so the gate's verdict is now an **explicit input**: `--judgment-gate clear|pending`, and **absent means `pending`**.

- **Keyed on `--current`** (the stage that just ran and owns the judgment), never on `(source, next)`. A user-customised `autonomy.pipeline` changes what follows plan/review while leaving the judgment where it is — pair-keying would silently stop gating for those users. `_HUMAN_GATED_STAGES` stays next-stage-keyed because it guards a one-way door: what matters there is what is about to be *entered*.
- **`clear` proceeds at every level, `auto_safe` included.** An earlier draft pinned `proceed: false` at `auto_safe` and called it "the default is unchanged"; it would have stopped every plan and review stage of every existing autopilot session.
- **`judgment_gate` PRESERVES the marker** (`merge_gate` clears it). Copying the clear would end the session at the first plan stage and starve `smoke_check` of the rows that let `/hm:health` tell "stopping correctly" from "never fires".
- **The review gate SPLITS** (ADR-010): `grade < threshold` is a quality threshold, mandatory at every level and never passed to the boundary; `human_review_needed` on an APPROVED review is the judgment half. The compensating control at `auto_full` is the recorded list of passed-over finding ids — `human_review_needed` is this harness's only provenance exception, and at `auto_full` that path is consumed automatically.
- **The Step 1/Step 2 discriminator is DERIVED from `summary_stage`** inside the shared partial. A caller-set Jinja variable would break the five stage templates outside the phase's scope under StrictUndefined.

**The blast radius showed up as promised**: three pre-existing tests (two unit, one e2e) went red because they call `boundary` without the flag on a clean chain. That is the fail-closed default working, not a regression — they now pass `--judgment-gate clear` with the reason recorded.
## [wiki:architecture] the-judgment-gate-has-three-values-and-absence-is-a-fourth-case | 2026-08-09
`autopilot_caps boundary --judgment-gate` takes `clear | pending | blocked`, and **an absent flag is a distinct fourth case** — not a synonym for any of them. Getting this wrong produced two separate P0s in one review, so the semantics are worth stating exactly:

| value | meaning | who clears it |
|---|---|---|
| `clear` | nothing pending | every level |
| `pending` | the caller ASSERTS an open judgment — a question with a defensible answer | `auto_full` only |
| `blocked` | the caller ASSERTS a failed quality threshold (a grade, a check) | **no level**, `auto_full` included |
| *(absent)* | the caller classified nothing | **no level** — halts and reports a stale render |

**`blocked` is honoured on EVERY stage**, not just `{plan, review}`: it is an assertion that something failed, and scoping it to the judgment stages made it a silent no-op on the other six.

**Why absence must not map to `pending`.** The first fix for the CHANGES_REQUESTED P0 set `default="pending"` and called that fail-closed. But `pending` is precisely the value `auto_full` is licensed to clear, so an omitted flag — a forgotten append, or a harness rendered before the flag existed — reopened the hole at the one level where it matters, and the stale-render diagnostic added in the same change sat in a branch `auto_full` could never reach. **Fail-closed is a distinction, not a default value.** Both round-2 reviewers found this independently.

**A `gated` marker never advances** (`kill_switch`, marker preserved — arming gated is how a session records "asked, declined"). `boundary` used to read `marker.level` only to decide *how* to advance; it now decides *whether* first.

**`gate_auto_answered` is written when the judgment is ANSWERED**, with `advanced` as a field — not only on the advancing path. Two reviewers proposed opposite placements; the row's question is "was a human judgment cleared?", and suppressing it on a run that then stopped at the land gate made that run byte-identical to a `clear`-gate `auto_safe` run, which is the indistinguishability the row exists to remove.

## [wiki:architecture] oracle-toolchains-declaration | 2026-08-09
The cross-model review oracle no longer decides what to run — the project does. `second_opinion_oracle._run_checks` used to hardcode `uv run pytest` / `ruff check` / `mypy --strict` and issue all three against every path a cross-model finding named, in every project. On a file those tools cannot parse that is not a degraded oracle, it is a fabricated one: measured on four lines of `.tsx`, `ruff` emits 3809 bytes of Python syntax errors at exit=1 (indistinguishable from a real lint failure), `pytest` exits 4, `mypy` exits 2 — three non-zero results that never parsed the subject, injected into `code-verifier` mode B as evidence, landing either as a false `accepted` or, once the 1500-char per-command budget truncates them, as a silent `unresolved`. The replacement is a `toolchains` declaration read from `harness.yaml`, grouping commands per toolchain under role keys, with a five-row extension default table when the key is absent (a `.py` path is byte-identical to the old behaviour). Two placement facts are load-bearing. First, the key sits at the **harness.yaml root, not under `second_opinion`** (ADR-002): eight other rendered surfaces already hardcode the same Python triple, so nesting it would make this the third encoding of one project fact and force a key migration the moment `verify` or `wrapup` follow. Second, `{path}` in a command decides the shape of the evidence: present ⇒ the command runs per path and the block is emitted id-LABELLED against the finding; absent ⇒ it runs exactly once per gather and the block is repo-wide and carries no finding id. A path whose extension no toolchain covers spawns **zero** subprocesses and its finding goes to the no-oracle tail — refusing to run is the feature, not a fallback.
## [wiki:gotcha] config-derived-argv0-is-code-trust | 2026-08-09
Moving a command from a source literal into a config file changes its security class, not just its flexibility. Before this change `second_opinion_oracle` built its argv from string literals in the module; after it, `argv[0]` comes from `harness.yaml`. `harness.yaml` is a file the harness itself invites the user (and any tool or agent with Write) to edit, and the rendered permission set pre-approves the Bash prefixes the oracle runs under — so a permitted config-file write silently became **unprompted arbitrary program execution** behind a rule the user already said yes to. Nothing about the write looks like an execution request at the moment of approval, which is exactly why the permission prompt cannot be the control. The fix is a fail-closed runner allowlist evaluated where the argv is assembled: only a known set of program names may occupy `argv[0]`, anything else is refused and the toolchain degrades to `no_oracle` rather than running. Two supporting invariants keep the rest of the argv honest — `shell` is never true, and the subject path is passed as its own argv element via a `{path}` placeholder substitution, so no adversarial file name can become a separate token. Generalisable rule for this repo: whenever a value that used to be a literal becomes config-derived, ask what the value is *used as*. If it reaches `argv[0]`, a file path outside the project, or a URL, the config file has inherited the trust level of the code, and it needs an allowlist at the point of use — the config schema's type check is not that allowlist.
## [wiki:architecture] multi-lens-phase-a5-gate | 2026-08-10
Phase A.5 of `/hm:execute` was the only gate in the harness with a single voter and serial retries; it now dispatches **three lens-scoped `test-reviewer` calls in one message** — `red-correctness` (does each test fail, and for the intended reason?), `discrimination` (would this assertion also pass against a plausibly WRONG implementation?), `coverage` (does the set cover the criterion, with no missing scenario and no duplicate?) — and retries by ROUND rather than by attempt. Measured basis for the shape, not a guess: a serially-retried single reviewer surfaced one failure category per round (2 findings, then 3); the three concurrent lenses surfaced 9 and 12, with **zero overlap between the two blocking lenses** — so the lenses are not redundant voters, they are disjoint detectors, and the old serial retry was re-asking the same question. **Merge algebra** (`src/harness_maker/templates/stages/execute.md.j2` §"Merge all three before judging"): `overall_assessment` is **recomputed** from the merged carriers — PASS iff every lens dispatched in THIS round returned PASS and the merged `blocking_issues[]` / `scenarios_missing[]` / `per_scenario[]` are clean; a lens's own header is never trusted, because a lens that reports a defect while stamping PASS is parseable and trusting the header silently drops the defect. `blocking_issues[]` is unioned and deduped on `test_file:test_function:category` **carrying the union of the `line`s** — not line-keyed (two lenses anchor one defect on whatever line their OBSERVE step cited, so a line key almost never merges them and the rewrite list gets it twice) and not line-blind (two genuinely different bad assertions in one function share file+function+category, and collapsing them drops one). `scenarios_missing[]` unions by id; `per_scenario[]` takes worst `quality`, union `covered_by`, `reason` from the worst lens. `passing_tests[]` is **intersected but advisory — it decides nothing**, because bare function names with no `test_file` cannot identify a test while `blocking_issues` entries can. **Three repair arms, one per carrier**: rewrite the functions named in merged `blocking_issues[]`; author one test per `scenarios_missing[]`; and **retarget-or-delete** for a `per_scenario[]` FAIL with no matching `blocking_issues` entry (duplicate coverage or a test aimed at another scenario) — the third arm is not optional, without it the same lens re-dispatches against an unchanged file, fails identically, and the 2-round budget burns with nothing changed. **Re-dispatch rule is one sentence: if you repaired anything, re-dispatch ALL THREE lenses** (plus any lens whose dispatch died, unchanged). No per-lens trigger list — a rewrite is a discrimination-class edit even when the discrimination lens passed, and a test authored for `scenarios_missing[]` has never been seen by `red-correctness` or `discrimination` at all. Worst case is 3 + 3 = 6 dispatches, the ceiling the budget already assumed. Two seam fixes rode along: `test-reviewer_body.md.j2`'s Hard Rule used to route out-of-lens findings into a `suggestions` field the JSON schema does not define (a reviewer that found a defect therefore emitted nothing and returned PASS), and `stuck_body.md.j2` still described the budget in "attempts".
## [wiki:architecture] interview-comprehension-depth-axis | 2026-08-13
`interview.comprehension.depth: minimal | standard | deep` is one ORDINAL, not four booleans (ADR-001), and it is a DISCLOSURE axis: `/hm:plan` Step 1 already builds the whole design picture — architecture sketch, phase skeleton, blast-radius-ranked ambiguities — under a heading that literally reads "NOT shown to user", and Step A's round-state block was explicitly optional. Nothing new is generated; existing output is re-routed. The four blocks are ordered: `minimal` = today (none), `standard` = brief + round_state, `deep` = + decision_depth + teach_back (teach_back is OUTPUT-only — no response, no gate). One shared partial `templates/agents/_partials/comprehension_block.md.j2` is included by both `/hm:plan` and `/hm:spec` and takes a `stage` argument, because `/hm:spec` has no internal Step 1 draft to disclose and already rendered its own "Decisions locked in so far" preamble — the partial SUBSUMES that block rather than adding a second one, so AC-006 asserts the same source + same enabled block SET, never text identity (ADR-007). Four facts worth carrying forward. (1) **The retrofit is deliberate and unannounced (ADR-006):** `answers_from_harness_yaml` rebuilds the whole `interview` block from `_preset_extras`, so an existing `harness.yaml` with NO `comprehension` key acquires `standard` on its next `--update` and its `/hm:plan` and `/hm:spec` grow. There is no fresh-install-vs-existing signal to branch on; the opt-out is `depth: minimal` via `/hm:configure`. (2) **`answers_from_harness_yaml` does NOT round-trip the `interview` block** — it reconstructs it and applies exactly one read-side overlay (`common_ground.llm_inference_enabled`), a consequence of 0.16.0 ADR-012 freezing ε/τ/cap as code constants. Any new `interview.*` key therefore needs its own EXPLICIT read-side overlay or it is silently reset to the preset default on every re-render. The overlay list is now two entries; a third should trigger a generic mechanism instead of a third hand-wired special case. (3) **The only mechanical proof that a `minimal` opt-out is free is SHA-256 against an immutable pre-change golden** captured BEFORE any template edit (`tests/structural/comprehension_zero_cost_golden.json`, AC-003). `surface_baseline.json` cannot be that oracle — it renders through the same defaulting function and gets regenerated at the new `standard` default, after which "minimal vs baseline" silently compares minimal against standard. The golden records a base-reachable `merge-base` SHA (`assert_sha_is_durable`), never the task-branch HEAD a squash-land deletes. (4) **Configure prose without a CLI flag is not an entry point** — `/hm:configure` is pure prose dispatching `hm cli make --<flag>`, so a prose-only dimension tells the user to pass a flag that does not exist while every render-grep passes; the dimension and the `cli.py` flag ship together, and its `--preset` / `--reinterview` interactions are where every P1 of this unit landed.
